# Platform audit — 2026-08-21 (session 256)

Twelve parallel dimension auditors over the monorepo, each finding then handed to an
independent verifier prompted to REFUTE it by default. 142 agents, ~6.2M tokens.

| | |
|---|---|
| Raw findings | 128 |
| Survived refutation | 114 |
| Refuted | 14 |
| Confirmed **and** attributable to a file (listed below) | 103 |

**Read the survival rate with suspicion.** 89% of findings surviving a refute-by-default
pass is high; a genuinely adversarial verifier kills more. Treat P1 as well-evidenced
(each was re-read end to end and several had their severity corrected downward), and
P2/P3 as leads worth confirming before acting. Eleven verdicts could not be matched back
to their finding and are omitted rather than guessed at.

No P0 was confirmed.

## Fixed in this session

Three of the confirmed findings were in the funnel this session was already
working on, so they were fixed rather than filed. Both form-engine ones landed on
code this session had itself just changed, which is the argument for auditing
your own fixes.

| Finding | What shipped |
|---|---|
| **[P1] Renaming a field label silently rekeys it** | Keys persisted by the server are snapshotted at hydrate and never regenerated from a label edit. Keys added in the current editing session still track their label, which is where that behaviour is wanted. |
| **[P1] "Log in to register" dead-ends on a permanently disabled button** | `canSave` no longer requires `dirty` for a FIRST registration, so an all-optional form (the default for every contest created through the editor) is submittable with nothing typed. Editing already-saved details still requires a change. |
| **[P1] Icon-only buttons with no accessible name** — partially | The registration form's controls gained `aria-invalid`, `aria-required` and an `aria-describedby` that points at the error text. The ten icon-only buttons the auditor names are NOT fixed and remain open. |

The session also fixed things the audit did not surface, because they were found
by walking the funnel in a browser rather than by reading: the `light`-mode
registration copy that told participants they were entered when they were not,
the missing "next: submit your project" step, the 20 required-field errors
rendered before any input, the silent proposal-mode-with-no-fields
configuration, and the unbalanced-HTML hydration corruption. See
`docs/sessions/256-contest-funnel-and-conditional-fields.md`.

## Confirmed findings

### The shared form engine

#### [P1] Renaming a field label silently rekeys it, orphaning every stored answer and consent row

`layers/base/utils/contestStages.ts:159`

`templateFieldLabelChanged` regenerates the machine `key` from the new label whenever the current key still matches the old label's slug, and because FormTemplateEditor never exposes or lets an operator edit `key`, that condition is true for every field an operator has not previously renamed — so a pure label edit on a live form changes the storage key.

**Failure:** A contest has 200 registrations against a field labelled "Repositry URL" (key `repositry_url`). The organizer fixes the typo to "Repository URL". `tracksLabel` is true (`'repositry_url' === fieldKeyFromLabel('Repositry URL')`), so the saved template now carries key `repository_url`. ContestRegistrantsPanel/`buildRegistrantsExport` build columns from the CURRENT template keys, so the column renders blank for all 200 registrants; `getViewerRegistration` prefill is blank too, and the next time any of those users saves the form `set.fields = publicFields` replaces the whole jsonb and the old-key answer is deleted. Do the same on an `agreement` label and `agreementKeys` (registrants.get.ts:45 / export.ts:199) no longer matches any `contest_agreement_acceptances.field_key`, so every registrant who did accept now reads `0/1` in the panel and the CSV consent column.

**Suggested fix:** Freeze the key once the template has been persisted (pass a `keysLocked`/`hasAnswers` flag into FormTemplateEditor and skip the rekey branch), or expose the key as a read-only chip with an explicit "change key (breaks existing answers)" affordance.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. contestStages.ts:155-168 rekeys on label edit whenever `field.key === fieldKeyFromLabel(field.label)`, and that condition holds for every field the UI can produce: FormTemplateEditor.vue exposes no `key` input (verified by grepping the whole SFC — every `key` occurrence is a v-for bind or e.key), and all key-producing paths derive from the label (templatePresetAdded contestSubmissionTemplates.ts:117, withKeys in the form templates, registrationMarkdown.ts:75). The documented "a hand-edited key is left alone" escape hatch is therefore unreachable except via the `_2` collision suffix. It reaches the registration form, not just stage templates: ContestEditor.vue:609 mounts FormTemplateEditor with v-model:template="registrationTemplate", and labelInput (FormTemplateEditor.vue:197) fires on every keystroke. No guard exists: useContestEditor.ts:438 PUTs the array verbatim and packages/server/src/contest/contest.ts:172 assigns it with no key reconciliation, no registrations-exist freeze, and no feature flag on the builder. Downstream is exactly as claimed: export.ts:177/199/211 and registrants.get.ts:45 + registrations.ts:414-434 key answer columns and the consent count off the CURRENT template keys and inArray-filter contest_agreement_acceptances.fieldKey by them (the comment at registrations.ts:415 literally anticipates "removed/renamed" and accepts the undercount); registrations.ts:164-226 sets publicFields = r.result.artifact and `set.fields = publicFields`, replacing the whole jsonb so the old-key answer is destroyed on the registrant's next save; getViewerRegistration (registrations.ts:341-363) merges by stored key so prefill goes blank. No test pins the behaviour — grep for templateFieldLabelChanged across layers/base/**/__tests__ returns zero hits and FormTemplateEditor.test.ts has no label-edit case. Introduced in ec51d517 as a stage-template convenience, before the shared registration reuse existed, so it is not a deliberate decision for this surface.

</details>

#### [P2] Stage submission form cannot prefill PII or agreement answers, forcing re-entry and duplicate consent rows

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestStageSubmission.vue:49`

The per-stage artifact form seeds its values exclusively from `existing.fields` (the PUBLIC `stageSubmissions` jsonb), but `validateSubmissionFields` partitions every `email`/`signature`/`address`/`file`/`pii:true` answer into `contest_entry_private_fields` and every accepted agreement into `contest_agreement_acceptances` — none of which are in that jsonb. The registration side was explicitly fixed for this (`getViewerRegistration` merges all three partitions, registrations.ts:335 'audit fix'); the entry side was not.

**Failure:** A stage template uses the built-in "Agreement" preset (`required: true, mustAccept: true`) plus a required `signature` field. An entrant submits successfully. They return to fix a typo in their repo URL: `next[f.key] = existing.value?.fields[f.key] ?? ''` yields '' for both `agreement` and `signature` (they live in the private/consent tables), so `blockingFields` reports them missing and `save()` aborts with "Please fill: Agreement, Signature". The entrant must retype their legal signature and re-tick the agreement to change one URL; each such edit appends another row to `contest_agreement_acceptances` because entry-scope inserts are append-only (`tx.insert(contestAgreementAcceptances).values(rows)` with no onConflict, submissions.ts:258). With a required `file` field they must re-upload the document to make any edit at all.

**Suggested fix:** Fetch `/api/contests/:slug/entries/:entryId/private` for the viewer's own entry and merge `fields` + `agreements.map(a => [a.fieldKey,'true'])` over `existing.fields`, exactly as `getViewerRegistration` does for registrations.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. validation.ts:82-96/103/184 partitions agreements out entirely and routes isFormFieldPii fields (address/file always, email/signature unless pii:false, any pii:true — schema/contest.ts:200-205) into `pii`, so submissions.ts:349 snapshots only `artifact` into stageSubmissions, and entries.ts:147 returns only that jsonb. ContestStageSubmission.vue:49 seeds exclusively from `existing.fields` and never calls /api/contests/:slug/entries/:entryId/private (its only caller is pages/contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId].vue:92, a display-only surface). The registration side has the exact fix the entry side lacks: registrations.ts:329-364 getViewerRegistration merges all three partitions with the comment "Without this merge, reopening the form would show every required PII/agreement field blank and block Save (audit fix)". No upstream guard exists — the server independently re-rejects (validation.ts:85 "You must accept: X", :108 "X is required"), so the blocked edit is not merely a client gate. Reachable at shipped config: the same FormTemplateEditor drives the per-stage template (ContestStageCard.vue:162) under contestStageSubmissions (default TRUE), the `required` checkbox is always available (FormTemplateEditor.vue:387), the agreement preset defaults required+mustAccept, and contestPii is `true` in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:23; the `email` preset is not even PII-gated yet still partitions to PII, so it fires at default flags. Duplicate-consent half also confirmed: submissions.ts:249-259 gives registration scope onConflictDoNothing but entry scope a bare insert, documented as "entry acceptances stay append-only". Additional unreported symptom: `dirty` (line 53) compares '' against undefined for those keys, so the form shows "Unsaved changes" permanently. Severity lowered to P2: it needs an organizer to put a PII/agreement field on a per-stage form (those usually live on the registration form) and the usual damage is forced re-entry plus consent-row pileup, not data loss or a security hole — only a required `file` field is a hard block.

</details>

#### [P2] Two fields with the same label produce duplicate keys and hard-400 the entire contest save with no field named

`layers/base/utils/contestStages.ts:159`

`templateFieldLabelChanged` derives the key from the label with no uniqueness check against the rest of the template (unlike `templatePresetAdded` and `registrationMarkdownToTemplate`, which both uniquify). `useContestEditor.buildPayload` ships the array verbatim with no client-side validation, and `registrationTemplateSchema`/`contestStageSchema.submissionTemplate` refuse duplicates.

**Failure:** Operator adds the "Short text" preset twice → keys `short_answer`, `short_answer_2`. They rename the first to "Full name" (tracksLabel true → key `full_name`), add a "Long text" preset (key `details`) under a second section and rename it to "Full name" too (tracksLabel true → key `full_name`). Duplicate. Pressing Save PUTs the whole contest; zod's array `.refine` fails, `parseBody` flattens to `{ registrationTemplate: ['Template field keys must be unique'] }`, and the toast reads "registrationTemplate: Template field keys must be unique". The key is invisible in the builder, so the operator has no way to identify which two fields collided, and every other unsaved edit (title, dates, prizes, blocks) is rejected with them. On a draft contest the 3s autosave re-fires the same 400 on every keystroke.

**Suggested fix:** Uniquify in `templateFieldLabelChanged` against the other fields' keys, and surface an inline per-card error in FormTemplateEditor for a duplicate/blank key or a blank option `value` so Save is blocked with the offending card highlighted instead of a top-level 400.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. contestStages.ts:155-168 `templateFieldLabelChanged` assigns `key: fieldKeyFromLabel(label)` with no uniqueness check against the array, unlike `templatePresetAdded`/`withKeys` (contestSubmissionTemplates.ts:28,39,117) and `registrationMarkdown.ts:74` which both call a `uniqueKey` helper. I simulated the real helper logic in node: two DIFFERENT presets ('Short answer' -> short_answer, 'Long answer' -> long_answer) both satisfy `tracksLabel` (key === fieldKeyFromLabel(label)), so renaming both to the same label (or to labels that normalize identically, e.g. 'Website' vs 'Website:', or two headings sharing a 40-char prefix given the .slice(40)) produces ['full_name','full_name'] — duplicate confirmed. Rejection is real: registrationTemplateSchema (packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:339-345) and contestStageSchema.submissionTemplate (:227-232) both `.refine(new Set(keys).size === fields.length)`, and updateContestSchema:446 uses it; the PUT route (layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/index.put.ts:10) calls parseBody, which throws 400 with `flatten().fieldErrors` (validate.ts:65-76). No server-side normalization/uniquification runs before validation. Because the refine sits on the ARRAY, fieldErrors keys only on `registrationTemplate` with no index, and useApiError.extract renders 'registrationTemplate: Template field keys must be unique' — and FormTemplateEditor never displays a field key (only as a showWhen <option> fallback), so the operator cannot identify the collision. No client pre-flight exists: useContestEditor.ts:438 ships the array verbatim, save() guards only dateError (:449-451), canSubmit checks only dates/title/dirty (:246-250), and there is no 'duplicate' check anywhere in components/contest/. Draft autosave is real (ContestEditor.vue:227-241, debounceMs 3000, canSave checks only isDraftAutosave/dateError/title), so a draft contest re-fires the same failing PUT and never persists any edit. The Registration tab is not flag-gated (BodyTab includes 'registration' unconditionally; only requireFeature('contests') applies). The existing test contestStages.test.ts:151-161 pins the tracks-vs-pinned key behaviour but asserts nothing about uniqueness, so the omission is unguarded rather than deliberate. One minor overstatement in the report: the autosave error toast is deduped by distinct message via lastAutosaveError, so it does not toast on every keystroke — but the PUT still fails every 3s and the edits stay unsaved, which is the load-bearing harm.

</details>

#### [P2] Clearing a PII answer silently fails: the private-field write is skipped and the old value survives

`packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:203`

`recordPrivateAndAgreements` no-ops entirely when the partitioned `pii` map is empty, and otherwise merges (`{...existing, ...pii}`) rather than replacing. Since `buildSubmissionPayload` drops blank values and `validateSubmissionFields` skips optional blanks, an erased PII answer never reaches the server as a key — so it can never be removed. The public `fields` jsonb, by contrast, IS wholesale replaced (registrations.ts:226).

**Failure:** A registrant has an optional `email` field (PII by default per `isFormFieldPii`) holding `a@b.c`. They open /contests/:slug/register, clear the box, and press Save. `collected` omits `email`, `dirty` is true (saved has it), the POST succeeds and toasts "Your details were saved". Server-side: `validateSubmissionFields` hits `if (!value) { ...; continue; }` so `pii === {}`, `Object.keys(pii).length > 0` is false, and `contest_registration_private_fields.fields` is left untouched. The organizer's registrants panel still shows `a@b.c`, and on the participant's next page load `getViewerRegistration` merges the private partition back so the field reappears populated — their deletion visibly reverts. Same asymmetry on the entry side via `submitStageArtifact`.

**Suggested fix:** Have `validateSubmissionFields` emit an explicit tombstone (or return the set of PII keys the template declares) so `recordPrivateAndAgreements` can compute `merged` as `{...existing, ...pii}` minus the template's PII keys that were submitted blank, and run the write whenever the template has any PII field rather than only when `pii` is non-empty.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. validation.ts `validateSubmissionFields` omits optional blanks (`if (!value) { if (field.required) return …; continue; }`), so a cleared PII answer yields no key in `pii`. submissions.ts:203 `if (Object.keys(pii).length > 0)` then skips the private write entirely, and when it does run both branches merge (`const merged = { ...(existing?.fields ?? {}), ...pii }`, lines 210/224) — so a key can never be deleted from contest_registration_private_fields/contest_entry_private_fields. registrations.ts:226 `if (input.fields !== undefined) set.fields = publicFields;` replaces the public jsonb outright, confirming the asymmetry. The client path is reachable and lies: buildSubmissionPayload (layers/base/utils/contestSubmission.ts:123) drops blanks, ContestRegistrationForm.vue:67 `dirty` compares the union of collected+saved keys so Save enables, register.vue:44 toasts "Your details were saved"; getViewerRegistration (registrations.ts:337) then merges the private partition back so the deleted value visibly reappears, and listContestRegistrants/includePii still shows it to organizers. `email` is PII by default per isFormFieldPii (schema/src/contest.ts:200). No guard rescues it: recordPrivateAndAgreements is the only writer of those tables; no test pins clear-behaviour; git blame (2b89586d) shows the merge was copied from the entry scope where it is load-bearing across stages, not authored deliberately for registration scope. The empty-pii no-op IS needed for a bare re-register (input.fields undefined), so the fix is to key the replace on input.fields !== undefined, not to drop the guard. Severity lowered to P2: it requires an operator-authored template containing a PII field — those types are only offered when features.contestPii is on (layer default false in layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:111, true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:23), and DEFAULT_REGISTRATION_TEMPLATE has none — and full erasure still works via unregister (contest_registration_private_fields cascades on the registration row). Real silent data-rectification failure, but not a daily path and not an exposure of data the organizer never held.

</details>

#### [P2] Editing an agreement's terms mid-contest never prompts re-consent and is invisible in the audit read-back

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestRegistrationForm.vue:63`

`getViewerRegistration` re-expresses any accepted agreement as `key: 'true'` regardless of which terms version was accepted, and the form's `dirty` gate compares only those string values — so after an operator edits `terms`, the checkbox is pre-ticked, `collected` equals `savedFields`, and Save is disabled. No new acceptance row (with the new `termsHash`) is ever written for existing registrants, and the consent count never reveals the split because it groups on `fieldKey` alone.

**Failure:** 200 people register accepting terms hash A. Legal asks for a wording change; the organizer edits the agreement's `terms` textarea and saves. Every registrant revisiting /contests/:slug/register sees the NEW terms with the box already ticked and Save greyed out (`canSave = dirty && !missing && !registering`, dirty is false). The panel and CSV keep reporting `1/1` consented for all 200, so the organizer has no signal that all recorded consents are against the superseded text — `contest_agreement_acceptances.terms_snapshot`/`terms_hash` hold the old wording but nothing reads them back.

**Suggested fix:** Return the accepted `termsHash` per agreement from `getViewerRegistration`, untick (and re-block) any agreement whose hash differs from `hashTerms(field.terms)`, and scope the consent count to the live `(fieldKey, termsHash)` pairs so a stale-version consent shows as outstanding rather than complete.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. (1) getViewerRegistration (packages/server/src/contest/registrations.ts:352-361) selects only fieldKey from contest_agreement_acceptances and rewrites every acceptance as key:'true', dropping terms_hash/terms_snapshot. (2) ContestRegistrationForm.vue seeds values from savedFields (L41-48), collected normalizes agreements to the literal 'true' (contestSubmission.ts buildSubmissionPayload L130-134), and dirty (L66-71) compares only those strings, so after a terms-only edit collected === savedFields and canSave (L73) is false. Un-ticking makes it dirty but then blockingFieldKeys puts the mustAccept key in `missing`, so canSave is still false and re-ticking returns to not-dirty: there is no interaction sequence by which an existing registrant can record acceptance of revised terms. (3) No upstream guard: updateContest (packages/server/src/contest/contest.ts:172) writes registrationTemplate with no status or registrant-exists check, and FormTemplateEditor.vue:413 edits `terms` alone, leaving the field `key` (and therefore the acceptance match) intact. The unique constraint uq_contest_agreements_registration_field_terms (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:676) is on (registrationId, fieldKey, termsHash) — the schema is version-aware, but nothing ever triggers the re-save that would use it. (4) Read-back confirmed: the consent queries at registrations.ts:420-433 and packages/server/src/contest/export.ts:199-214 are count(distinct field_key) filtered only by inArray(fieldKey, agreementKeys), with no terms_hash predicate, and ContestRegistrantsPanel.vue:88-91 renders that as a green N/N. terms_snapshot/terms_hash are read back only in the user's own GDPR export (profile/export.ts:413-418) and the entry-scope getEntryAcceptances (submissions.ts:706-714) — never on the organizer's registration surface. (5) Reachability verified empirically, not assumed: contestPii defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:41), which would gate the builder's agreement type, but `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns contestPii:true and the live status:active contest `qualcommforamoreresilientamerica` has a 42-field registration template with seven mustAccept:true agreements (eligibility, IP licence, taxes/liability, governing law, dev-kit, open-source). No test pins terms-version behaviour; the only acknowledgement of stale consent anywhere (packages/config/src/types.ts:326-327) calls account-level re-acceptance "a future re-acceptance gate", i.e. unimplemented rather than deliberately declined. Severity P2 is right: it requires an operator action rather than a daily user path and destroys no data (old hash/snapshot survive and are recoverable by query), but the organizer read-back affirmatively reports full consent against superseded legal text on a live sponsored contest.

</details>

#### [P3] Consent denominator reimplements the required-agreement rule and drops `required: true, mustAccept: false` agreements

`layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/registrants.get.ts:45`

Three call sites compute "which agreements count toward consent" as `f.type === 'agreement' && f.mustAccept !== false`, which is not the enforcement rule. `validateSubmissionFields` requires acceptance when `field.required || field.mustAccept !== false` (validation.ts:69) and `isRequiredFormField` agrees (`f.required === true || f.mustAccept !== false`), so an agreement with `required: true, mustAccept: false` is blocking on both client and server but is excluded from the audit denominator.

**Failure:** The FormTemplateEditor shows a generic "Required" checkbox for agreement cards (line 348, gated only on `tf.type !== 'section'`) alongside "Must accept to submit" (line 379). An operator ticks Required and unticks Must accept on their sole rules agreement. Registration is correctly blocked until each entrant accepts, and acceptance rows are written. But `agreementKeys` is `[]`, so `listContestRegistrants` skips the consent query entirely and every registrant shows `consentCount: 0`; `agreementCount` is 0 so ContestRegistrantsPanel hides the Consents column and `buildRegistrantsExport` omits it from the CSV header. The organizer's only compliance read-back for a legally required agreement disappears while the acceptance rows sit in the table.

**Suggested fix:** Replace all three with `f.type === 'agreement' && isRequiredFormField(f)`, and export a shared `requiredAgreementKeys(template)` helper from @commonpub/schema so there is one implementation rather than three copies.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. The enforcement predicate and the audit denominator genuinely disagree for `{type:'agreement', required:true, mustAccept:false}`. Enforcement: validation.ts:83-93 blocks submission on `(field.required || mustAccept) && !accepted` and writes an acceptance row when accepted; registrations.ts:157-171 routes registration through validateSubmissionFields whenever templateHasRequiredField (which delegates to isRequiredFormField, contest.ts:216-219 = `f.required === true || f.mustAccept !== false`) holds; ContestSubmissionField.vue:82,92 marks it required to the entrant. Denominator: registrants.get.ts:45, export.ts:199 and ContestRegistrantsPanel.vue:45 each independently compute `f.type === 'agreement' && f.mustAccept !== false`, none importing the shared helper. With that field as the sole agreement, agreementKeys is [], so registrations.ts:419-421 skips the consent query (consentCount 0 for all), ContestRegistrantsPanel.vue:76,88-91 hides the Consents column, and export.ts:228,236 drops it from the CSV. No guard blocks the combination: submissionTemplateFieldSchema (validators/contest.ts:167-205) has no refine coupling required to mustAccept, contestStages.ts:282 only defaults mustAccept on type change, and FormTemplateEditor.vue:388-391 (generic Required checkbox for every non-section card) plus :415 (independent "Must accept to submit") let an operator produce it. Only the `contests` feature flag gates the route, and contests are live. Git history supports the finding rather than refuting it: 7242f24d narrowed the filter to `mustAccept !== false` with the stated intent "only REQUIRED agreements count", six days after a79cfab6 introduced isRequiredFormField for exactly that predicate. No test covers required:true+mustAccept:false (validators.test.ts:1145 only pairs mustAccept:false with required:false). Severity lowered to P3: the trigger needs a self-contradictory operator config, and the harm is a missing organizer read-back column, not lost or leaked consent data (acceptance rows persist and remain reachable via consent/DSAR export).

</details>

### The contest participation funnel — stranger landing on a contest page to a submitted entry

#### [P1] Contest entries fetch has no limit, silently capping the page at 20 and corrupting myEntries

`layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:15`

The page fetches `/api/contests/:slug/entries` with no `limit`, the server defaults to 20 (normalizePagination), and neither the page nor ContestEntries paginates — so once a contest passes 20 entries the grid, the tab count, the participants list and, critically, the viewer's own `myEntries` are all computed from a truncated list.

**Failure:** deveco's contest reaches 21+ entries. `listContestEntries` orders `desc(submittedAt), desc(id)` (packages/server/src/contest/entries.ts:80) and returns the newest 20; `total` is returned but never read. Consequences for a participant who entered early: (1) `myEntries` (index.vue:237, filters `entries.value`) becomes empty, so `ContestStageSubmission` (index.vue:629, `v-if="currentSubmissionStage && myEntries.length"`) vanishes and they can no longer submit or edit their per-stage artifact for the open round; (2) `ContestProposalForm` (index.vue:640, `v-if="... && !myEntries.length && ..."`) reappears, inviting them to submit a SECOND proposal, which the server accepts (submitContestProposal has no already-entered check, only the optional maxEntriesPerUser cap) creating a duplicate entry plus a duplicate draft project; (3) `enteredContentIds` (index.vue:216) no longer contains their project, so the submit dialog re-enables an already-entered tile and the POST fails with the misleading "Couldn't submit, you may have already entered this project, or reached the contest's entry limit." (entries.post.ts:67); (4) the Entries tab chip (index.vue:113) and the Participants tab (index.vue:85, :114) show 20 / a truncated roster as if that were the whole field.

**Suggested fix:** Pass `limit: 100` and render a load-more using the returned `total`; separately, derive `myEntries` from a dedicated `/entries?mine=1` fetch (or from the registration/entry state endpoint) so the entrant's own submit gating never depends on which page of the public grid they happen to be on.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the whole chain; no guard exists anywhere. 1. FETCH — `layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:15` is exactly `useLazyFetch<{ items: Serialized<ContestEntryItem>[]; total: number }>(`/api/contests/${slug}/entries`)` with no `query` option. `grep -n "total"` on the page returns ONLY line 15 (the type parameter), so the returned `total` is never read and the truncation is invisible. 2. SERVER DEFAULT — `entries.get.ts` validates `limit` as `.optional()` and passes `limit: query.limit` (undefined) into `listContestEntries`. `packages/server/src/contest/entries.ts:80` calls `normalizePagination(opts)` with NO `defaults` argument; `packages/server/src/query.ts:157` then computes `fallback = Math.min(Math.max(Math.trunc(defaults.limit ?? 20), 1), maxLimit)` → 20. The query is `.orderBy(desc(submittedAt), desc(id)).limit(20).offset(0)` — newest 20 only. Deliberateness ruled out: the sibling page `layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/results.vue:12` DOES pass `{ order: 'rank', limit: 100 }`, so the omission on the main page is an oversight, and `git log -L 15,15` shows the line unchanged since the original contest-system commit 287bb058. 3. NO CLIENT PAGINATION — `ContestEntries.vue` takes a plain `entries` prop and renders `v-for` over it; grep for load/more/slice/page finds nothing, the template ends at the empty state. `myEntries` (index.vue:237), `enteredContentIds` (216), `participants` (87) and the Entries tab chip (113) are ALL computed from `entries.value`, i.e. the truncated 20. 4. CONSEQUENCES VERIFIED — (1) Template line 629 `v-if="currentSubmissionStage && myEntries.length"` gates `ContestStageSubmission`; the flag it depends on, `contestStageSubmissions`, is `z.boolean().default(true)` (packages/config/src/schema.ts:35), so this path is ON by default everywhere. An early entrant pushed past row 20 loses their stage-artifact form. (2) Line 640 `ContestProposalForm v-if="... && !myEntries.length && ..."` reappears. `submitContestProposal` (packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:400-530) has NO already-entered check — only the optional `maxEntriesPerUser` (nullable column, schema/src/contest.ts:474, no default). Its only conflict guard is `onConflictDoNothing()` on a row whose `contentId` is a freshly created placeholder, and the code's own comment concedes "the fresh contentId means onConflictDoNothing can't catch a double-submit". So a second proposal + a second draft project really is created. (This one is narrower: `currentProposalStage` requires `features.contestProposals === true`, which is `.default(false)`.) (3) `enteredContentIds` drives `:disabled` and the "Entered" badge on the picker tiles (index.vue:542-549); once dropped, the tile re-enables, and `submitContestEntry` dedupes via `onConflictDoNothing` → returns null → entries.post.ts:67 throws the misleading "you may have already entered this project, or reached the contest's entry limit." (4) Tab chip and Participants roster (index.vue:113, 85, 703) cap at 20 / a truncated distinct-author set. The only thing standing between the code and the failure is a contest not yet having 21 visible entries — a data condition, not a guard. No flag blocks it, no validator rejects it, and I found no test pinning the fetch size. Severity P1 stands: contests are the live funnel (deveco contest active), the failure is silent, and its worst branch strands a paying-attention participant who cannot submit their round artifact while being invited to create a duplicate entry. Fix is one line: pass `{ query: { limit: 100 } }` like results.vue, and ideally read `total` to drive a load-more.

</details>

#### [P2] Proposal-mode stage with an empty submissionTemplate silently disables the proposal path

`layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:253`

`currentProposalStage` requires `stage.submissionTemplate?.length`, and ContestProposalForm additionally guards `v-if="template.length"`, so a stage configured `kind:'submission', submissionMode:'proposal'` with an empty template renders no proposal form at all; the stage editor offers the mode and the template as two independent controls and never warns that one without the other is inert.

**Failure:** An organizer opens the Stages tab, sets "How entrants submit" to "Proposal form (creates a draft project)" (ContestStageCard.vue:148-156, whose hint promises "Proposal mode lets entrants apply with just this form"), and leaves the separate "Submission form, this stage" builder empty — encouraged to do so by that builder's own hint, "Optional... Leave empty if entering a project is enough" (ContestStageCard.vue:168). Note also `onTemplateUpdate` (ContestStageCard.vue:46) writes `undefined` when the array is cleared, so deleting the last field silently produces this state from a previously working configuration. On the live page `currentProposalStage` is null and `currentSubmissionStage` is null too (index.vue:235 has the same `submissionTemplate?.length` requirement), so an authenticated, fully registered participant on the Entries tab sees only the generic "Enter this contest / Submit one of your published projects, or start a new one" CTA. There is no form, no explanation, and nothing anywhere tells the organizer their proposal round is switched off. The server would happily accept a proposal for that stage (submitContestProposal validates mode and currency but not template emptiness, submissions.ts:432-440) — only the UI refuses to render the entry point.

**Suggested fix:** In ContestStageCard, when `stage.submissionMode === 'proposal' && !stage.submissionTemplate?.length`, render a blocking warning next to the mode select ("Proposal mode needs at least one form field, entrants will see no proposal form"), and reject the save in the contest PUT validator. Independently, decide whether an empty-template proposal stage should fall back to the attach path with an explicit message rather than rendering nothing.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code, but the impact is overstated and the render guard itself is intentional. Real and unguarded: index.vue:253 (and :235) gate on `submissionTemplate?.length`, so a stage with `submissionMode:'proposal'` and an empty template yields null for both computeds; ContestStageCard.vue:146-171 exposes the mode select and FormTemplateEditor as independent controls with contradictory hints; ContestStageCard.vue:46 writes `undefined` when the field array empties, so deleting the last field silently regresses a working proposal round; and nothing cross-validates — contestStageSchema (packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:210-243) has no refine tying submissionMode to submissionTemplate, ContestStagesEditor.vue never references either key, and submitContestProposal (submissions.ts:432-440) checks kind/mode/currency only. Reachable in production: I curled /api/features on deveco.io and commonpub.io and contestProposals is true on both. Corrections to the finding: (1) the empty-template no-render is deliberate and pinned by ContestProposalForm.test.ts:65 ('renders nothing when the stage has no submission template') — a zero-field proposal form would be a bare submit button creating an untitled draft, so the defect is the missing organizer warning, not the guard; (2) the entrant is not stranded — index.vue:645-666 deliberately keeps the attach CTA for proposal-mode contests, and submitContestEntry (entries.ts:225-270) has no stage/mode check, so attaching a published project still works, degrading the funnel rather than killing it; (3) the default authoring path is safe — standardContestTemplate (contestTemplates.ts:88-91) always seeds a template alongside proposal mode, so the broken state needs a manual mode flip with no fields or a cleared template.

</details>

#### [P2] Contest signup redirect is silently overridden by a referral claim, abandoning the registration intent

`layers/base/pages/auth/register.vue:79`

After signup the referral claim's `destination` replaces the caller's `?redirect`, so a stranger who signed up specifically to register for a contest is sent somewhere else with no indication that their contest registration never happened.

**Failure:** A stranger arrives at deveco.io via a referral link (referral cookie/code set, session 229), browses to the contest, presses "Log in to register", follows the login page's "create an account" link which carries `?redirect=/contests/<slug>/register` (login.vue:54), and completes signup. `target` is set to that path at line 72, then `/api/referrals/claim` returns a destination (e.g. the hub they were auto-joined to) and line 79 overwrites `target` with it. They land on the hub, never see the registration form, are not registered, and nothing tells them the contest step was dropped. Because registration is the server-side gate on entering (entries.post.ts:40-47), they subsequently hit a 403 if they try to submit.

**Suggested fix:** Prefer an explicit `?redirect` over the referral destination (`if (!route.query.redirect && res?.destination) target = res.destination`), or keep the referral destination but surface a toast/onward link back to the contest registration.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. register.vue:72-84 seeds `target` from `route.query.redirect` then unconditionally overwrites it with the referral claim's `destination`; the explicit caller intent has no precedence. The path is real: ContestHero.vue:232, ContestActionBar.vue:82, ContestSignup.vue:41 and pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:670 all point at /auth/login?redirect=/contests/<slug>/register, and login.vue:52-55 forwards that redirect to /auth/register. The override fires broadly, not just for onboarding actions: packages/server/src/referral/attribution.ts:48-62 returns a redirect action, ELSE link.landingPath, else a non-private join_hub hub, so any promo link with a landing path produces a destination. The carrier survives the browse — /r/[code].get.ts sets cpub_ref with maxAge = attributionWindowDays * 86400 (default 60 days). No guard, validator or test blocks it: registerConsent.test.ts:34 mocks referralLinks as false so the branch is never exercised, and no unit or E2E test pins redirect precedence. The flag default OFF does not save it: `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns "referralLinks":true, i.e. it is enabled on the instance running the live contest. Downstream consequence confirmed at entries.post.ts (contestEntryRequiresRegistration default ON) which 403s a non-`full` entrant. Git history (69af81f3, session 229) shows the "prefer its destination" comment predates the login/register redirect carry-through, so it is an oversight, not a deliberate precedence rule. Severity P2 is right: compound precondition (live referral cookie + redirect-carrying signup) keeps it off the every-signup path, but it is live in production on the contest instance and fails silently.

</details>

### Data integrity, transactions and concurrency

#### [P2] reconcile-counters.mjs recomputes hubs.member_count including pending join requests, corrupting it

`scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:43`

The `hubs.member_count` task recomputes `count(*) FROM hub_members` with no `status` filter, but the application only increments the counter for `status='active'` rows — `requestToJoinHub` deliberately inserts a `pending` row WITHOUT bumping the count.

**Failure:** Hub H has `joinPolicy='approval'`, 10 active members and 5 outstanding join requests. `member_count` is correctly 10 and `listMembers` (which filters `status='active'`) returns 10. An operator runs `node scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs` — documented as "Safe to run on production (only UPDATEs rows whose stored counter already disagrees)" — and it rewrites `member_count` to 15. The hub card now advertises 15 members while the roster shows 10, and `--check` reports permanent "drift" on every subsequent run.

**Suggested fix:** Add `AND m.status = 'active'` to the hubs.member_count recompute, mirroring the events.attendee_count task.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:40-44 recomputes hubs.member_count as `(SELECT count(*) FROM hub_members m WHERE m.hub_id = t.id)` with no status predicate, while `hub_member_status` is a two-value enum ('pending','active') (packages/schema/src/enums.ts:100) and every application write treats member_count as the ACTIVE-only count: joinHub bumps +1 only on the active insert (members.ts:84); requestToJoinHub inserts `{status:'pending'}` and its own doc comment says "WITHOUT bumping memberCount (the count tracks active members; the bump happens at approve-time)" (members.ts:118-140); approveJoinRequest does the +1 tied to the pending→active flip (members.ts:229); leaveHub explicitly deletes a pending row WITHOUT decrementing, again commented "(a pending request never incremented it)" (members.ts:297-304). So a pending row contributes 0 to the stored counter but 1 to the recompute. No guard makes this unreachable: joinPolicy is a plain hub column defaulting to 'open' with an 'approval' value (packages/schema/src/hub.ts:44, enums.ts:95), behind no feature flag, and the approval workflow ships real routes (layers/base/server/api/hubs/[slug]/requests.get.ts and .../requests/[userId]/approve.post.ts). listMembers/listJoinRequests both filter on status, so the roster stays at the active number while the recomputed counter would include pending. The omission is demonstrably an oversight rather than intent: the sibling events.attendee_count task at line 55 does carry `AND a.status = 'registered'`, and the file's own header only documents federated-remainder exclusions, not a status semantics choice. Git history shows only two touches (a86d4d7a initial, 4aab8302 the remote_like_count fix) — nothing that deliberately chose all-rows for hub_members. No test anywhere pins the recompute (grep for the SQL string returns only the script itself; scripts/ has no __tests__). Reachability of the operator action is documented: docs/STATUS.md:758-759 and docs/ROADMAP.md:72 make `reconcile-counters --check` a per-droplet release step expecting 0 drift, so an approval hub with outstanding requests produces a standing false "drift" report and running the fixer writes the inflated value (also federated out as `cpub:memberCount`, packages/server/src/federation/hubFederation.ts:121). P2 is right: it corrupts a publicly displayed and federated counter, but only for approval-policy hubs with outstanding requests and only when an operator runs the script — not a path a user hits directly.

</details>

#### [P2] Two tables both drive hub_posts.like_count; the reconciler reads one and deletes the other's likes

`packages/server/src/social/social.ts:115`

Hub-post likes are recorded in `hub_post_likes` by `likePost`, and ALSO in the generic `likes` table by `toggleLike` when `targetType='post'` — both increment the same `hub_posts.like_count`. `reconcile-counters.mjs` recomputes that counter purely from `hub_post_likes` + `remote_like_count`, so every like taken through the generic endpoint is erased on reconcile; and because the two read paths check different tables, the same user can like one post twice.

**Failure:** A user POSTs `/api/social/like` with `{targetType:'post', targetId:<hubPostId>}` — the route validator `likeTargetTypeSchema` accepts 'post' — inserting into `likes` and bumping `hub_posts.like_count` to 1. They then hit `/api/hubs/:slug/posts/:postId/like`: `likePost` checks `hub_post_likes` (empty), inserts, and bumps the counter to 2 for a single user. Later an operator runs reconcile-counters: `like_count` is recomputed as `count(hub_post_likes)+remote_like_count` = 1, silently discarding every like ever recorded via `/api/social/like`.

**Suggested fix:** Pick one storage table for hub-post likes. Simplest: reject `targetType==='post'` in the `/api/social/like` route (or route it to `likePost`), and add `hub_post_likes` rows for any existing `likes` rows with `target_type='post'` before the next reconcile run.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every link in the chain. `likeTargetTypeSchema` (packages/schema/src/validators/social.ts:5-13) accepts 'post', and the DB enum `like_target_type` (packages/schema/src/enums.ts:20-28, migrations/0000_session128_baseline.sql:25) also contains 'post', so the insert does not throw. The route layers/base/server/api/social/like.post.ts gates only on `requireFeature('social')` (default true, packages/config/src/schema.ts:27) and `requireAuth` — no hub membership, ban, or privacy check. toggleLike (social.ts:55-56) inserts into `likes` and updateLikeCount case 'post' (social.ts:115-125) bumps `hub_posts.like_count`, guarded for idempotency only by the `likes_user_target` unique constraint on the `likes` table (packages/schema/src/social.ts:39). likePost (packages/server/src/hub/posts.ts:421-435) guards on a different table (`hub_post_likes` onConflictDoNothing) and bumps the same counter, so one user can reach +2. hasLikedPost reads hub_post_likes (posts.ts:482-493) while isLiked reads likes (social.ts:146-158) — divergent read paths. scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:58-64 recomputes hub_posts.like_count as count(hub_post_likes)+remote_like_count only, so generic-table likes are silently discarded; the file's own header shows the author reasoned about the federated source but not about the second local source. No flag, validator, or test blocks the path — the only `targetType: 'post'` usages in tests are for comments, where the shared generic table is genuinely the design. Not refuted. Severity stays P2 rather than higher because no shipped UI sends 'post' to /api/social/like (useEngagement.ts:125 is mounted only from ProjectView/ArticleView/ExplainerView with content-item types; hub post likes use the dedicated route), so it needs a hand-made API call, and the inflation is bounded at 2x per user. Aggravating detail found while verifying: the generic path also bypasses checkBan and hub privacy that the hub route enforces, and still fires an author notification (social.ts:77-90), so a non-member or banned user can notify a private-hub post author — further evidence 'post' on this endpoint is an unintended surface.

</details>

#### [P2] Counter decrements are not conditional on the delete removing a row, so concurrent deletes drift counters

`packages/server/src/contest/entries.ts:399`

Several delete paths read the row, then unconditionally issue `GREATEST(counter - 1, 0)` without checking that their own DELETE actually removed anything. Two concurrent requests for the same row both pass the pre-check, one DELETE affects zero rows, and the counter is decremented twice. For `content_items.comment_count` and `hub_posts.reply_count` this drift is permanent — reconcile-counters.mjs explicitly excludes both.

**Failure:** Concrete case, `deleteComment`: user double-clicks "delete" on their comment. Both requests SELECT the comment (found), both compute `totalDeleted = 1`, both run `DELETE FROM comments WHERE id=…` (the second removes 0 rows), and both run `commentCount = GREATEST(commentCount - 1, 0)`. A project with 5 comments now reports 3 forever: the reconciler's header comment lists `content_items.comment_count` as deliberately NOT reconcilable. Same shape in `withdrawContestEntry` (entries.ts:399-404 -> `contests.entry_count` on the live deveco contest), `leaveHub` (members.ts:310-317), `kickMember` (members.ts:549-559, which is not even wrapped in a transaction), `unlikePost` (posts.ts:474-475) and `deleteReply` (posts.ts:711-715 -> non-reconcilable `reply_count`).

**Suggested fix:** Follow the `deletePost` pattern everywhere: `.returning({id})` on the DELETE and skip the decrement when zero rows came back. Move `kickMember`'s two statements into one transaction while you are there.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every cited site. entries.ts:398-404 (withdrawContestEntry) deletes without .returning() and decrements contests.entryCount unconditionally; the owner/status pre-check at 366-384 is a SELECT outside the transaction, so it is advisory only. social/social.ts:463-502 (deleteComment) is worse than claimed: it is not wrapped in a transaction at all, computing totalDeleted from a pre-check then subtracting it regardless of rows affected. Same shape confirmed at members.ts:307-317 (leaveHub, in a tx) and 549-558 (kickMember, NOT in a tx), posts.ts:474-475 (unlikePost) and 709-715 (deleteReply). No upstream guard exists: both DELETE routes (layers/base/server/api/social/comments/[id].delete.ts, .../entries/[entryId].delete.ts) are requireFeature+requireAuth+parseParams+call with no idempotency key, and there is no rate limiter on them (rateLimit appears only in public-api-auth.ts and an admin route). No client guard either: CommentSection.vue:132-135 deleteComment() has no in-flight flag and the delete buttons at 203-210/232-239 carry no :disabled, unlike the submit button, so a double-click really does issue two DELETEs. The pattern is not deliberate: the matching INCREMENT paths in the same files are explicitly hardened against this exact race (likePost uses onConflictDoNothing().returning() with the comment 'Use ON CONFLICT to handle concurrent requests atomically', posts.ts:426; submitContestEntry takes pg_advisory_xact_lock with a TOCTOU comment, entries.ts:276), and deletePost at posts.ts:247-262 is the correct contrast ('Only decrement postCount when a row was actually removed', from commit fe8e3131). The reconciler exclusion is real: scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:19-22 states content_items.comment_count and hub_posts.reply_count are deliberately not reconcilable, so deleteComment and deleteReply drift is permanent; contests.entry_count, hubs.member_count and hub_posts.like_count ARE listed and recoverable by running the script. No test pins the behaviour (the contest suite has concurrency tests for voting at line 949 and judge scoring at 1311, none for delete/decrement). Severity P2 stands: the window requires both requests to SELECT before either DELETE commits, GREATEST(...,0) prevents negatives, and 4 of 6 counters are reconcilable, so it is not P1; but comment delete is a daily path with no double-click guard and comment_count/reply_count drift is unrecoverable, so it is not P3.

</details>

#### [P2] tags.usage_count is never written, so the public trending-tags endpoint is always empty

`packages/server/src/publicApi/metrics.ts:250`

`getTrendingTags` reads the denormalized `tags.usage_count` and filters `> 0`, but nothing in the repo ever increments it — `setContentTags` writes `content_tags` join rows only, there is no reconciler task for it, and no migration backfills it. The column is 0 for every tag on every instance.

**Failure:** `GET /api/public/v1/metrics/tags/trending?limit=10` on any live instance returns `{ items: [] }` regardless of how many tagged projects exist, because `WHERE usage_count > 0` matches nothing. Meanwhile `GET /api/public/v1/tags` returns correct counts for the same tags, because that route computes them live with `count(content_tags.content_id)`. Two documented public endpoints disagree about the same data.

**Suggested fix:** Derive it in `getTrendingTags` with a join+count on `content_tags` (matching the /tags route), or add a reconciler task plus increments in `setContentTags`. Do not leave a filter on a column nothing maintains.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. packages/server/src/publicApi/metrics.ts:246-254 filters `.where(gt(tags.usageCount, 0))` and orders by that column. A repo-wide grep for usageCount|usage_count (excluding node_modules/.output/.nuxt) yields only the column definition (packages/schema/src/content.ts:190), the baseline migration default (0000_session128_baseline.sql:269), the serializer, this read, the OpenAPI schema entry, the live-derived count in layers/base/server/api/public/v1/tags/index.get.ts:26, and test fixtures — there is no UPDATE, increment, backfill, DB trigger (no TRIGGER in any migration), and no tasks/cron directory anywhere. The real tag writer is `syncTags` at packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1032-1062 (the finding calls it `setContentTags`, the only inaccuracy in the report): it deletes/inserts content_tags rows and does insert(tags).onConflictDoNothing(), so new tags land with the schema default 0 and are never updated. No upstream guard prevents the failure: layers/base/server/middleware/public-api-auth.ts:34 404s the surface only when features.publicApi is off, and I verified live state empirically rather than trusting the default — https://deveco.io/api/features returns "publicApi":true and GET /api/public/v1/metrics/tags/trending returns 401 (feature on, API key required) while commonpub.io returns 404 (feature off). So on deveco.io any read:analytics key holder gets a permanently empty array. The integration test is green only because packages/server/src/__tests__/metrics.integration.test.ts:77-81 seeds usageCount: 50/10/0 directly, bypassing syncTags. git log -S "gt(tags.usageCount, 0)" shows a single commit f3e79e86 with no follow-up, and the route docblock ("Tags ranked by lifetime usage count") shows the author assumed a maintained counter that was never wired up — an oversight, not deliberate. Severity P2 is correct: a documented public endpoint is 100% broken on a live instance and contradicts /api/public/v1/tags for the same data, but it is gated behind a non-default feature flag plus a scoped API key, fails silently with an empty set rather than corrupting data, and no browser-facing user path consumes it.

</details>

#### [P2] No per-inbox delivery ledger: one failing inbox re-delivers to every healthy follower on retry

`packages/server/src/federation/delivery.ts:221`

`deliverActivity` fans one `activities` row out to N follower inboxes in a loop and then records a single all-or-nothing outcome. Any non-empty `errors` array (including the synthetic "circuit open (skipped)" entry) marks the whole activity for retry, and the retry re-POSTs the body to every inbox that already accepted it. After `maxRetries` the activity is marked `failed` and dead-lettered even though it reached everyone except the one broken host.

**Failure:** A user with 400 followers across 12 instances publishes a project. One instance is down, so `recordDeliveryFailure` trips its circuit breaker. Delivery 1: 399 inboxes accept, 1 error -> `incrementAttempts`. Deliveries 2-6 (backoff 1m/5m/30m/2h/12h): the circuit is open for that domain, so `errors` is non-empty every single time, and all 399 healthy inboxes are POSTed the identical Create six more times — 2,394 redundant signed requests, and duplicates on any receiver that doesn't dedupe by activity id. On attempt 6 the row flips to `status='failed'` + `deadLetteredAt`, so operator dashboards report a delivery failure for an activity 399/400 followers received.

**Suggested fix:** Track per-(activity, inbox) delivery state (an `activity_deliveries` row, or a jsonb array of settled inboxes on the activity) and, on retry, only POST to inboxes not yet marked delivered. Treat circuit-open inboxes as "deferred" rather than "error" so they don't burn the attempt budget.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading packages/server/src/federation/delivery.ts in full. deliverActivity (:150-224) loops all target inboxes accumulating one shared `errors` array; the only partial-outcome guard is `if (skippedCircuitOpen === targetInboxes.length) return` (:214-217), which fires only when EVERY inbox was skipped. Any single failing/skipped inbox leaves `errors.length > 0`, so `incrementAttempts` (:532-554) runs, and at `newAttempts >= maxRetries` (default 6 via DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS and layers/base/server/plugins/federation-delivery.ts `maxDeliveryRetries ?? 6`) the row flips to status 'failed' + deadLetteredAt even though most inboxes accepted. On each retry `resolveTargetInboxes` (:250-450) recomputes the full follower set from followRelationships/hubFollowers with no filter for inboxes that already returned 2xx. No per-inbox ledger exists anywhere (grep for activity_deliveries/deliveredInboxes finds nothing; processed_activities in activityDedup.ts is inbound-only). No test pins partial-delivery behaviour (delivery.integration.test.ts and delivery-retry.test.ts cover only single-inbox resolution and no-inbox cases). The path is live: the worker gates only on config.features.federation, which is enabled. Two magnitude corrections that do NOT rescue the code: resolveFollowerInboxes prefers sharedInbox and the caller dedupes with new Set(), so 400 followers across 12 instances collapses to ~12 targets rather than 399, and the circuit breaker needs 50 consecutive failures (DEFAULT_FAILURE_THRESHOLD) rather than one down host — but without the circuit ever opening the broken inbox errors on every pass, so all healthy inboxes still get the identical signed body re-POSTed on all six attempts. Receiver-side dedupe (CommonPub by activity id, Mastodon by object id) limits duplicate side effects but not the redundant signed traffic or the false 'failed'/dead-lettered status an operator sees for an activity nearly everyone received. Severity P2 stands: live daily path, no data loss, misleading delivery accounting plus wasted repeated delivery.

</details>

#### [P3] Check-then-insert against a UNIQUE constraint: double-click on like/bookmark/vote returns 500

`packages/server/src/social/social.ts:55`

`toggleLike`, `toggleBookmark`, `voteOnPost` and `voteOnPoll` all SELECT for an existing row and then INSERT inside a READ COMMITTED transaction with no `onConflictDoNothing` and no row lock. The target tables all carry a UNIQUE constraint, so two concurrent requests from the same user both miss on the SELECT (neither sees the other's uncommitted insert) and the loser raises a unique violation that aborts the transaction and surfaces as an unhandled 500.

**Failure:** A user double-taps the Like button on a contest entry (or the browser retries the POST). Request A and B both run `SELECT id FROM likes WHERE user_id=… AND target_type='project' AND target_id=…` -> empty. A inserts and commits. B's `INSERT INTO likes` violates `likes_user_target` -> `db.transaction` rolls back and throws -> `/api/social/like` returns 500 instead of the toggle result. Identical for `bookmarks_user_target`, `uq_hub_post_votes_post_user` and `uq_poll_votes_post_user`.

**Suggested fix:** Append `.onConflictDoNothing({ target: [...] }).returning({id})` and branch on an empty `returning()`, exactly as `toggleBuildMark`/`rsvpEvent` do.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. social.ts:36-56 (toggleLike) and 504-530 (toggleBookmark) do SELECT-then-bare-INSERT inside db.transaction with no onConflictDoNothing and no row lock; voting.ts:73 (voteOnPost) and :184 (voteOnPoll) have the identical shape. The UNIQUE constraints exist as claimed (schema/src/social.ts likes_user_target, bookmarks_user_target; schema/src/voting.ts:21 uq_hub_post_votes_post_user, :53 uq_poll_votes_post_user). No guard makes it unreachable: layers/base/server/utils/db.ts:31 is plain drizzle(pool) on node-postgres with no isolationLevel and no retry wrapper, so READ COMMITTED applies and the loser's INSERT raises 23505 after the winner commits; there is no global duplicate-key handler (23505 is handled only locally in notification.ts, federation/mirroring.ts and persona/consent.ts, none on this path); the routes layers/base/server/api/social/like.post.ts and bookmark.post.ts have no try/catch so the throw becomes a 500; config/src/schema.ts:27 sets features.social default true; and there is no in-flight guard client-side (useEngagement.ts:111-133 has no pending ref, EngagementBar.vue:48 has no :disabled), so a double-tap really does issue two overlapping POSTs. The remedy is established repo practice 60 lines away: voteOnContestEntry (voting.ts:240-251) uses onConflictDoNothing().returning() with a comment naming exactly this race and the unhandled 500. Severity corrected to P3 rather than P2: the transaction rolls back cleanly so counters are not corrupted, the client catches the error and reverts its optimistic state, and the window is only the losing transaction's duration — the cost is an occasional spurious 500 and error-log noise on an otherwise daily path.

</details>

#### [P3] deleteUser compensates only 4 of the ~9 counters its cascade destroys, and mis-decrements memberCount

`packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:672`

`deleteUser` hand-decrements like/comment/hub-member/enrollment counters before `DELETE FROM users`, but every other user-scoped table with `ON DELETE cascade` is missed, so their denormalized counters are left overcounting. Additionally the membership loop does not filter `status='active'`, so a pending join request decrements a counter it never incremented.

**Failure:** A deveco contest participant deletes their own account via `POST /api/auth/delete-user`. Postgres cascades `contest_entries.user_id` -> their entry row vanishes, but `contests.entry_count` is never decremented, so the public contest page permanently overstates the field. Same for `event_attendees` -> `events.attendee_count`, `content_builds` -> `content_items.build_count`, `hub_post_votes` -> `hub_posts.vote_score`, `poll_votes` -> `poll_options.vote_count`, `hub_post_likes` -> `hub_posts.like_count`, and completed enrollments -> `learning_paths.completion_count`. Separately: a user with only a *pending* request to join an approval hub deletes their account, and admin.ts:672-681 decrements that hub's `member_count` even though `requestToJoinHub` never incremented it — the hub loses a member from its count.

**Suggested fix:** Add `eq(hubMembers.status,'active')` to the memberships select, and add compensating decrements for contest_entries/event_attendees/content_builds/hub_post_votes/poll_votes/hub_post_likes — or delete those rows explicitly through the same functions that own their counters.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. admin.ts:612-700 compensates only likes/comments/hub_members/enrollments, then does `tx.delete(users)`. The cascades named are real and applied: contest_entries_user_id, event_attendees_user_id, content_builds_user_id, hub_post_likes_user_id all `ON DELETE cascade` in migrations/0000_session128_baseline.sql:1075-1127, plus hub_post_votes.user_id and poll_votes.user_id cascade in packages/schema/src/voting.ts. Each has a live denormalized counter incremented on write and read on a public path: contests.entry_count (entries.ts:297, submissions.ts:498/613 -> read.ts:112, serializers.ts:534, ContestHero.vue), events.attendee_count (events.ts:383), content_items.build_count (content.ts:1112), hub_posts.vote_score (voting.ts:54), poll_options.vote_count, learning_paths.completion_count (learning.ts:785). Note hub post likes live in the SEPARATE hubPostLikes table (posts.ts:428), so admin.ts's `case 'post'` on the generic `likes` table does not cover them. No guard upstream: /api/auth/delete-user (delete-user.post.ts:57) is requireAuth only, the admin route is behind requireFeature('admin'), and neither does extra compensation. No test in packages/server references deleteUser. The memberCount sub-claim also holds: members.ts:122 documents that a pending row does NOT bump memberCount and leaveHub (members.ts:298) skips the decrement for pending, while admin.ts:672-675 has no status predicate. (Internal inconsistency worth noting: scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs:40-44 recomputes member_count as unfiltered count(*), disagreeing with the increment path.) Severity lowered to P3: scripts/reconcile-counters.mjs recomputes exactly these eight counters idempotently and `--check` is a standing per-droplet release step (docs/STATUS.md:758-759, docs/ROADMAP.md:72), so the drift is routinely detectable and repairable rather than permanent; account deletion is rare and the impact is an off-by-N public number with no data loss or leak.

</details>

#### [P3] rsvpEvent reads attendeeCount without a lock, so concurrent RSVPs can exceed event capacity

`packages/server/src/events/events.ts:357`

The capacity decision reads `events.attendee_count` with a plain SELECT (no `FOR UPDATE`) and the increment is a separate statement, so two transactions can both observe the same sub-capacity count and both register.

**Failure:** An event has `capacity = 50` and `attendee_count = 49`. Two users RSVP within the same millisecond. Both transactions SELECT `attendeeCount = 49`, both evaluate `49 >= 50` as false, both insert with `status:'registered'` (different users, so the `event_attendees_event_user_unique` constraint does not fire), and both run `attendeeCount + 1`. The event ends at 51 registered attendees against a capacity of 50, and the waitlist never engaged.

**Suggested fix:** Add `.for('update')` to the events select so concurrent RSVPs serialize on the event row (the same fix already used in `transferOwnership` and `judgeContestEntry`).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading /Users/obsidian/Projects/ossuary-projects/commonpub/packages/server/src/events/events.ts:326-388. The capacity read at line 332-337 is a plain `.select({status, capacity, attendeeCount}).from(events).where(eq(events.id, eventId)).limit(1)` with no `.for('update')`; the decision at line 357-358 (`event.capacity && event.attendeeCount >= event.capacity ? 'waitlisted' : 'registered'`) and the increment at 382-385 (`sql\`${events.attendeeCount} + 1\``) are separate statements inside the same READ COMMITTED transaction, so two concurrent RSVPs from DIFFERENT users can both read 49/50 and both insert 'registered'. Nothing guards it: (a) the `event_attendees_event_user_unique` constraint plus `onConflictDoNothing` added in commit 70adb9e2 deliberately fixes only the SAME-user double-click race, not the capacity race — the commit message says exactly that; (b) packages/schema/src/events.ts has no CHECK constraint tying attendee_count to capacity and migration 0000 declares `capacity integer` bare; (c) no isolation-level override anywhere (grep for isolationLevel/transaction_isolation returns nothing), so the transaction is Postgres-default READ COMMITTED; (d) grep shows ZERO tests touching rsvpEvent — concurrency.integration.test.ts covers content versions and likes only, so no test pins the behaviour either way. The inconsistency is telling: the same codebase uses `.for('update')` for exactly this read-modify-write class in content.ts:947, learning.ts:688, admin.ts:353 and federation/timeline.ts:245, and rsvpEvent is the one that skips it — the line-355 comment "atomic within transaction" is simply wrong. Severity P3 is right, not higher: the only caller is layers/base/server/api/events/[slug]/rsvp.post.ts which begins with `requireFeature('events')`, and `events` defaults to false in both packages/config/src/schema.ts:84 and layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:119 (env/DB override can flip it, so it is not unreachable); the window is one statement wide, it only bites events that both set a `capacity` and are at the boundary, and the overshoot is bounded by the number of simultaneous requests and self-corrects on cancelRsvp's decrement plus FIFO waitlist promotion. Real but low-impact and behind a default-off flag.

</details>

#### [P3] publishDueScheduled swallows side-effect failures with no retry: post goes live, never federates

`packages/server/src/content/content.ts:924`

The claim UPDATE commits `status='published'` and clears `scheduled_at` before the side effects run. `createContentVersion` + `onContentPublished` (federation Create, search indexing, hooks) then run in a try/catch that only logs. Because `scheduled_at` is already NULL and status is already 'published', the item can never be re-claimed, so the side effects are dropped permanently.

**Failure:** A blog post is scheduled for 09:00. The worker tick at 09:00 flips it to 'published' (committed). Meilisearch is briefly unreachable / the federation insert times out / the process is restarted by a deploy mid-loop. The catch logs `[scheduled-publishing] post-publish side effects failed` and moves on. The post is live on the site but has no `content_versions` snapshot, is absent from search, and no `Create` activity was ever queued — so no follower on any remote instance ever sees it, and nothing will ever retry.

**Suggested fix:** Either run the side effects before clearing the claim (leave a `published_side_effects_at` marker column the worker can re-scan), or enqueue the federation/index work into a durable queue inside the same transaction as the status flip.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED, with two corrections to the evidence and a severity downgrade. What I verified by reading code: 1. `packages/server/src/content/content.ts:900-928` — `publishDueScheduled` is exactly as described. The claim is a single `db.update(contentItems).set({status:'published', publishedAt: COALESCE(...), scheduledAt: null, updatedAt}).where(status='scheduled' AND deletedAt IS NULL AND scheduledAt <= now).returning({id, authorId})`, committed on its own. Then a plain `for (const item of claimed) { try { createContentVersion; onContentPublished } catch { console.error } }`. Side effects are outside the claiming statement, the catch is log-only, and the two `where` predicates are both falsified by the claim, so a dropped item can never be re-claimed. The idempotency test at `packages/server/src/__tests__/content.integration.test.ts:440-448` actively pins that (`second` run returns 0). No retry column, no dead-letter, no sweeper: `ls layers/base/server/plugins/` shows no job that re-federates published content missing an activity. 2. Not behind a flag. `layers/base/server/plugins/scheduled-publishing.ts` starts unconditionally (only `NODE_ENV === 'test'` skips), and scheduling is a live user-facing control — `layers/base/components/editors/ArticleEditor.vue:551-561` ("Schedule for later" + datetime-local) → `layers/base/composables/useContentSave.ts:278` → `POST /api/content/[id]/schedule`. Session 199's log explicitly says "multi-replica safety comes from the atomic UPDATE claim, not a feature flag", and records no decision to drop side-effect failures, so this is an oversight, not a documented tradeoff. CORRECTION 1 (mechanism): federation errors never reach the cited catch. `onContentPublished` (content.ts:1352-1394) wraps `federateContent` in its own try/catch, logs `[federation] publish:` and RETURNS `{federated:false, error}` — and `publishDueScheduled` discards that return value. Same for search: `emitHook` (hooks.ts:190-200) try/catches every handler individually, and `layers/base/server/plugins/search-index.ts:31-37` try/catches `indexContent` again, so an unreachable Meilisearch is swallowed twice before it could propagate. So the search half of the failure scenario is a pre-existing property of EVERY publish path (`layers/base/server/api/content/[id]/publish.post.ts:15` discards the same result object), not something this catch causes. The cited catch only ever fires on a DB fault in `createContentVersion` or in `onContentPublished`'s SELECT/apObjectId UPDATE. The outcome the finding describes — dropped permanently, nothing retries — is nonetheless correct, just realized one frame lower. CORRECTION 2 (impact): "no follower on any remote instance ever sees it" is overstated. `packages/server/src/federation/federation.ts:561-620` shows `federateContent` does only local DB work (SELECTs + one INSERT into `activities`), and `packages/server/src/federation/outboxQueries.ts:1-24` documents that both the instance and per-user outboxes are a PROJECTION over `content_items`, not a scan of the activities queue. A dropped Create costs the push delivery to existing followers' inboxes; the post remains in the actor's outbox and is still reachable by pull/backfill. Manual repair also exists: `layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/refederate.post.ts` (idempotent, `contentId`-scoped) and `layers/base/server/api/admin/search/reindex.post.ts`. Severity: P3, not P2. Triggering it needs an infrastructure fault or a SIGTERM landing in the millisecond window between the claim commit and the per-item side effects (the realistic case being a deploy restart mid-sweep with several posts due at the same minute) — not a path a user hits on a normal day. When it does fire the loss is silent with no alerting, but the durable damage is a missing `content_versions` snapshot plus a missing push Create, both operator-repairable, with the outbox projection preventing the content itself from becoming invisible to remote instances. The right fix is still cheap: claim to an intermediate state (or re-arm `scheduled_at`/record a `publish_attempts` counter) and only clear it after the side effects succeed.

</details>

### Privacy, PII and data protection

#### [P1] /api/users and /api/search?type=people enumerate every account, ignoring profileVisibility and status

`layers/base/server/api/users/index.get.ts:26`

Both the member directory listing and the people search build their WHERE from `isNull(users.deletedAt)` alone — no `profileVisibility = 'public'`, no `status = 'active'`, no auth — so private and suspended members are listed and searchable by anonymous callers.

**Failure:** Anonymous `GET /api/users?limit=50&offset=0` (or `GET /api/search?q=al&type=people`) returns id, username, displayName, headline, avatarUrl and followerCount for every row in `users`, including members who chose "Only me" and accounts an admin has suspended. Paging with offset enumerates the entire membership roster of the instance.

**Suggested fix:** Add `eq(users.profileVisibility,'public')` (widened to include 'members' only for a signed-in viewer) and `eq(users.status,'active')` to both condition lists; factor the predicate into one exported helper so a third listing cannot be written without it.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code; no guard, flag, or test blocks it. (1) layers/base/server/api/users/index.get.ts:22 is literally `const conditions = [isNull(users.deletedAt)]`, with only optional ids/search predicates pushed after; the select at :36-49 returns id/username/displayName/headline/avatarUrl/createdAt plus a joined followerCount. search/index.get.ts:59-62 is the same one-predicate WHERE. (2) No auth: middleware/auth.ts only resolves and enriches a session, never requires one for these paths; nuxt.config.ts:186 is `routeRules: {}`; security.ts is rate-limit/CSP only; neither handler calls requireUser/getOptionalUser. (3) No flag: explore.vue:194 renders the People tab unconditionally (Hubs/Learn immediately above ARE flag-gated) and fires useFetch('/api/users') at :119; search.vue:45 pushes the People pill unconditionally; neither page has definePageMeta middleware. (4) No test pins it — the sole file in api/users/__tests__ is persona-profile-route.test.ts, covering a different route; git history for index.get.ts is 3 commits, none about visibility. (5) The columns are real and user-settable: schema/src/auth.ts:28-29 (status default 'active', profileVisibility default 'public', enum public|members|private), set from settings/privacy.vue:917-919 via PUT /api/profile at :528. (6) The promise is explicit: privacy.vue:501-502 says private = "Only you can see your profile", members = "Only people signed in to this site can see your profile", and :815 claims "a member whose profile is private is never returned". (7) The cited contrast is accurate: public/v1/users/index.get.ts:29,38-42 requires `read:users` scope AND pushes eq(profileVisibility,'public') + eq(status,'active') + an isPublicUser filter. Scope is in fact WIDER than claimed: packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:35-40 (getUserByUsername, backing /api/users/[username]) and searchUsers at :30 also filter on deletedAt alone, so profileVisibility is unenforced across the whole internal profile surface — only the persona route (:235) and public/v1 honour it. Severity P1 is right: unauthenticated, unflagged, on a page the app loads by default, silently voiding a documented user-facing privacy control and permitting full roster enumeration via offset. Not P0 only because the leaked field set is limited to name/headline/avatar/followerCount (no email, bio, location, or credentials).

</details>

#### [P1] GET /api/users/:username serves a `private` profile in full to anonymous callers

`packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:35`

`getUserByUsername` filters only on `deletedAt` — it never reads `profile_visibility` or `status` — and `layers/base/server/api/users/[username].get.ts` calls it with no auth and no gate, so a profile set to "Only me" is returned in full to any unauthenticated request.

**Failure:** Alice sets Profile visibility = "Only me" on /settings/privacy (the page's own copy: `private: 'Only you can see your profile.'`, layers/base/pages/settings/privacy.vue:502). An anonymous `curl https://deveco.io/api/users/alice` returns 200 with her `bio`, `headline`, `location`, `website`, `bannerUrl`, `socialLinks`, `skills`, `experience`, `pronouns`, `emailNotifications` preferences, follower/following counts and view/like totals. Same for a suspended account (`status !== 'active'`). The sibling `/api/public/v1/users/[username].get.ts:33` does gate on `isPublicUser`, so the two endpoints disagree about the same row.

**Suggested fix:** Select `status` + `profileVisibility` in `getUserByUsername` and return null unless (public) or (members + signed-in viewer) or (viewer is the owner); pass the viewer down from the route. Reuse `listableUserWhere()` from persona/directory.ts so all four readers share one predicate.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:35-40 selects users filtered only on isNull(deletedAt) — no profileVisibility, no status — and returns bio/headline/location/website/bannerUrl/socialLinks/skills/experience/pronouns/emailNotifications (lines 86-100). layers/base/server/api/users/[username].get.ts calls it with no requireAuth/requireFeature/visibility check and has been untouched since commit 00790b98. I enumerated layers/base/server/middleware/* (auth, csrf, features, security, public-api-auth, theme, …) — none path-gates /api/users, so there is no upstream guard.
