Decisively, the team's own plan docs/plans/content-privacy-enforcement.md:157-159 lists this under DEFERRED: "profileVisibility — the enum/column exist and the public API reads them, but no writer sets it (default public, no settings UI/API), so it is a LATENT surface with nothing to leak today. Gate getUserByUsername (+ followers/following/feed) when a writer is added." Session 255 (b4ed0a1c) added the writer and never added the gate: validators/auth.ts:82 accepts profileVisibility, profile.ts:182 writes it, pages/settings/privacy.vue:528 PUTs it, and the control is unflagged (privacy.vue:901 "Profile visibility and the subject rights are NOT inside the sharing gate"; the <section> at :907 has no v-if), with the copy at :502 promising "Only you can see your profile." The sibling /api/public/v1/users/[username].get.ts:30 does gate (eq(users.status,'active') + isPublicUser, serializers.ts:74), and persona.get.ts:207-237 re-implements the gate locally with the verbatim comment the finding quotes. No test pins the correct behaviour — persona-profile-route.test.ts:575 only asserts the persona route avoids the helper. Scope is in fact wider than claimed: followers/following/content/learning/feed.xml/webfinger and the AP actor route routes/users/[username].ts share the same ungated reader, so a private profile also federates. Severity corrected to P1 rather than P0: it is live, unauthenticated and unflagged, but profile_visibility defaults to 'public', the control only shipped 2026-08-15, and the exposed fields are self-authored profile metadata the member had already published — the failure is that retraction silently does nothing, not that previously-secret data (credentials, email, private content) becomes readable.

</details>

#### [P2] Account deletion leaves the deleted user's DM text and id in `conversations`

`packages/schema/src/social.ts:166`

`conversations.participants` is a `jsonb string[]` of user ids with no foreign key, and `conversations.lastMessage` stores up to 200 characters of the last message body. `deleteUser` relies entirely on FK cascades, so deleting an account removes the `messages` rows but leaves the conversation row holding the erased user's id and their message text.

**Failure:** Bob DMs Alice "my address is 14 Foo St, call me on 555-0123", then deletes his account via /settings/account. `messages` cascades away (social.ts:180 senderId cascade) but the `conversations` row survives with `participants = ['<bob-uuid>','<alice-uuid>']` and `lastMessage = 'my address is 14 Foo St, call me on 555-0123'`. Alice's inbox (`listConversations`, matched by `participants @> [alice]`) still renders that preview forever, and opening it shows zero messages. Bob's Art. 17 request has not erased his message content.

**Suggested fix:** In `deleteUser`, inside the transaction, delete every `conversations` row whose `participants @> [userId]` (or, to keep the other party's own sent messages, strip the id from `participants` and null `lastMessage` when the last message was the deleted user's). Add a regression test that asserts no `conversations` row references a deleted user id.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every link in the chain. (1) packages/schema/src/social.ts:163-171 — `conversations` has `participants: jsonb().$type<string[]>().notNull()` and `lastMessage: text('last_message')`, neither with a FK; the baseline migration (packages/schema/migrations/0000_session128_baseline.sql:803-809) creates the table with no FK and no trigger, and `grep -rn "delete(conversations)|DELETE FROM conversations"` over packages+layers returns ZERO hits — nothing anywhere deletes a conversation row. (2) packages/server/src/messaging/messaging.ts:209-229 — `const truncated = truncateUnicode(body, 200)` then `.set({ lastMessageAt, lastMessage: truncated })`, so the row really does store up to 200 chars of the last message body. (3) packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:611-695 `deleteUser` only decrements like/comment/member/enrollment counters then `await tx.delete(users)…` — `conversations` is never mentioned. (4) The self-serve path is real and unguarded: layers/base/pages/settings/account.vue:53 POSTs `/api/auth/delete-user`, and layers/base/server/api/auth/delete-user.post.ts does federation cleanup + private-file purge + `await deleteUser(db, user.id, user.id)` — it explicitly comments "cascades to all related data", which is exactly the assumption that fails here. (5) No flag protects it: messaging has no `requireFeature` call anywhere under layers/base/server/api/messages/ (unlike every social route, which calls `requireFeature('social')`), so DMs are live on every instance. (6) The residue is user-visible: messaging.ts:38-46 `listConversations` matches only `participants @> [userId]` with no existence check on the other participant; layers/base/server/api/messages/index.get.ts:34-42 resolves participant ids against `users` and, when the row is gone, falls back to `{ username: id }` — so the counterparty's inbox renders the deleted user's raw UUID as their name; layers/base/pages/messages/index.vue:188 renders `conv.lastMessage` as the preview, and layers/base/server/api/messages/[conversationId].get.ts opens to zero messages (the `messages` rows did cascade via social.ts:180). So the failure scenario reproduces exactly as written. It is not deliberate: the repo's own docs/reviews/full-review-2026-07-17.md finding #20 already flagged this same line ("A deleted user leaves a ghost DM shell with stale preview… Fix: sweep on user delete or normalize to a join table") and it was never fixed. Severity corrected to P2, not P1: the retained text is bounded (only the single most recent message per conversation, and only where the deleted user sent it), instance-local (CLAUDE.md federation table has no messaging row; nothing serializes conversations through @commonpub/protocol), and visible only to the counterparty who already received that message — there is no exposure to a new audience. It is a genuine Art. 17 erasure gap on a live unflagged path, but a retention failure rather than a leak.

</details>

#### [P2] Password-reset links are written to stdout by the default email adapter

`layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:54`

`sendResetPasswordEmail` hands the reset URL (which carries the reset token) straight to the email adapter with no `isEmailDeliverable()` guard, while its sibling `sendVerificationEmail` twelve lines below does guard. `ConsoleEmailAdapter` — the DEFAULT adapter, and the silent fallback for a half-configured SMTP/Resend — `console.log`s the recipient address and the message body.

**Failure:** An instance runs with `NUXT_EMAIL_ADAPTER` unset, or with `smtp` set but `SMTP_PASS` missing (both resolve to `ConsoleEmailAdapter`, layers/base/server/utils/email.ts:29-40,63-67). Any visitor requests a password reset for admin@instance; the container log now contains `[EMAIL] To: admin@instance` and `[EMAIL] Body: ... /auth/reset-password?token=<valid token> ...`. Anyone with log access (operator, log shipper, CI artifact, `docker logs`) can take over any account, including admin. Secondarily, every notification/contest email body plus its recipient address is logged as PII.

**Suggested fix:** Add the same `if (!isEmailDeliverable()) return;` guard to `sendResetPasswordEmail`, and reduce `ConsoleEmailAdapter` to logging only the message id/subject (never `to`, never the body) unless an explicit `EMAIL_DEBUG_BODIES` flag is set.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full chain. (1) layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:54-57 — sendResetPasswordEmail calls emailAdapter.send() with no isEmailDeliverable() guard, while the sibling sendVerificationEmail at :69-72 does guard, with a comment stating "A console sink is not delivery." (2) layers/base/server/utils/email.ts:30 defaults the adapter to 'console', and :39-43 / :54-58 silently fall back to ConsoleEmailAdapter on half-configured SMTP/Resend with module-level `deliverable` left false (:5); production only console.warns (:65-67), it does not refuse. (3) packages/infra/src/email/adapters.ts:144-148 logs `To:`, `Subject:`, and `Body: ${message.text ?? message.html.slice(0,200)}` — the text branch is untruncated. (4) packages/infra/src/email/templates.ts:50 sets `text: "Reset your password: ${resetUrl}"`, so the full token-bearing URL is the logged body; no redaction exists in packages/infra/src/email/. (5) Reachable: pages/auth/forgot-password.vue:19 POSTs /api/auth/request-password-reset (linked from login.vue:222); that path is not in isCustomAuthRoute and is not the 404'd send-verification-email, so it dispatches to Better Auth, and packages/auth/src/createAuth.ts:64-68 wires sendResetPassword unconditionally with emailAndPassword.enabled = config.auth.emailPassword, which defaults true (packages/config/src/schema.ts:224). Refutations tried and rejected: not flag-gated (emailPassword defaults true, sender wired unconditionally unlike verification); not deliberate — `git log -L 53,58:layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts` shows commit 139c65c6 added the guard to sendVerificationEmail and left sendResetPasswordEmail byte-identical, with no comment exempting it; not pinned by any test — isEmailDeliverable has only four references and the sole test (resend-verification-route.test.ts:55) covers the resend route, while grep across layers/base finds sendResetPasswordEmail only at its definition. Severity corrected P1 -> P2: the leak is real and a live instance does run the console sink, but harm requires a second actor with host log access who on these single-operator self-hosted deployments is typically already the operator with DB access, and password reset is already non-functional on such an instance, so the one-line fix converts a token leak into a clean no-op rather than restoring a working feature.

</details>

#### [P2] Phone numbers (`tel`) are not personal data to the partition, and the one-click Phone preset ships without `pii`

`packages/schema/src/contest.ts:169`

`isFormFieldPii` — the single source of truth for the PII partition — returns false for `type: 'tel'` unless the operator explicitly sets `pii: true`, and the form-builder's "Phone" preset seeds exactly such a field with no `pii`. Phone numbers therefore land in the PUBLIC jsonb (`contest_registrations.fields` / `contestEntries.stageSubmissions`) and are released under the non-`contest.pii` gate.

**Failure:** An organizer opens the contest form builder and clicks the "Phone" preset (the palette's Contact group). Entrants type their mobile numbers. Those numbers are written to `contest_registrations.fields` rather than `contest_registration_private_fields`; a per-contest editor who holds NO `contest.pii` permission then sees them in `/api/contests/:slug/registrants` and downloads them in `registrants-export.csv` (the column survives the `includePii || !isPii(f)` filter), and on the entry side they appear in the "Submission" column that `summarizeArtifact` builds for every export regardless of `includePii`. On an instance with `features.contestPii` off, the per-field "personal data" checkbox is not even rendered, so the operator cannot correct it.

**Suggested fix:** Add `if (f.type === 'tel') return f.pii !== false;` to `isFormFieldPii` (same opt-out shape as email/signature), add `pii: true` + the "Stored privately" help string to the `tel` preset, and gate the preset behind `pii` like `address` so it is not offered on an instance that cannot partition it. A backfill is needed for any existing contest with a `tel` answer already in the public jsonb.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/schema/src/contest.ts:200-205 `isFormFieldPii` forces only address/file, defaults email/signature private, and returns false for `tel`; there is no tel case and no test pins it (validators.test.ts:1126-1135 omits tel). layers/base/utils/contestSubmissionTemplates.ts:69 seeds the one-click "Phone" preset as `{ label: 'Phone number', type: 'tel', required: false }` with no `pii`, and the preset itself carries no `pii: true` marker, so `availableFieldPresets(false)` still offers it when features.contestPii is off — while the comprehensive-intake template in the SAME file writes `{ label: 'Phone number', type: 'tel', required: false, pii: true }`, an internal contradiction. FormTemplateEditor.vue:86-92 places `tel` in the Contact group beside `email` (which IS default-private), and :484 renders the "Personal data" checkbox only `v-if="piiEnabled"`, so a contestPii-off operator has no UI to correct it. The write path is unflagged: packages/server/src/contest/validation.ts:21,103,184 `(isPiiField(field) ? pii : artifact)[field.key] = stored`, so the number lands in the public jsonb. Read paths confirm the exposure: registrations.ts:441-446 returns `fields` to every organizer and `privateFields` only when includePii; export.ts:176 `cols = template.filter(... (includePii || !isPii(f)))` keeps the phone column for a non-contest.pii requester; export.ts:36-40 summarizeArtifact dumps all artifact keys ungated. Refutations tried and rejected: (a) flag guard — contestPii gates only what the builder OFFERS, not the partition, and the tel preset is not flag-marked; (b) deliberate opt-in design — undercut by email being default-private in the same UI group and by the codebase's own `pii: true` tel field; (c) unreachable — reachable from both the registration and stage-submission builders (shared component). Severity lowered to P2: the audience is authenticated contest staff lacking only the fine-grained contest.pii permission plus organizer-gated CSVs (registrants.get.ts:29-36 403s non-managers; entries.ts:146 withholds stageSubmissions from non-owners), there is no unauthenticated/public leak, and it requires the operator to add a phone field — but it is a genuine misclassification of personal data with the wrong storage partition, which also skews DSAR/export/retention.

</details>

#### [P2] hub_flags raised against a member survive that member's erasure

`packages/schema/src/hub.ts:210`

`hub_flags.targetId` is polymorphic and deliberately carries no FK, so a flag whose `targetType = 'member'` and whose `targetId` is a user's id is not reached by the `DELETE FROM users` cascade that `deleteUser` relies on. The free-text `reason` written about that person persists indefinitely after they delete their account.

**Failure:** A hub steward flags Carol with reason "repeatedly no-shows and left the laser cutter damaged". Carol deletes her account. `deleteUser` cascades away the flags Carol raised (`flagged_by_id`) but the row about her stays in `hub_flags` with her (now dangling) uuid, an open status and the accusation text, and it keeps rendering in the hub owner's review queue. Her Art. 17 request has not erased data the instance holds about her, and the same row is excluded from her Art. 15 export.

**Suggested fix:** In `deleteUser`, add an explicit `DELETE FROM hub_flags WHERE target_type = 'member' AND target_id = $userId` inside the transaction (and the same treatment for `reports.targetId` where `target_type = 'user'`), since neither can be expressed as an FK.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. packages/schema/src/hub.ts:210-229 and the DDL in packages/schema/migrations/0039_striped_loa.sql:18-20 show hub_flags has FKs only on hub_id (cascade), flagged_by_id (cascade) and resolved_by_id (set null); target_id has none and there is no trigger in any migration. packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:612-704 ends with `await tx.delete(users).where(eq(users.id, userId))` and never touches hubFlags; both callers (layers/base/server/api/auth/delete-user.post.ts:57 self-serve, layers/base/server/api/admin/users/[id].delete.ts:13) only add file-byte purging. A repo-wide grep finds hubFlags in just five files and there is no `delete(hubFlags)` anywhere — no cleanup on erasure or on member kick. packages/server/src/hub/flags.ts:96-145 does not drop unresolvable targets: the memberLabel lookup misses and falls back to 'Unknown', so the row, its free-text reason and its open status keep rendering in the owner's queue. Guards ruled out: the three routes require the hubGovernance flag, which defaults false, but `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns "hubGovernance":true, so the path is live in production (commonpub.io is false). No validator or test pins the correct behaviour. Two corrections that do not save the finding: (1) the Art. 15 export omission is deliberate and pinned by packages/server/src/__tests__/consent-export.integration.test.ts:193-194,255, which asserts a third party's flag must NOT appear in the subject's export — the Art. 17 retention gap is the real defect; (2) it is a class, not a one-off: packages/schema/src/social.ts:143-160 gives reports.targetId the same FK-less polymorphic shape with the same missing cleanup, and reports is gated by no flag at all, so a fix must sweep both tables. Severity P2 holds: reachable on a live instance, but flag volume is tiny and the surviving row is partially de-identified (dangling uuid, 'Unknown' label), leaving the free-text reason as the residual personal data.

</details>

#### [P2] DSAR export omits several tables holding the subject's own data

`packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:95`

`buildUserDataExport` covers 38 tables but silently omits at least eight that carry rows keyed by the subject's `user_id`, so the downloadable Art. 15 copy is incomplete for anyone who has used those features.

**Failure:** A member requests their data via /settings/account → Download my data. The JSON contains their enrollments but not their per-lesson `lesson_progress` completions; not `hub_bans` (the fact they were banned from a hub, by whom and why — data about them the instance acts on); not `hub_post_likes` / `hub_post_votes` / `poll_votes` (their recorded opinions — `likes` covers content only); not `federated_accounts` (the remote ActivityPub identity linked to their account); not `user_federated_hub_follows`; not `contest_reminder_sends` (which mails were sent to them); not `message_reads`; not `user_roles`. They cannot verify or dispute records the instance holds.

**Suggested fix:** Add the missing sections, and add a guard test that reflects over the Drizzle schema for every table with a `user_id`/author/owner column referencing `users` and fails unless each is either exported or on an explicit, commented exclusion list (the file already documents an exclusion list for keys/sessions/audit logs — make it machine-checked).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/server/src/profile/export.ts (function is exportUserData, not buildUserDataExport) selects from exactly the 38 tables listed; none of lessonProgress, hubBans, hubPostLikes, hubPostVotes, pollVotes, federatedAccounts, userFederatedHubFollows, contestReminderSends, messageReads or userRoles appear. Each of those exists with a user_id FK to users.id (learning.ts:100, hub.ts:149/134, voting.ts:10/40, auth.ts:109, federation.ts:425, contest.ts:789, social.ts:189, rbac.ts:61) and each is written by production code (contestReminderSends via raw SQL at packages/server/src/contest/reminders.ts:198). No guard blocks the scenario: layers/base/server/api/auth/export-data.get.ts is requireAuth-only and explicitly NOT feature-flagged, linked from settings/account.vue:92 and settings/privacy.vue:952 whose copy promises 'a copy of all your data'. The only parity test (personaExport.integration.test.ts:195) walks schema/src/persona.ts alone and pins nothing outside persona tables. The deliberate-exclusion note at export.ts:47-56 covers only keypairs, sessions/accounts, audit_logs, email_outbox/broadcasts and build artifacts, so none of the eight is a documented exclusion; the docstring's 'bounded scope' disclaimer acknowledges incompleteness rather than justifying it, and the module's own projection pattern (reports, hubFlags, referralAttributions) would handle the third-party columns in hub_bans. Weakest items are federatedAccounts (holds an encrypted access token, adjacent to the excluded credential tables, though its identity fields are subject data) and userRoles (administrative), but lesson_progress, hub_post_likes/votes, poll_votes, message_reads and contest_reminder_sends are unambiguously the subject's own rows. P2 stands: user-reachable Art. 15 completeness gap, withholding rather than leaking.

</details>

#### [P2] The `cpub_ref` referral-attribution cookie has no BUILTIN_COOKIES disclosure and no consent gate

`layers/base/server/utils/referralCookie.ts:9`

`GET /r/:code` drops a `cpub_ref` cookie carrying a referral code plus the click timestamp, for up to `attributionWindowDays`, on an anonymous visitor with no consent check — and it is absent from `BUILTIN_COOKIES`, from the analytics-derived list and from anything an operator declares, so it never appears on the /cookies page and never enters the consent scope digest.

**Failure:** An instance with `features.referralLinks` on publishes a /r/spring-drive link. A visitor from the EU clicks it; before any banner interaction the server sets `cpub_ref=spring-drive.1755000000000` with a 30-day `maxAge`. The visitor opens the cookie policy page and `cpub_ref` is not listed; if they choose "Essential only" the cookie is still set and still used at signup to write a `referral_attributions` row linking their new account to that campaign. Three further app cookies are also undisclosed: `cpub-admin-sidebar-collapsed`, `cpub-editor-palette-hidden`, `cpub-editor-inspector-hidden`.

**Suggested fix:** Add a `cpub_ref` entry to `BUILTIN_COOKIES` (category `functional` or `marketing`, which puts it in the scope digest and behind consent) and gate the `setCookie` in `/r/:code` on the visitor's consent level, falling back to the existing `config.referral.cookieless` path; add the three UI-preference cookies as `essential` entries. Back it with a scanning test that greps for `useCookie(`/`setCookie(` names and asserts each is disclosed.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by code reading and live verification. (1) layers/base/server/routes/r/[code].get.ts:25-31 sets `cpub_ref` via setCookie with maxAge = attributionWindowDays*86400; the only guards are requireFeature('referralLinks') and `if (!config.referral?.cookieless)` (cookieless defaults false). No consent read exists on that path, nor in layers/base/server/middleware/referral-claim.ts which later consumes the cookie and calls claimReferral. (2) layers/base/composables/useCookieConsent.ts:9-58 BUILTIN_COOKIES contains exactly the five claimed names; the registry is BUILTIN + analyticsCookieDefs + runtimeConfig.public.instanceCookies (defaulted to [] at layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:163). pages/cookies.vue:13-15 renders only from that registry. A repo-wide grep for cpub_ref hits only server code and docs/plans/referral-links.md — zero registry or config entries; privacy.vue never mentions referrals; no completeness scanner test exists. (3) Reachability is not theoretical: `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns "referralLinks":true, and `curl -D- https://deveco.io/r/<bogus>` returns 302 to /auth/register rather than 404, proving requireFeature passes on a live production instance. Fetching https://deveco.io/cookies shows the essential table rendering exactly better-auth.session_token, cpub-consent, cpub-color-scheme, cpub-verify-dismissed, cpub-persona-invite-dismissed plus GA rows; grep -c cpub_ref on the served HTML is 0. (4) Not deliberate: git log shows referralCookie.ts landed once in #68 (session 229), and the later consent-scoping rework (#76) and persona entry (#79) both edited useCookieConsent.ts without adding it. docs/plans/referral-links.md:109 itself classifies the cookie as "functional", and in this codebase allowsFunctional === (effectiveLevel === 'all'), i.e. the project's own taxonomy makes it consent-requiring; line 246 only claims the cookieless mode sidesteps the gate. (5) The three extra names are real: useAdminSidebar.ts:38 and useEditorChrome.ts:22-23 declare useCookie entries absent from the registry. Partial defence that does not refute: cpub_ref is first-party, httpOnly, carries a public campaign code plus a timestamp, no identifier and no third-party processor, so it has a genuine ePrivacy Art 5(3) "explicitly requested service" argument — but the file makes that same argument for cpub-color-scheme and cpub-verify-dismissed and still DECLARES them as essential so they render on /cookies. The disclosure gap therefore stands under the project's own convention; only the consent-gate half of the claim is arguable. Severity P2 upheld: live on a production instance with an EU-facing consent framework, but transparency/Art 13 rather than covert tracking (no PII, no third party, and the attribution is surfaced at signup by the "Invited by X" banner), so not P1; and not P3 since the path is enabled in production and the policy page presents its list as complete.

</details>

### Authentication, sessions, and authorization

#### [P1] Federated OAuth callback links an attacker's remote actor to the victim's account (CSRF → account takeover)

`layers/base/server/api/auth/federated/callback.get.ts:68`

`GET /api/auth/federated/callback` performs a state-changing account LINK for whoever's session cookie happens to accompany the request, with no CSRF binding between the OAuth `state` token and the browser that started the flow.

**Failure:** Attacker holds an account on any instance in `auth.trustedInstances` (or on a domain an operator later adds via `/api/admin/federation/trusted-instances`). (1) Attacker calls `POST /api/auth/federated/login {instanceDomain: <trusted>}` on the victim instance and receives `authorizationUrl` + `state`. (2) Attacker completes the remote authorize with their OWN remote account and captures the redirect `…/api/auth/federated/callback?code=X&state=Y` WITHOUT following it (the state is single-use and must survive). (3) Attacker sends that URL to a logged-in victim on the victim instance. A top-level GET navigation is exactly what `SameSite=Lax` still sends cookies for, and `csrf.ts` skips `/api/auth/` entirely and skips GET entirely, so the victim's session cookie arrives. (4) `findUserByFederatedAccount(attackerActorUri)` is null, `event.context.auth.user` is the victim, so line 69 runs `linkFederatedAccount(db, victimId, attackerActorUri, …)` — which inserts, because `oauth.ts:257` only rejects when the actorUri is already bound to a DIFFERENT user. (5) Attacker now runs the federated login flow again for themselves; this time `existingLink` resolves to the VICTIM's userId and line 58 mints `createFederatedSession(db, victimUserId)` and sets a valid Better Auth cookie. Full account takeover of any user, including an admin, from one clicked link. No consent screen is shown to the victim at any point.

**Suggested fix:** Bind the OAuth state to the initiating browser: have `POST /api/auth/federated/login` set an httpOnly `SameSite=Lax` nonce cookie and store its hash in the state row, then require an exact match in the callback. Additionally, when `auth.user` is present, do not link implicitly — redirect to a `/settings/account/confirm-link?token=…` page that requires an explicit same-origin POST (which csrf.ts would then cover).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full call graph. callback.get.ts:66-95 links whatever actorUri the consumed state produced to whatever session cookie accompanied the GET, with no binding between the two. packages/server/src/federation/oauth.ts:496-525 shows OAuthLoginState carries only {tokenEndpoint, clientId, clientSecret, redirectUri, instanceDomain, expiresAt} — no userId, no nonce — and consumeOAuthState (530-550) checks only existence and expiry. middleware/csrf.ts returns at line 47 for any non-POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE method and again at :55 for /api/auth/, so a top-level SameSite=Lax GET navigation is doubly exempt. middleware/auth.ts explicitly lists /api/auth/federated/ as isCustomAuthRoute, so it bypasses Better Auth's own CSRF yet still runs resolveSession + enrichUser, meaning event.context.auth.user IS the victim on that GET. linkFederatedAccount (oauth.ts:238-286) matches WHERE actorUri = $1 and throws only when that row belongs to a different user, so the INSERT succeeds for a victim who has no row for the attacker's actor. The takeover leg is real: findUserByFederatedAccount (oauth.ts:362-377) matches on actorUri alone and callback:49-63 then mints createFederatedSession(db, existingLink.userId) and sets a valid Better Auth cookie; createFederatedSession (oauth.ts:633-658) only checks users.status === 'active'. Reachability holds: apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts sets features.federation: true and auth.trustedInstances: ['deveco.io'] (apps/shell also federation: true), login.post.ts gates only on isDomainTrusted, and the full remote OAuth server exists at api/auth/oauth2/{register,authorize,token}. No other middleware intercepts — require-terms.ts and csrf.ts both bail on safe methods, security.ts only rate-limits. Refutation attempts failed: no test pins this, git log on the file shows the only security commit (7242f24d) added the suspend-throw catch, and docs/reviews/full-review-2026-07-17.md:94 logs a DIFFERENT defect on this file (actorUri host not validated against oauthState.instanceDomain) with no mention of state/browser binding. The 10-minute code+state TTL is not a barrier since the attacker can mint a fresh code/state server-side and 302 the victim in one visit. Severity P1 is right: one-click full account takeover including admins, on a flag that is on in the shipped configs, requiring only an ordinary account on a trusted peer (deveco.io, open signup) rather than a compromised instance — strictly easier than the P2 already logged on this same file. Fix: persist the initiating userId (or a browser-bound nonce cookie) in storeOAuthState and require it to match event.context.auth.user.id before linkFederatedAccount, plus an explicit confirmation step for the link outcome.

</details>

#### [P2] Admin user delete and suspend have no last-admin guard, unlike every sibling path

`packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:612`

`deleteUser` and `updateUserStatus` accept any target with no check that the target is an admin or that another admin would remain — while `updateUserRole` (same file, line 346-355) and `POST /api/auth/delete-user` (layer, lines 12-25) both explicitly refuse to strip the last admin.

**Failure:** An operator on deveco.io opens the admin users panel and deletes (or sets status='suspended' on) the only admin account — their own colleague's, or their own via the panel. `DELETE /api/admin/users/{id}` calls `deleteUser(db, id, admin.id)`, which only checks the row exists, then cascades. `PUT /api/admin/users/{id}/status` calls `updateUserStatus`, which additionally wipes every session for that user and — via `enrichUser` in middleware/auth.ts, which nulls `auth.user` whenever `row.status !== 'active'` — makes the account unable to authenticate at all. The instance is left with zero usable admins: `requirePermission(event,'admin.access')` and the admin floor both key off `users.role === 'admin'`, so no route can restore it and recovery requires direct DB access. The same two calls are also the reachable end of a `users.manage` / `users.delete` custom role (RBAC on), letting a non-admin staffer lock out every admin — the very backdoor `role.put.ts:15` was written to close for promotion.

**Suggested fix:** Hoist the LAST_ADMIN check out of `updateUserRole` into a shared `assertNotLastAdmin(tx, userId)` and call it from `deleteUser` and from `updateUserStatus` whenever `newStatus !== 'active'`. Also require the admin floor (`requirePermission(event,'admin.access')`) when the TARGET is an admin, mirroring the promotion guard at role.put.ts:15.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts: deleteUser (612+) selects only {id, username}, throws only 'User not found', then transactions to tx.delete(users) — no role or admin-count check. updateUserStatus (388+) selects only {status}, writes status/deletedAt and deletes all sessions for suspend/delete — no role or count check. The same file's updateUserRole (346-355) implements exactly that guard with a FOR UPDATE lock and a documented INV-4 rationale, and layers/base/server/api/auth/delete-user.post.ts:11-25 repeats it for self-delete, so the invariant is acknowledged and simply missing on these two paths. No upstream guard exists: [id].delete.ts is requireFeature('admin') + requirePermission('users.delete') then straight to deleteUser; status.put.ts is the same with 'users.manage'; the sibling role.put.ts:15 by contrast adds the extra admin.access check and a LAST_ADMIN catch. adminUpdateStatusSchema validates the enum only. layers/base/pages/admin/users.vue:186-203 renders the status toggle and trash button on every row with no v-if excluding admins or self. The lockout is confirmed by layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:151-155, which nulls auth.user/auth.session whenever row.status !== 'active'; requirePermission's admin floor reads user.role off that nulled user, so no route can restore it. Tests pin the guard for updateUserRole only (admin.integration.test.ts:228-231); the updateUserStatus block (298-341) pins soft-delete/reactivate semantics and says nothing about roles. Git history (adcc2e84, 48f593d1) shows no deliberate decision. Severity stays P2: the reachable path needs an existing admin, giving accidental self-lockout with DB-only recovery; the non-admin-staffer escalation via a users.manage custom role is real code but sits behind the rbac flag, which packages/config/src/schema.ts:136 defaults to false and neither apps/reference nor apps/shell enables — so it is not P1. The admin flag itself is true in both app configs, so the admin-facing half is live.

</details>

#### [P2] CSRF middleware exempts /api/auth/* wholesale, but six custom route families under it are NOT handled by Better Auth

`layers/base/server/middleware/csrf.ts:55`

`csrf.ts` skips every `/api/auth/` path on the documented premise that "Better Auth enforces its own CSRF via trustedOrigins" — but `middleware/auth.ts:200-206` deliberately routes six path families AWAY from Better Auth to plain Nitro handlers, so those routes get neither CSRF check.

**Failure:** `POST /api/auth/delete-user` is cookie-authenticated (`requireAuth` at line 7) and irreversibly deletes the caller's account and cascades all their content, yet no origin check runs on it: csrf.ts:55 returns early, and `isBetterAuthRoute` is false for it so `handleAuthRoute`/trustedOrigins never sees it. Any page on a sibling host of the instance domain (SameSite=Lax treats `evil.deveco.io` and `deveco.io` as same-site, so the cookie IS sent) can issue `fetch('https://deveco.io/api/auth/delete-user', {method:'POST', credentials:'include', mode:'no-cors'})` and destroy a logged-in visitor's account with no readable response needed. The same exemption covers `/api/auth/oauth2/authorize` (POST, mints an authorization code), `/api/auth/federated/link`, `/api/auth/sign-in-username`, and both OAuth callbacks. The existing test only pins the Better-Auth-owned `/api/auth/sign-in` case.

**Suggested fix:** Derive the exemption from the SAME predicate auth.ts uses rather than restating it: export `isCustomAuthRoute` from a shared util and change csrf.ts to `if (pathname.startsWith('/api/auth/') && !isCustomAuthRoute(pathname)) return;`. Add a test that walks the actual route files under `server/api/auth/` and asserts every non-Better-Auth one is CSRF-guarded (a guard that counts the files it walked).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the full call graph; no upstream or downstream guard exists. What I verified: 1. `layers/base/server/middleware/csrf.ts:55` — `if (pathname.startsWith('/api/auth/') || pathname.startsWith('/api/public/')) return;` is an unconditional early return, before the session-cookie check and before any origin comparison. Its stated justification is at lines 22-24: "`/api/auth/*` — Better Auth enforces its own CSRF via trustedOrigins." 2. `layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:200-206` — `isCustomAuthRoute` excludes `/api/auth/federated/*`, `/api/auth/oauth2/*`, `/api/auth/mastodon/*`, `/api/auth/sign-in-username`, `/api/auth/delete-user`, `/api/auth/export-data` from `isBetterAuthRoute`, so `middleware.handleAuthRoute` (line 227) is never called for them. The premise in the csrf.ts comment is therefore factually false for those paths. 3. `packages/auth/src/hooks.ts:36-41` — `handleAuthRoute` is the ONLY path into `auth.handler(request)`. `resolveSession` (lines 44-59) calls `auth.api.getSession({headers})` and performs no origin/trustedOrigins check. So the six families get neither Better Auth's CSRF nor csrf.ts's. 4. Those routes exist as real Nitro handlers: `layers/base/server/api/auth/delete-user.post.ts`, `sign-in-username.post.ts`, `oauth2/{register,token,authorize}.post.ts`, `federated/{login,link}.post.ts`. 5. `delete-user.post.ts` is `requireAuth(event)` cookie-auth, reads NO body (so no content-type barrier to a simple cross-origin POST), requires no password re-entry and no confirmation token, and calls `deleteUser(db, user.id, user.id)` which cascades all related data plus federated Delete activities and private-file purge. Irreversible. 6. No other CSRF layer exists: grepping `csrf` across `layers/base/server`, `packages/auth/src`, `packages/server/src` and `nuxt.config.ts` returns only csrf.ts, its test, the mastodon OAuth `state` token, and comments. `security.ts` does rate limiting and headers only. 7. The test file pins the wrong thing: `csrf.test.ts:102-106` case (e) asserts the exemption using `/api/auth/sign-in` only — a genuinely Better-Auth-owned path — so it gives false confidence that the exemption is safe. Corrections to the claim (which is why this is P2, not P1): - The session cookie is explicitly `sameSite: "lax"` (verified in better-auth `dist/cookies/index.mjs:33`, `createCookieGetter`), with `crossSubDomainCookies` NOT enabled (`packages/auth/src/createAuth.ts:125` sets only `advanced.database.generateId`). So a POST from an unrelated attacker origin does NOT carry the cookie. The claim's own scenario is correct that a sibling subdomain (`evil.deveco.io` → `deveco.io`) is same-site under Lax and DOES send the cookie, but that requires attacker control of a subdomain, subdomain takeover, or HTML injection on a sibling host. This is a defense-in-depth gap, not a drive-by from any web page. - `/api/auth/export-data` and both OAuth callbacks are GET, and csrf.ts:47 already returns on safe methods, so those were never in scope — that part of the claim is wrong. - `federated/link.post.ts` requires the victim's password plus a single-use server-side `linkToken`, so it is not meaningfully CSRF-able either. - The genuinely unprotected unsafe-method surface is: `delete-user` (the real impact), `sign-in-username` (login CSRF), and the four oauth2/federated POSTs. P2 is right: not P1 because SameSite=Lax blocks the arbitrary-origin case; not P3 because every other custom `/api/*` route is protected under that same SameSite baseline (so the middleware exists precisely for this threat model), the exempted route is irreversible account destruction with no re-auth, and the file's own documented rationale is untrue.

</details>

#### [P2] Mastodon sign-in callback allows the same link-CSRF takeover from ANY remote host, with no trust list

`layers/base/server/api/auth/mastodon/callback.get.ts:131`

Identical implicit-link-on-GET pattern as the federated callback, but `/api/auth/mastodon/start` accepts an arbitrary `host` with only a syntactic `isValidHost` check, so the attacker does not need an account on a trusted instance.

**Failure:** Attacker creates an account on mastodon.social. They open `GET /api/auth/mastodon/start?host=mastodon.social`, authorize as themselves, and capture the resulting `…/api/auth/mastodon/callback?code=X&state=Y` without following it. They send that URL to a logged-in victim. Top-level GET → SameSite=Lax sends the cookie → csrf.ts exempts `/api/auth/` and GET → line 132 runs `linkFederatedAccount(db, victimId, 'https://mastodon.social/users/attacker', …)`. The attacker then repeats `/start?host=mastodon.social`; outcome 1 fires (`existingLink` = victim), `createFederatedSession(db, victimUserId)` mints a session and line 109 sets the cookie. Any Mastodon account on any server becomes a takeover primitive against any local account.

**Suggested fix:** Same fix as the federated callback (nonce cookie bound to the state, explicit POST confirmation for the link branch). Additionally gate `/api/auth/mastodon/start`'s `host` against the trusted-instance list, or at minimum require the link branch to be initiated from an authenticated same-origin POST on the settings page.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every link in the chain. (1) start.get.ts:37 validates only isValidHost(), which at packages/server/src/federation/mastodonLogin.ts:101-114 is a syntactic DNS-label regex plus an isPrivateUrl SSRF check — no trust list, unlike federated/login.post.ts:22 which does call isDomainTrusted and 403s. (2) storeMastodonLoginState (mastodonLogin.ts:196-206) persists only {host, redirectUri, returnTo, expiresAt} — no cookie nonce, no PKCE verifier, no userId — so a state minted in the attacker's browser is fully redeemable in the victim's. (3) middleware/csrf.ts:33,47 guards only POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE and returns immediately for GET, and line 55 additionally exempts /api/auth/*; there is no Sec-Fetch-Site guard anywhere in layers/base/server/middleware/. (4) event.context.auth IS populated here: middleware/auth.ts:198 lists /api/auth/mastodon/ in isCustomAuthRoute, so isBetterAuthRoute is false and execution falls through to resolveSession at :246; the session cookie is sameSite:'lax' (utils/betterAuthCookie.ts:25), so a top-level GET navigation carries it. (5) linkFederatedAccount (packages/server/src/federation/oauth.ts:255-259) rejects only when the actorUri is already bound to a DIFFERENT user; a fresh attacker actor is unbound, so the INSERT binds it to the victim's id, and a repeat /start then hits outcome 1 (callback.get.ts:100-109) and mints a session on the victim's account. Prior audits touched adjacent lines in this same handler (session 242's returnTo open-redirect fix; commit 7242f24d's suspend catch) without ever binding the state, so this is an oversight rather than a deliberate accepted risk. Severity corrected P1 -> P2: signInWithRemote defaults false in packages/config/src/schema.ts:16 and layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:155, both routes 404 when off, and I verified empirically via curl that /api/features on deveco.io AND commonpub.io both report identity.signInWithRemote:false. It is also not admin-togglable — identity is a nested object and the admin features route filters to typeof value === 'boolean', pinned by layers/base/server/api/admin/__tests__/features-override-cap.test.ts — so enabling requires an operator config/env change plus CPUB_FED_TOKEN_KEY. The 10-minute state TTL and single-use OAuth code add further friction. A genuine account-takeover primitive that must be fixed before the flag is ever enabled, but exploitable on zero instances today.

</details>

#### [P3] Contest vote and withdraw routes skip canViewContest, so a private contest's entries are reachable by entry id

`layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId]/vote.post.ts:13`

Every other per-entry contest route gates on `canViewContest` before touching the entry; the three vote/withdraw routes gate only on `requireAuth` and pass `entryId` straight to the server layer, which never consults contest visibility or the `:slug` in the path.

**Failure:** A contest has `visibility: 'private'` with `visibleToRoles: ['staff']`, `communityVotingEnabled: true`, and status `active`. A plain member who is not the owner, not a stakeholder, not a judge, and not in `visibleToRoles` is 404'd by `GET /api/contests/:slug` and by `/entries`. But `POST /api/contests/anything/entries/<entryId>/vote` succeeds: `voteOnContestEntry` (packages/server/src/voting/voting.ts:208-250) checks only that the entry exists, that the voter is not the entrant, that `communityVotingEnabled` is true, and that status is active/judging. The unauthorised member casts votes that count toward a private contest's community tally, and can confirm entry-id existence (200 'voted' vs 400 'Entry not found'). The `:slug` route parameter is never used, so the entry need not even belong to the contest named in the URL.

**Suggested fix:** Give the three routes the same preamble as `index.get.ts`: resolve the contest from `:slug`, `if (!(await canViewContest(db, contest, user))) throw 404`, load the entry, and assert `entry.contestId === contest.id` before acting.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed for vote.post.ts only. I read layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId]/vote.post.ts: it calls requireFeature('contests') + requireAuth, parses only entryId, and passes it to voteOnContestEntry. packages/server/src/voting/voting.ts:208-250 checks entry existence, self-vote, communityVotingEnabled and status in (active, judging) — it never reads contests.visibility/visibleToRoles and never compares entry.contestId to the :slug contest. canViewContest (packages/server/src/contest/read.ts:175-196) is the gate every read route uses; the sibling votes.get.ts and entries/[entryId]/index.get.ts both call it and additionally assert entry.contestId === contest.id. Nothing in layers/base/server/middleware/ supplies the check, and contest.integration.test.ts:930-947 only pins happy-path/duplicate/race, so no test contradicts. Contests are enabled in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts (schema default is false). Two of the three cited routes are REFUTED: entries/[entryId].delete.ts is safe — withdrawContestEntry (packages/server/src/contest/entries.ts:383) returns 'Not the entry owner' unless row.entry.userId === userId; and vote.delete.ts is safe — removeContestEntryVote only deletes the caller's own (entryId, userId) row and returns false otherwise, so there is no cross-user effect and no meaningful oracle. The failure scenario is also overstated: an unauthorized voter needs an entry UUID, and I found no surface that leaks one to a viewer who fails canViewContest — /entries, /entries/:entryId and /votes all gate, and unauthenticated public/v1/contests/[slug].get.ts filters via isPublicContest and returns no entry ids. Realistic exploitation is a formerly-authorized viewer (demoted role, removed stakeholder/judge) reusing cached entry ids, against a contest that is simultaneously private, communityVotingEnabled and active/judging. Genuine missing authz check, narrow reach, no data disclosure — P3 stands.

</details>

#### [P3] Better Auth routes bypass the layer's rate-limit tier and security headers entirely

`layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:225`

`middleware/auth.ts` terminates the request with `sendWebResponse` for every Better-Auth-owned `/api/auth/*` route, so `middleware/security.ts` — which sorts last alphabetically and owns both the IP rate limiter and the security headers — never executes for them. The dedicated 5-req/60s `auth` tier written specifically for `/api/auth/` is unreachable code.

**Failure:** `POST /api/auth/forget-password`, `/api/auth/sign-up/email`, `/api/auth/callback/*` and every other Better Auth path are handled at auth.ts:227-231 and returned immediately. `checkRateLimit(store, ip, pathname, userId)` at security.ts:69 never runs, so `DEFAULT_TIERS.auth = { limit: 5, windowMs: 60_000 }` and its `pathname.startsWith('/api/auth/')` selector (packages/infra/src/security.ts:248,258) are dead for their only intended target — the shared Redis-backed store that survives multi-pod is replaced by Better Auth's own in-memory, per-process limiter (100/10s default, 3/10s only on sign-in/sign-up/change-password prefixes). Responses on those paths also ship without the CSP and security headers `getSecurityHeaders`/`buildCspHeader` apply to everything else. The comment at auth.ts:186-192 already notes this ordering hazard for one endpoint without generalising it.

**Suggested fix:** Move the rate-limit + security-header logic into a middleware that sorts BEFORE `auth.ts` (e.g. `00-security.ts`), or call `checkRateLimit`/`getSecurityHeaders` inline in auth.ts before dispatching to `handleAuthRoute`. Add a test asserting a 429 after 6 rapid `POST /api/auth/forget-password` calls.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED in mechanism, but the framing overstates impact — downgrading P2 to P3. What I verified by reading code: 1. The short-circuit is real. `layers/base/server/middleware/auth.ts:206` computes `isBetterAuthRoute`, and :225-233 calls `middleware.handleAuthRoute(...)` then `return sendWebResponse(event, response)`. `packages/auth/src/hooks.ts:37-42` shows `handleAuthRoute` returns `auth.handler(request)` unconditionally for any path under `/api/auth`, so `response` is always truthy (Better Auth returns its own 404 Response for unknown sub-paths) — the branch ALWAYS terminates. 2. It really does stop the chain. h3 1.15.10 `dist/index.mjs:942` — `sendWebResponse` calls `event.node.res.end()` / `sendStream`. `H3Event.handled` (:1809) is `this._handled || this.node.res.writableEnded || this.node.res.headersSent`. The app loop at :2017-2038 returns as soon as `event.handled` is true. Downstream middleware never runs. 3. Ordering is as claimed. nitropack 2.13.2 `dist/core/index.mjs:958-978` — `scanDir` ends with `.sort((a, b) => a.path.localeCompare(b.path))`, and `scanMiddleware` (:904) preserves that order. `auth.ts` < `csrf.ts` < `security.ts`. `apps/reference/server/middleware/` does not exist, so nothing preempts it. 4. No compensating layer control. `layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:186` is `routeRules: {}` (empty). `packages/auth/src/createAuth.ts:39-150` passes NO `rateLimit` and no `secondaryStorage`. 5. The codebase already knows. `git log -L 208,225` shows the comment added in fb25b1fc (session 253) states verbatim: "everything under /api/auth is dispatched here via sendWebResponse, which ends the response — so the CSRF and rate-limit middleware that run after this one never execute for it." They fixed exactly one endpoint (404'ing `/api/auth/send-verification-email`) and left the class open. This is the documented-hazard-not-fixed case, not a deliberate design. Where the finding is WRONG / overstated (why P3, not P2): a. "The auth tier is unreachable code" is false. `getTierForPath` (packages/infra/src/security.ts:258) also matches `pathname.startsWith('/auth/')`, and `layers/base/app/pages/auth/` contains login.vue, register.vue, forgot-password.vue, reset-password.vue, verify-email.vue — all SSR page loads that DO reach security.ts. So do the custom API routes excluded at auth.ts:200-205 (`/api/auth/sign-in-username`, `/api/auth/federated/*`, `/api/auth/oauth2/*`, `/api/auth/mastodon/*`, `/api/auth/delete-user`, `/api/auth/export-data`). The tier is live; it just misses the Better-Auth-owned subset. b. "Responses ship without the CSP" is factually wrong. security.ts:100 guards CSP with `if (!pathname.startsWith('/api/'))` — CSP is skipped for EVERY API route, auth or not. There is no auth-specific CSP regression. The `getSecurityHeaders` set (nosniff / X-Frame-Options / Referrer-Policy / Permissions-Policy / HSTS) genuinely is missing, but on JSON and 302 responses that is near-zero impact. c. A real compensating limiter exists and is stronger than the finding implies. better-auth 1.6.4 `dist/context/create-context.mjs:168` defaults `enabled: isProduction` (on in prod), and `dist/api/rate-limiter/index.mjs:184-197` `getDefaultSpecialRules()` gives 3-per-10s to `/sign-in*`, `/sign-up*`, `/change-password*`, `/change-email*` and 3-per-60s to `/forget-password*`, `/request-password-reset`, `/send-verification-otp`. So the brute-force-relevant paths the finding names (sign-up/email, forget-password) are capped at 3/10s and 3/60s — comparable to or tighter than the layer's 5/60s. The finding's own "3/10s only on sign-in/sign-up/change-password prefixes" understates it by omitting the 3/60s password-reset rule. Residual real risk (genuine, but defense-in-depth): non-special paths (`/api/auth/reset-password`, `/api/auth/verify-email`, `/api/auth/callback/*`, `/api/auth/get-session`) fall to the 100-per-10s default instead of 5/60s; the store is `memory` (per-process, resets on restart, no Redis) so a multi-pod self-hoster loses the shared cap; and it is disabled entirely outside NODE_ENV=production. Tokens on those paths are cryptographic, so there is no practical brute-force path — this is a hardening gap, not an exploitable authz hole. Note also that the only tests touching this (packages/infra/src/__tests__/security.test.ts:106, packages/server/src/__tests__/security.test.ts:62-63) assert `getTierForPath('/api/auth/...')` as a pure function. They pass while the integration is bypassed — they do not pin the behaviour.

</details>

### ActivityPub Federation

#### [P1] WebFinger actor search lets any host claim any actor URI, poisoning the remote_actors cache

`packages/server/src/federation/federation.ts:306`

`searchRemoteActor` upserts the remote-actor cache keyed on the *self-declared* `actor.id` from the fetched document, never checking that `actor.id`'s host matches the WebFinger domain that was queried, so a hostile host can overwrite the cached inbox/public key/profile of any actor on any other instance.

**Failure:** Attacker controls evil.example. They serve `/.well-known/webfinger?resource=acct:x@evil.example` whose `self` link points at `https://evil.example/fake`, which returns a valid AP actor document with `id: "https://mastodon.social/users/gargron"`, `inbox: "https://evil.example/inbox"`, and their own `publicKeyPem`. Anyone (the route is unauthenticated — `getOptionalUser`, and CSRF passes cookie-less POSTs) hits `POST /api/federation/search {"query":"@x@evil.example"}`. Because `remote_actors.actor_uri` is UNIQUE and the existing row is found by `actor.id`, the legitimate cached row for `https://mastodon.social/users/gargron` is UPDATEd: its `inbox` becomes `https://evil.example/inbox`, its display name/avatar become the attacker's. From then on `resolveTargetInboxes` (delivery.ts:357-362 for DMs, :274-278 for Accept/Reject, :285-291 for Follow) reads that poisoned `inbox`, so a local user's federated DM addressed to gargron is delivered to the attacker's server, and the mirror/timeline UI shows the attacker's name and avatar for that actor.

**Suggested fix:** After resolving via WebFinger, reject when `new URL(actor.id).hostname !== domain` (and likewise reject when the self-link href host differs from the WebFinger domain). Key the cache row on the URI actually fetched, not the document's self-declared `id`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED. packages/server/src/federation/federation.ts:271-311: searchRemoteActor resolves via WebFinger against the user-supplied `domain`, then keys the cache row entirely on the fetched document's self-declared `actor.id` — `select ... where(eq(remoteActors.actorUri, actor.id))` (282) followed by an unconditional `db.update(remoteActors).set(actorData).where(eq(remoteActors.actorUri, actor.id))` (300-303), where actorData (285-296) includes inbox, sharedInbox, publicKeyPem, preferredUsername, displayName, avatarUrl, bannerUrl, lastFetchedAt. No comparison of `new URL(actor.id).hostname` to `domain`; grep for `hostname` in the file hits only line 173, inside the correct resolveRemoteActor which keys on the REQUESTED actorUri. packages/protocol/src/actorResolver.ts:141-177 passes selfLink.href verbatim to resolveActor, which validates only shape (apActorSchema: id is z.string().url()) plus SSRF/private-IP; no host binding anywhere in protocol (grep hostname in actorResolver.ts/webfinger.ts: no matches). Reachable: layers/base/server/api/federation/search.post.ts is requireFeature('federation') + getOptionalUser (unauthenticated), body only z.string().min(3).max(256). federation is true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:31 and apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts:20 (schema default false at packages/config/src/schema.ts:87, but shipped configs enable it; federation live since session 188). Downstream confirmed: delivery.ts resolveTargetInboxes reads remoteActors.inbox by actorUri with NO re-resolution on the DM path and on Accept/Reject; only Follow/Offer falls back to resolveRemoteActor, and only when the row is ABSENT. resolveRemoteActor itself serves the poisoned row from cache for 24h (age < 24h short-circuit, lines 138-160), and the attacker resets lastFetchedAt on each re-poison. Compounding: messaging.ts:34-53 looks up by (preferredUsername, instanceDomain); the UPDATE does not touch instanceDomain, so the victim row keeps instanceDomain=mastodon.social while inbox becomes the attacker's, so @gargron@mastodon.social resolves from cache straight to the attacker's inbox. Refutations attempted and rejected: (a) signature forgery is NOT enabled — layers/base/server/utils/inbox.ts:106 does a LIVE resolveActor and verifies against that fetch's publicKeyPem, not the cache, so the poisoned key is inert for inbound auth (this is why it is P1, not P0); (b) no test pins correct behaviour — packages/server/src/__tests__/remote-actor.integration.test.ts:127-160 covers only handle parsing and its own comment says full tests need HTTP mocking; (c) not deliberate — git log on the file shows SSRF/safeFetch/deadline hardening but nothing on actor-id host binding, while messaging.ts:64-78 uses onConflictDoNothing() (cannot overwrite) and packages/server/src/__tests__/oauth-actor-host-binding.test.ts:35 explicitly tests rejecting an actorUri on a different host as a cross-instance takeover, showing the codebase treats host binding as a requirement and simply omitted it on this path. Severity P1 stands: unauthenticated, flag on in shipped configs, yields federated DM misdelivery to an attacker-controlled inbox plus display-name/avatar spoofing of any remote actor, but no HTTP-signature forgery.

</details>

#### [P1] Repeat Follow to a hub silently self-approves the follower, bypassing joinPolicy and hub privacy

`packages/server/src/federation/hubFederation.ts:164`

`handleHubFollow` gates the *initial* follower status on `hub.privacy === 'public' && hub.joinPolicy === 'open'`, but the branch taken when a follower row already exists sets `status: 'accepted'` unconditionally, so a second Follow flips a pending/unapproved remote follower to accepted on any hub, including private and approval-gated ones.

**Failure:** Hub `makers` on an instance with `features.federateHubs: true` (set in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:32) has `joinPolicy: 'approval'` (or `privacy: 'private'`). Remote actor evil@evil.example POSTs a signed `Follow` to `/hubs/makers/inbox` → row inserted with `status: 'pending'` (line 171, correct). They POST a second, identical `Follow` with a *different* `activity.id` (so `recordActivitySeen` dedup at hubs/[slug]/inbox.ts:31 does not fire) → `existing.length > 0` → line 164 sets `status: 'accepted'`. They are now an accepted hub follower with no admin approval. They then POST `Create{Note}` to the same inbox; inboxHandlers.ts:650-656 checks only `hubFollowers.status === 'accepted'`, so their post is inserted into `hub_posts` with `authorId: null` (inboxHandlers.ts:682-689) and, for non-private hubs, relayed out as an Announce. For non-private hubs they also start receiving every hub Announce, since delivery.ts:394-397 fans out to `hubFollowers` where `status = 'accepted'`.

**Suggested fix:** Apply the same `hub.privacy === 'public' && hub.joinPolicy === 'open'` predicate in the update branch, and never downgrade-to-upgrade an existing `pending`/`rejected` row: only refresh `activityUri`/`updatedAt` unless the policy permits auto-accept.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. hubFederation.ts:161-165 sets status:'accepted' unconditionally in the existing-row branch while the policy predicate (hub.privacy==='public' && hub.joinPolicy==='open') appears only in the insert branch at line 171; git log -L shows this asymmetry unchanged since the original commit 9580d2a7, so it is an oversight, not deliberate. No guard blocks it: layers/base/server/routes/hubs/[slug]/inbox.ts checks only features.federation && features.federateHubs and never hub privacy, so a private hub's inbox is still POST-able at its predictable URL (buildHubGroupActor's private-hub null only affects actor discovery); inboxHandlers.ts:160-162 routes straight to handleHubFollow with no policy check; recordActivitySeen dedups on attacker-controlled body.id only, so a second Follow with a fresh id reaches the existing-row branch (guaranteed by the hub_followers_fed_pair unique constraint on (hubId, followerActorUri)). Downstream, 'accepted' is the sole gate: inboxHandlers.ts:650-656 inserts remote Create{Note} into hub_posts with authorId:null, and delivery.ts:394-397 fans Announces to status='accepted'. Aggravating: I grepped every hubFollowers reference across packages/, layers/, apps/ and NOTHING except handleHubFollow ever writes hubFollowers.status - there is no approval UI or API - so on a non-open hub 'pending' is terminal and this is the only path to 'accepted'. No test pins it: hub-federation.integration.test.ts:108-141 only uses a public+open hub and its 'duplicate Follow (idempotent)' case asserts count, not status. Reachable: federateHubs defaults false in packages/config/src/schema.ts:89 but is true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:32, and the in-file comment at hubFederation.ts:91 states it is ON on commonpub.io + deveco.io; joinPolicy is operator-selectable in layers/base/pages/hubs/create.vue and hubs/[slug]/settings.vue. Broader than reported: privacy 'unlisted' + joinPolicy 'open' is also inserted as pending and then escalated. Severity P1 confirmed - remote, unauthenticated authorization bypass on a flag that is on for live instances - though impact is bounded: it grants federated-follower status (post injection into gated/private hubs, subscription to gated public/unlisted hub Announces), not local membership, and pre-existing private-hub content is still not exfiltrated because federateHubPost/federateHubActor/federateHubUpdate all return early on privacy==='private'.

</details>

#### [P2] Unbounded remote-controlled activity id is written to a text PRIMARY KEY, producing an uncaught 500 on the public inbox

`layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts:28`

`body.id` is passed straight to `recordActivitySeen`, which inserts it into `processed_activities.activity_id`, a `text` PRIMARY KEY; a value longer than Postgres' btree index-entry limit (~2704 bytes) makes the INSERT throw, and the call sits outside the route's try/catch so the error escapes as a 500.

**Failure:** Any party who can produce a valid HTTP signature (i.e. anyone who stands up an actor on a domain they control) POSTs to /inbox a well-formed, correctly-signed activity whose `"id"` is a 4000-character string. Verification and `assertActorMatchesSigner` pass. `recordActivitySeen` runs `INSERT INTO processed_activities (activity_id) VALUES ($1) ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING RETURNING ...` and Postgres raises `index row size ... exceeds btree version 4 maximum 2704 for index "processed_activities_pkey"`. The route body has no try/catch around this call (the try starts at line 37), so Nitro returns HTTP 500 instead of a 4xx. Repeated at the federation rate-limit ceiling (60/min/IP) this is also a cheap way to fill the never-pruned `processed_activities` table with ~2.7KB rows. Same code path in the per-user inbox (line 26) and hub inbox (line 31).

**Suggested fix:** Validate the activity id before use — require it to parse as an http(s) URL and cap its length (e.g. 1024 chars), rejecting with 400 otherwise — and add a retention job that prunes `processed_activities` older than the signature replay window plus margin.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full call path. layers/base/server/routes/inbox.ts:26-32 passes body.id into recordActivitySeen with only a `typeof === 'string' && length > 0` check, and the route's try block starts at line 38, so the call is genuinely unguarded. packages/server/src/federation/activityDedup.ts:28-34 does a bare insert with no length validation and no try/catch. packages/schema/src/federation.ts:84-90 declares `activityId: text('activity_id').primaryKey()` — an unbounded btree PK. Every upstream guard was checked and none bounds the id: verifyInboxRequest (layers/base/server/utils/inbox.ts) caps only the 1MB body, checks signature/keyId-domain/Date/digest; assertActorMatchesSigner compares only hostnames, both attacker-controlled; processInboxActivity (the schema validator) runs at line 39, AFTER the insert. Grep across packages/protocol/src, packages/server/src/federation and the inbox routes found no id-length cap. I reproduced the Postgres failure against the project's own container: `ERROR: index row size 4016 exceeds btree version 4 maximum 2704 for index "zz_probe_pkey"`. One nuance the finding missed: a compressible 4000-char value inserts fine due to index-tuple compression, so the attack requires a high-entropy id — trivially attacker-controlled, so not a refutation. The growth vector is also real: grep for processedActivities across packages/server/src, packages/schema/src and layers/base/server shows only the schema and activityDedup.ts, no pruning job, and packages/infra/src/security.ts:250,269 confirms /inbox maps to the federation tier at 60/min. Same unguarded code exists at layers/base/server/routes/users/[username]/inbox.ts:24-30 and hubs/[slug]/inbox.ts:30. features.federation defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:87) but is live on the deployed instances; no test pins long-id behaviour. Severity P2 sits near the P2/P3 line — the 500-vs-400 alone is low harm (single statement, no transaction or pool leak, Nitro sanitizes the message in prod) — but it is held at P2 by the combination with the never-pruned table, where a signature-authenticated remote can push ~160KB/min/IP of permanent rows with no eviction path.

</details>

#### [P3] Configured maxDeliveryRetries is ignored when dead-lettering, stranding activities as permanently pending

`packages/server/src/federation/delivery.ts:224`

`deliverActivity` calls `incrementAttempts` without forwarding the caller's `maxRetries`, so dead-lettering always uses the hard-coded `DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 6` while the candidate query uses the configured value — a configured value below 6 produces rows that stop being selected but are never marked failed/dead-lettered, and a value above 6 is silently capped.

**Failure:** Operator sets `federation.maxDeliveryRetries: 3` (valid per packages/config/src/schema.ts:270, which allows 1..20). An activity to an unreachable inbox fails: attempts 1,2,3,4 (the query filter is `lte(attempts, 3)`, so attempts=3 is still selected once more). At attempts=4 `incrementAttempts`'s own check is `4 >= 6` → false, so `status` stays `'pending'` and `deadLetteredAt` stays NULL. The candidate query now excludes it (`lte(attempts, 3)` fails), so it is never retried, never marked failed, and `cleanupDeliveredActivities` never deletes it (delivery.ts:571-578 requires delivered/failed/dead-lettered). Every subsequent undeliverable activity adds another permanent row to `activities`, and /api/admin/federation/pending reports a monotonically growing pending queue that no worker will ever drain. Conversely with `maxDeliveryRetries: 20`, activities dead-letter at 6 attempts regardless.

**Suggested fix:** Thread `maxRetries` from `deliverPendingActivities` through `deliverActivity` into `incrementAttempts`, and make the candidate filter and the dead-letter check use the same bound (`lt(attempts, maxRetries)`).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the code end to end. 1. `packages/server/src/federation/delivery.ts:62` — `const maxRetries = opts.maxRetries ?? DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS;` and :76 `lte(activities.attempts, maxRetries)` in the candidate query. The candidate filter honours the configured value. 2. :108 is the ONLY call site of `deliverActivity`, and it is `await deliverActivity(db, activity, domain)` — the signature at :120 is `(db, activity, domain)`, with no maxRetries parameter at all, so the value cannot reach the failure branch. 3. :224 `await incrementAttempts(db, activity.id, errors.join('; '))` is the ONLY call site of `incrementAttempts`, and it passes no fourth argument; :532 `async function incrementAttempts(db, activityId, error, maxRetries = DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS)`. Its `maxRetries` parameter has zero callers — a dead parameter. Dead-lettering therefore always uses the hard-coded 6 (`newAttempts >= maxRetries` → `status: 'failed'` + `deadLetteredAt`). The config value is genuinely plumbed, so the mismatch is reachable: `layers/base/server/plugins/federation-delivery.ts:29` reads `fedConfig.maxDeliveryRetries ?? 6` and :45 passes `{ batchSize, maxRetries }` to `deliverPendingActivities`. `packages/config/src/schema.ts:274` validates `z.number().int().positive().max(20).default(6)`, so 1..20 are all valid operator inputs. I looked hard for a rescue path and found none: - No test pins the forwarding. `packages/server/src/__tests__/delivery-retry.test.ts` only exercises the default (it calls `deliverPendingActivities(db, DOMAIN)` with no options) and never sets maxRetries; the other four integration suites pass a bare number, which the :57-59 normalizer turns into `{ batchSize }` only. - `layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/retry.post.ts` only updates rows `WHERE status = 'failed'`. The stranded rows are `status='pending'`, so the manual admin remedy does not touch them either. - `cleanupDeliveredActivities` (:571-578) requires `delivered | failed | deadLetteredAt IS NOT NULL`, so stranded pending rows are never reaped, confirming the unbounded-growth half of the scenario. - No sweeper anywhere else writes `deadLetteredAt` for outbound activities (grep across the repo: only delivery.ts, the schema column, and the unrelated email outbox, which by contrast does its dead-lettering attempt-accurately in `packages/server/src/comms/outbox.ts:187`). - Nothing in git history suggests deliberateness; the default parameter with no caller matches an incomplete plumbing job rather than an intentional cap. The arithmetic in the failure scenario checks out: with maxRetries=3, attempts reach 4 (`lte(attempts, 3)` still selects at attempts=3), `4 >= 6` is false so status stays 'pending' with NULL deadLetteredAt, and the candidate query then excludes it forever. With maxRetries=20 it dead-letters at 6, silently ignoring the config. Severity correction: P2 → P3. The defect is latent on every shipped configuration — the schema default is 6, which exactly matches DEFAULT_MAX_ATTEMPTS, and neither `apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts` nor `apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts` sets a `federation` config block at all (only `features.federation: true`). No instance in this repo can currently hit it; it fires only when an operator sets a documented, validated option to a non-default value. Real, silent, and with no admin remedy once triggered, but not on a path anyone hits today.

</details>

#### [P3] User AP actor document and on-demand keypair minting are served with features.federation off

`layers/base/server/routes/users/[username].ts:15`

Unlike every other federation route, the per-user actor endpoint never checks `config.features.federation`, so an instance with federation disabled still publishes AP Person documents (including bio and public key) and generates RSA keypairs on demand for unauthenticated callers.

**Failure:** Operator ships with `features.federation: false` and expects no ActivityPub surface. An unauthenticated request `GET /users/alice` with `Accept: application/activity+json` still returns a full Person document with `inbox`, `sharedInbox`, `summary` (the user's bio) and `publicKey`, and `getOrCreateActorKeypair` mints and persists a 2048-bit RSA keypair for alice as a side effect of that anonymous GET. Meanwhile `/users/alice/inbox` and `/users/alice/outbox` correctly 404 (both start with `if (!config.features.federation) throw createError({ statusCode: 404 })`), so the published actor advertises endpoints that do not exist — remote instances that discover it will fail every delivery.

**Suggested fix:** Add the same `if (!config.features.federation) throw createError({ statusCode: 404 })` guard at the top of the handler, before the profile lookup and keypair creation.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. layers/base/server/routes/users/[username].ts contains no config.features.federation check (useConfig() is used only for instance.domain), and there is no upstream guard: layers/base/server/middleware/features.ts gates only /learn,/docs,/videos,/admin,/contests,/events,/explainer,/settings/referral-links — /users is absent from ROUTE_FEATURE_MAP. getOrCreateActorKeypair (packages/server/src/federation/federation.ts:39-67) genuinely does generateKeypair() + db.insert(actorKeypairs) on a miss, so an anonymous AP-Accept GET is an unauthenticated write. features.federation defaults to false (packages/config/src/__tests__/config.test.ts:34), so this is the default-install path. Git history shows the file was added in 00790b98 and never modified — the guards on routes/actor.ts, routes/inbox.ts and users/[username]/inbox.ts were added later and this route was missed, not deliberately exempted. No test pins the flag-off behaviour (the only test touching the file, openapi-route-parity.test.ts:102, greps it for persona tokens). Two corrections to the reported evidence, neither fatal: (1) the finding cites routes/actor/outbox.ts, which is the INSTANCE Service outbox; the real user outbox routes/users/[username]/outbox.ts has no federation guard either, nor do users/[username]/followers.ts and following.ts — so only the inbox 404s and the "advertises endpoints that do not exist" reasoning is half wrong, while the actual gap is four unguarded AP surfaces rather than one; (2) the bio/summary disclosure is not incremental, since layers/base/server/api/users/[username].get.ts already returns the full profile including bio to unauthenticated callers regardless of the flag. What survives is a flag-less ActivityPub surface (standing rule 2) plus unauthenticated RSA keypair minting bounded to one keypair per existing user (unknown usernames 404 before the keygen). P3 is correct: no incremental data exposure, bounded resource cost, off any real-user path.

</details>

#### [P3] Federated timeline does not filter by config.instance.contentTypes, contradicting the documented content-type protection

`packages/server/src/federation/timeline.ts:77`

CLAUDE.md states "federated feeds filter by `config.instance.contentTypes` so unsupported types don't leak into an instance's UI", but `listFederatedTimeline` only applies a caller-supplied `cpubType` filter and neither the route nor the mirror page consults the instance's enabled content types.

**Failure:** An instance is configured with `instance.contentTypes: ['project']` (explainers deliberately disabled — the local surfaces honour it via layers/base/server/utils/contentQuery.ts:37 and useContentTypes). A mirrored CommonPub peer sends `Create{ Article, 'cpub:type': 'explainer' }`. `onCreate` stores it with `cpubType='explainer'` (inboxHandlers.ts:766-767, no allow-list check), `GET /api/federation/timeline` returns it (no contentTypes predicate), and `/mirror/{id}` renders it through `ViewsExplainerView` (layers/base/pages/mirror/[id].vue:102) — a content type the operator switched off is now live in their UI. The same absence means an unrecognised `cpub:type` string of up to 32 chars is persisted verbatim and drives the render branch.

**Suggested fix:** Add an `inArray(federatedContent.cpubType, config.instance.contentTypes)` (plus a null/unknown fallback bucket) predicate in the timeline route and a 404 in /api/federation/content/[id] when the item's cpubType is not enabled — or amend CLAUDE.md to stop claiming the protection exists.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the code end to end. (1) packages/server/src/federation/timeline.ts:74-78 — `listFederatedTimeline` builds `conditions` from only `deletedAt IS NULL`, `isHidden = false`, and the caller-supplied `apType`/`cpubType`/`originDomain`; the options interface (lines 51-60) has no allow-list parameter at all. `getFederatedContent` (same file) filters on id/deletedAt/isHidden only. (2) layers/base/server/api/federation/timeline.get.ts, .../federation/content/[id].get.ts and .../search/federated.get.ts each call `requireFeature('federation')` and pass the raw query straight through — no `useConfig()`, no contentTypes predicate. (3) Ingest does not gate either: inboxHandlers.ts:766-767 stores `cpub:type` verbatim (only `article`→`blog` normalization) into `federatedContent.cpubType` (varchar(32), packages/schema/src/federation.ts:118) with no allow-list check. (4) The contentTypes protection genuinely exists only on the LOCAL content path: `allowedContentTypes` is threaded from layers/base/server/utils/contentQuery.ts:37 and public/v1/content/index.get.ts:43 into `queryFederatedAsListItems` (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:160-178). Commit 200238c3 ("content type protection on federated feeds") scoped that fix to /api/content only, so the timeline path was never covered — it is an omission, not a documented exemption. (5) Reachability is real, not hypothetical: the live deveco instance sets `instance.contentTypes: ['project','blog']` with `features.explainers:false` and `federation:true, seamlessFederation:true` (/Users/obsidian/Projects/ossuary-projects/deveco-io/commonpub.config.ts), while the peer heatsynclabs-io enables explainer. Such an item surfaces on layers/base/pages/federation/index.vue (unfiltered fetch, no cpubType param), on the "fediverse" tab of pages/search.vue (both the /api/search/federated call and its /api/federation/timeline fallback at line 104), and renders through `ViewsExplainerView` at layers/base/pages/mirror/[id].vue:102. Nothing 404s or degrades it. Two caveats that shape the fix but do not refute the finding: the timeline deliberately carries non-CommonPub content with `cpubType = null` (Mastodon Notes, Lemmy Articles — pinned by packages/server/src/__tests__/federation-content-types.integration.test.ts, incl. "no filter returns all content" expecting 6 items), so a naive `cpubType IN (...)` predicate would wrongly drop all Notes; the correct guard filters only rows whose cpubType is non-null and outside the allow-list.
