Severity P3 is right: no privacy or security impact (the content is already public on its origin instance), no data corruption; the harm is a config-fidelity/UX leak of an operator-disabled content type on secondary surfaces (an unlinked /federation page, the fediverse search tab, and the mirror renderer).

</details>

### Accessibility

#### [P1] Icon-only buttons with no accessible name (10 sites, incl. comment cancel-reply and video pagination)

`layers/base/components/CommentSection.vue:154`

Ten `<button>` elements contain only a Font Awesome `<i>` and carry no text, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby` or `title`, so their accessible name is empty.

**Failure:** A screen-reader user replying to a comment hears "Replying to @alice, button" for the cancel control and has no idea what pressing it does. On /videos, the pager announces "button" and "button" either side of "Page 2 of 7" with nothing to distinguish previous from next, so paging is guesswork.

**Suggested fix:** Add `aria-label` to each ("Cancel reply", "Previous page", "Next page", "Close", "Move up", "Move down", "Delete learning path") and `aria-hidden="true"` on the decorative `<i>`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading all ten cited locations. CommentSection.vue:154 is exactly `<button class="cpub-cancel-reply" @click="cancelReply"><i class="fa-solid fa-xmark"></i></button>` — no text node, no aria-label, no aria-labelledby, no title. The same holds at videos/index.vue:177 and :184 (cpub-page-btn wrapping only fa-chevron-left/right), hub/HubProjects.vue:78, editors/MarkdownImportDialog.vue:67, federated-hubs/[id]/posts/[postId].vue:171, hubs/[slug]/posts/[postId].vue:230, admin/navigation.vue:248 and :251, and learn/[slug]/edit.vue:231. Refutation attempts that all failed: (1) No global mitigation — layers/base/plugins/ contains only analytics, auth, global-overlays, seo-brand, theme; nothing injects accessible names. (2) Font Awesome cannot supply the name: it is loaded as plain CDN CSS (nuxt.config.ts:21, FA 6.5.1 all.min.css), so icons are ::before Private Use Area glyphs (U+F00D etc.) — a PUA codepoint is not a usable accessible name and screen readers announce nothing meaningful. (3) Not flag-gated or unreachable: videos/index.vue has no feature-flag guard, and CommentSection is mounted on ProjectView, ArticleView, ExplainerView, videos/[id].vue and u/[username]/[type]/[slug] — core public content pages hit daily. (4) No test pins the behaviour: the only axe suites are components/admin/layouts/__tests__/editor-axe.test.ts and block-view tests; nothing covers CommentSection or the videos page. (5) Not deliberate — the opposite. layers/base has 486 aria-label usages and the identical pattern is labelled everywhere else (MirrorRequestApproveModal.vue:85, ShareToHubModal.vue:50, ContentPicker.vue:70, ProductEditModal.vue:70 all use `class="cpub-modal-close" aria-label="Close"` with the same fa-xmark). Decisively, the SAME file labels its other icon-only buttons: CommentSection.vue:207 and :237 both carry aria-label="Delete comment". git blame puts line 154 in deed366e (session 109 federation work) as an incidental omission, not a design decision. Severity P1 is correct: WCAG 2.1 SC 4.1.2 Name/Role/Value is Level A, below the project's own mandated AA floor (CLAUDE.md standing rule 12). The videos pager is genuinely blocking — a screen-reader user hears "button / Page 2 of 7 / button" with nothing distinguishing previous from next on a public, unflagged page. One minor imprecision in the reported scenario: "Replying to @alice" is a text node in the parent div.cpub-replying-to, not inside the button, so it never contributes to the button's accessible name — it is announced separately in browse mode. This makes the button's name emptier, not less empty, so it strengthens the finding.

</details>

#### [P2] Required state is aria-hidden-only for checkbox/radio/address/file submission fields

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestSubmissionField.vue:132`

For the `radio`, `checkbox`, `address` and `file` branches the only mark of a required field is a `*` span carrying `aria-hidden="true"`; no `required`, no `aria-required`, and (unlike the generic `v-else` branch at line 180) no `cpub-sr-only` "(required)" text, so assistive tech is never told the field is mandatory.

**Failure:** On the live deveco contest a registration/entry template contains a required `radio` ("Which track?") and a required `checkbox`. A screen-reader user tabs the form: line 132 renders `<span id=fieldId>Which track? <span aria-hidden=true>*</span></span>` and the radiogroup at line 134 is labelled only by that span, so the announced name is "Which track?, radio group" with no required state. They skip it, then find the Save button is `disabled` (ContestRegistrationForm.vue:99) and therefore not in the tab order at all — no control to activate, no announced reason, registration cannot be completed.

**Suggested fix:** Bind `:aria-required="field.required || undefined"` on the radiogroup (line 134), the address `role="group"` (line 115), the file input (line 153) and the checkbox input (line 99), and add the same `cpub-sr-only` "(required)" span the generic branch already uses to the four label spans.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading ContestSubmissionField.vue: the radio (132), checkbox (106), address (113) and file (151) branches mark required with only `<span class="cpub-subfield-req" aria-hidden="true">*</span>`, no `required`, no `aria-required`, and no `cpub-sr-only "(required)"` sibling — that sr-only text exists only in the generic `v-else` branch (line 180). Accessible names genuinely drop the state (checkbox label wraps the input with the asterisk aria-hidden; address role=group is aria-labelledby that same span; file uses aria-label=field.label). `grep -rn aria-required` over layers/packages/apps hits only bundled jsdom, zero source hits. Reachable and load-bearing: FormTemplateEditor.vue:387 exposes the required toggle for every field type and isRequiredFormField (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:216) honours `required===true` for these types, so Save is really gated. No test pins it — ContestSubmissionField.test.ts axe-scans all 11 types including a required radio/address and passes, because axe has no rule for missing required state. Git history (a833ee00) shows the sr-only text shipped with the original generic branch; radio/file were added in later phases and simply never carried it, so it is an omission, not a deliberate choice. SEVERITY CORRECTED from P0 to P2: the cited failure scenario is overstated. ContestRegistrationForm computes `missing` eagerly (no submit gate), so each unfilled required field already renders `<p role="alert">This field is required.</p>` in DOM reading order immediately after the control — a screen reader in browse mode does get told. And the other two consumers do not disable submit at all: ContestProposalForm.vue:79 and ContestStageSubmission.vue:122 keep the button enabled and toast the missing field labels. So this is a real WCAG 1.3.1/3.3.2 state-announcement gap on a live form, inconsistent with the sibling branch, but not a blocking dead end.

</details>

#### [P2] Blocking Save button is disabled and unreachable; required-field errors are not associated with their inputs

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestRegistrationForm.vue:99`

Save is `:disabled="!canSave"` (canSave requires `missing.size === 0`), and the per-field "This field is required." paragraph at line 91 is a bare `role="alert"` sibling — it is not referenced by `aria-describedby` and no input gets `aria-invalid`, so nothing about the blocked state reaches a control the user can focus.

**Failure:** A screen-reader user in NVDA forms mode fills every field they perceived, then Shift+Tab looking for Save. A `<button disabled>` is removed from the tab order, so they never reach it. Moving through the fields again announces each label with no error and no invalid state, because the `<p role="alert">` at line 91 is not part of any field's accessible description. The form is unfinishable with no diagnosable cause.

**Suggested fix:** Keep Save enabled and validate on click, announcing a summary into a persistent live region; give the missing-field paragraph an id, pass it into `ContestSubmissionField` so it joins `aria-describedby` alongside `helpId`, and set `aria-invalid="true"` on the offending control.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. ContestRegistrationForm.vue really does gate the only submit control with :disabled="!canSave" (line 128; canSave at line 89 requires missing.size === 0), and the per-field error at line 120 is a bare <p role="alert"> with no id. ContestSubmissionField.vue wires aria-describedby to helpId only; a grep over layers/base/components/ finds aria-invalid/aria-required exclusively in AdminLayoutsAutoForm.vue and PersonaFieldInput.vue, so the erroring control carries no invalid state and no link to its message. I tried three refutations and all failed: (1) blockingFieldKeys (utils/contestSubmission.ts:86) filters by visibleFormFieldKeys, so hidden required fields do not silently block — that closes an escalation but not this finding; (2) the behaviour is pinned by ContestRegistrationForm.test.ts:42 and both test files run axe clean, but axe flags neither a disabled submit nor unassociated error text, so it is not a guard; (3) the sibling ContestProposalForm.vue:79 uses the opposite, correct pattern (submit always enabled, toast listing blockingFields), proving the codebase knows the alternative. The finding does overstate: the <p> is real text in reading order right after each field (readable in NVDA browse mode) and the generic text/select/number branch emits an sr-only "(required)", so the form is not literally undiagnosable. Two details aggravate it instead: the checkbox/radio/address/file branches convey required only via an aria-hidden asterisk (the pinned test template line 22 is exactly such a required radio), and a required file field with contestPrivateFiles at its false default (packages/config/src/schema.ts:52) renders no control while still counting as blocking, permanently disabling Save for every user. Severity corrected to P2: a genuine WCAG 3.3.1/4.1.2-adjacent gap on a live registration path that diverges from the house pattern, but perceivable in browse mode and recoverable, so not P1.

</details>

#### [P2] Docs page tree treeitems are not focusable — pages cannot be selected or reordered by keyboard

`layers/base/components/editors/DocsPageTree.vue:265`

Every `role="treeitem"` is a `<div>` with `@click="emit('select', node.id)"` and no `tabindex`, and the `role="tree"` container has no keydown handler, so the tree implements none of the WAI-ARIA tree keyboard pattern; reordering is `draggable="true"` drag-and-drop only, with no keyboard alternative in the context menu.

**Failure:** A keyboard-only editor opens the docs editor. Tab moves only to the expand chevron (line 290) and the kebab (line 305) — never to the row itself — so pressing Enter/Space can never fire `emit('select')` and no docs page can be opened for editing. Reordering is impossible too: the context menu (lines 340-351) offers only Rename/Duplicate/Add Child/Delete, and `@dragstart/@drop` (lines 283-286) require a pointer.

**Suggested fix:** Give the tree a roving `tabindex` (0 on the selected node, -1 elsewhere), handle Up/Down/Left/Right/Home/End/Enter on the tree container, and add "Move up"/"Move down"/"Outdent"/"Indent" items to the context menu as the keyboard equivalent of the drag.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full file plus its consumer and wrapper. DocsPageTree.vue:265-288 renders each role="treeitem" as a <div> with @click="emit('select', node.id)", no tabindex; the role="tree" container at line 264 has no keydown handler; grep for tabindex/keydown in the file returns only the inline add input's @keydown.enter/.escape at 321-322. The wrapper packages/editor/vue/components/EditorShell.vue has zero keydown/tabindex/role matches, so no upstream component supplies the tree pattern. No guard exists: packages/config/src/schema.ts:29 sets docs default true (not flag-off), and the only related test (layers/base/composables/__tests__/useDocsPageTree.test.ts) covers composable CRUD, not component keyboard behavior. Reordering is drag-only (280-287) and the context menu (340-351) has Rename/Duplicate/Add Child/Delete with no move action, so there is no keyboard reorder path at all. Two corrections to the claim: (1) it overstates the impact — edit.vue:234-247 auto-selects a page on mount (?page= query, else lowest sortOrder), so a keyboard user can edit one page and reach others only by hand-editing the URL; the real defect is that switching pages and reordering have no keyboard path. (2) It understates one point — .cpub-tree-kebab is opacity:0 (line 489) revealed only on .cpub-tree-item:hover (493) with no :focus-visible rule, so the sole focusable per-row control is invisible while focused. Severity lowered to P2: a real WCAG 2.1.1 Level A failure against the project's stated AA bar, but on an auth-gated (middleware: 'auth') admin/editor-only authoring surface with a partial workaround, so it degrades rather than blocks.

</details>

#### [P2] Editor metadata labels are never associated with their inputs (18 controls across 3 editors)

`layers/base/components/editors/ProjectEditor.vue:297`

The whole settings panel of ProjectEditor, ArticleEditor and ExplainerEditor uses `<label class="cpub-ep-flabel">Slug</label>` as a *sibling* of the control, with no `for`, no wrapping, and no `aria-label`/`aria-labelledby` on the input.

**Failure:** An author opens the project editor with a screen reader and moves through the settings sidebar. Every control announces as "edit text, blank" / "combo box" with no name — Slug, Build Time, Estimated Cost, Description, Category, SEO description, Series, Scheduled at. The only hint is the placeholder, which several screen readers do not read as a name and which disappears once anything is typed, so the author cannot tell which box is which.

**Suggested fix:** Add a unique id per control and `:for` on each label (or wrap the control in the label). The same fix applies to `cpub-editor-label` and `cpub-new-msg-label`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the source. `grep -rn 'cpub-ep-flabel' layers/base` returns exactly 18 hits across layers/base/components/editors/{ProjectEditor,ArticleEditor,ExplainerEditor}.vue, and zero of those lines carry `for=`. Every one closes `</label>` on its own line, so none wraps its control either. I checked each control that follows: ProjectEditor.vue:297-322 (slug input, difficulty button group, buildTime, estimatedCost, description textarea), ArticleEditor.vue:444-561 (slug, subtitle, description, category `<select>`, seoDescription, visibility, tags, series, `type="datetime-local"` publish date), ExplainerEditor.vue:333-349 (slug, description, estimatedMinutes, tags). `grep -c 'aria-label'` is 4 per file and none of those 4 land on a `cpub-ep-input`/`cpub-ep-select`/`cpub-ep-textarea` line — they are on the `<aside aria-label="Project settings">` wrappers and chip-remove buttons. So no explicit `for`, no implicit wrapping, no aria-label, no aria-labelledby: the accessible name is empty or, at best, placeholder-derived. No guard exists upstream. The only consumer is layers/base/pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/edit.vue, which resolves EditorsArticleEditor / EditorsExplainerEditor / EditorsProjectEditor at lines 141-144 behind `definePageMeta({ layout: false, middleware: 'auth' })` — an auth check, not a feature flag. Every authenticated author hits this surface on every edit. There is no `__tests__` directory under components/editors and no axe test referencing these components, so nothing pins the current or the correct behaviour. Git history (fb25b1fc, 4d486147, c74cf91c) shows the pattern was carried forward through recent edits, not a deliberate a11y decision. This also violates the repo's own standing rule 12 ("Accessibility-first — WCAG 2.1 AA minimum") and the component standard "ARIA labels on all interactive elements". The sibling patterns the finding cites are real too: pages/admin/navigation.vue:200 `<label class="cpub-editor-label">Label</label>` and pages/messages/index.vue:129 `<label class="cpub-new-msg-label">Recipients</label>`, both bare siblings. Severity corrected to P2, not P1. The failure scenario is overstated in extent: most of the 18 are text inputs/textareas with a non-empty placeholder, which browsers do feed into the accessible-name computation as a fallback, so they announce something (badly — the placeholder, and axe flags this only as the best-practice `label-title-only` class). The controls with literally no accessible name are the narrower set: ArticleEditor's Category `<select>` (line 458), its `datetime-local` Publish Date (line 561, no placeholder possible), ExplainerEditor's bare Difficulty `<select>`, and ProjectEditor's Difficulty button group (class-only `active` state, no `aria-pressed`, no group name). That is a genuine WCAG 4.1.2 Name/Role/Value failure on a daily authoring path with no flag, which is serious — but it degrades rather than blocks (title and body editing are unaffected, visible label text is adjacent and readable in browse mode), and it is an authoring surface, not a public reading surface.

</details>

#### [P2] Contest submit dialog: radiogroup owns a link, and its radios implement no radio keyboard pattern

`layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue:529`

`role="radiogroup"` wraps a `NuxtLink` (line 530) as its first child — an owned element that is not a `radio` — and each `role="radio"` button (line 539) is a separate native tab stop with no roving `tabindex` and no arrow-key handler.

**Failure:** An entrant on deveco opens "Submit entry" with a screen reader. It announces "Select a project to submit, radio group, 1 of N" and reads the "Create a new project" link as an unexpected group member. The user presses Down/Right as the announced role instructs — nothing moves, because there is no keydown handler on the group. Tab then steps through every tile individually, contradicting the single-stop semantics the radiogroup role promised.

**Suggested fix:** Move the "Create a new…" link outside the radiogroup, and give the tiles roving `tabindex` plus an arrow/Home/End keydown handler like `onTabKey` at line 161.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/index.vue. Line 529 is `<div class="cpub-submit-gallery" role="radiogroup" aria-label="Select a project to submit">` whose FIRST child (line 530) is `<NuxtLink :to="createForContestLink" class="cpub-submit-new">` — a link, not a `radio`, so the radiogroup owns a non-permitted child (axe aria-required-children / ARIA 1.2 required owned elements). Lines 535-544 are `<button type="button" role="radio" :aria-checked="submitContentId === item.id" ... @click="submitContentId = item.id">` with NO :tabindex binding and NO @keydown. Grep of the whole SFC shows the only keyboard handler is onTabKey (line 161, wired at line 508 to the tablist), which implements roving tabindex + Arrow/Home/End correctly for the tabs directly above — so the radios are each a native tab stop and arrow keys do nothing, exactly as claimed. No upstream guard removes the failure: openSubmitDialog() (line 300) just sets showSubmitDialog = true, and useFocusTrap (layers/base/composables/useFocusTrap.ts) handles only Escape and Tab; its focusable query enumerates `button:not([disabled])` and `[href]`, which is precisely why every tile and the link are separate Tab stops. No test pins this markup (grep for cpub-submit-gallery / "Select a project to submit" finds only the page; the contest radiogroup tests cover ContestRegistrationForm/ContestSubmissionField, which use native <input type=radio> and get the pattern for free — this page is the outlier). Git history refutes "deliberate": commit bea00495 replaced a working native `<select aria-label="Select a project to submit">` with the button gallery and added ARIA roles without the keyboard pattern; no comment justifies it. Severity P2 stands: features.contests defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:31) so most instances never see it, but it is ON for deveco with a live contest and this dialog is the core entry-submission path there. It is a semantics/keyboard-convention defect, not a hard block — Tab still reaches each tile, Enter/Space still selects, aria-checked updates — so not P1, but a serious axe violation on a real production path, so not P3.

</details>

#### [P2] Interactive slider block removes its focus indicator and never announces its output

`layers/base/components/blocks/BlockSliderView.vue:164`

`.cpub-slider-input` sets `outline: none` in the base rule with no `:focus`/`:focus-visible` replacement anywhere in the file, and the feedback panel that is the whole point of the block (lines 78-85) has no `aria-live`/`role="status"`.

**Failure:** A reader tabs through an explainer page containing a slider block. The scoped selector `.cpub-slider-input[data-v-x]` (0,2,0) beats the global `:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent) }` in packages/ui/theme/base.css:434 (0,1,0) and applies in every state, so the slider takes focus with zero visual change — the user cannot tell which control the arrow keys will move. If they guess and press Right, the value and the `.cpub-slider-output` message change silently; a screen reader announces the new value from the native range but never the "Too high — the heater will trip" feedback text that is the block's actual content.

**Suggested fix:** Replace with `.cpub-slider-input:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 4px; }` (and a thumb treatment), and add `role="status"` to a container that is always present in the DOM so the message change is announced.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code, and the cascade situation is actually worse than the finding claims. 1. Focus indicator. `layers/base/components/blocks/BlockSliderView.vue:157-168` — `.cpub-slider-input { -webkit-appearance: none; appearance: none; ... outline: none; }`. I read the whole `<style scoped>` block (lines 89-236): there is no `:focus`, `:focus-visible`, `:focus-within`, or thumb-focus rule anywhere in the file, and nothing else changes on focus (the border is a static `var(--border-width-default) solid var(--border)`, the fill track is `pointer-events: none` decoration). The global ring at `packages/ui/theme/base.css:434` (`:focus-visible { outline: 2px solid var(--accent); outline-offset: 2px; }`) cannot win: base.css is wrapped in `@layer commonpub {` from line 1, and Vue SFC scoped styles are emitted unlayered (no `cssLayer`/postcss layer config in `layers/base` or `apps/reference`), so the unlayered `outline: none` beats the layered rule on cascade layers alone, before specificity even matters. The finding's specificity argument (0,2,0 vs 0,1,0) reaches the right conclusion by a slightly wrong mechanism. I also grepped `packages/ui/theme/*.css` for `slider-input`, `type=range`, and `range-thumb`: zero matches, so no global compensating rule exists. Because `appearance: none` also removes the UA-supplied focus styling, a keyboard user gets no visual focus on the slider at all — WCAG 2.4.7 (AA) failure on a path CLAUDE.md pins at "WCAG 2.1 AA minimum". 2. No live region. Lines 78-85 render the feedback panel with only `class` and `:class`. Its two sibling interactive blocks do the opposite: `BlockQuizView.vue:80` and `BlockCheckpointView.vue:21` both carry `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` on exactly this kind of feedback panel. So the omission is an inconsistency with the established in-repo pattern, not a deliberate convention. WCAG 4.1.3 (AA). No guard makes this unreachable. `BlockContentRenderer.vue:73-74` maps both `interactiveSlider` and `slider` to this component with no feature-flag check, and that renderer is mounted on public reader paths — `views/ArticleView.vue:175`, `views/ExplainerView.vue:301`, `pages/docs/[siteSlug]/[...pagePath].vue:326`, plus contest surfaces. There is no test pinning the behaviour: `layers/base/components/blocks/__tests__/` has ten test files and none is `BlockSliderView.test.ts`. Git history shows the file has three commits, none touching focus styling (`00790b98` original, `13498978` border-width theming, `f5c777e5` semantic text tokens), so `outline: none` is boilerplate carried from the initial port, not a considered decision. Severity P2 is right. It is a genuine double AA failure on an unflagged public reader path, but scoped to one optional block type that only appears where an author inserted a slider, so it is not a P1 site-wide blocker.

</details>

#### [P2] Keyboard reorder in FormTemplateEditor oscillates because the v-for is keyed by index

`layers/base/components/contest/FormTemplateEditor.vue:332`

The field cards are `:key="fi"` (the array index), so after `move()` splices the array Vue patches the existing DOM nodes in place rather than moving them; the focused Move-down button stays at DOM position `fi`, which now renders a *different* field.

**Failure:** An operator builds a registration form with 5 fields and wants to move field 1 to position 4 using the keyboard. They focus "Move field A down" and press Enter — A goes to index 1, and the aria-live region (line 328) correctly says "Moved A to position 2". Focus is still on the button in DOM slot 0, which now belongs to field B. Pressing Enter again moves B down, swapping A back to position 1. Repeated presses toggle forever; the field can never be moved more than one slot without re-finding it by mouse.

**Suggested fix:** Key by a stable per-field identity (`tf.key`, or a generated uid) so Vue moves the nodes with the data and focus travels with the moved card; or explicitly refocus `cardEls.value[to]`'s matching button after the emit.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed empirically, not just by reading. I mounted FormTemplateEditor in a wrapper with a real v-model:template (matching ContestEditor.vue:610), focused "Move Alpha down" on [Alpha, Beta, Gamma, Delta], and clicked document.activeElement three times: press 1 -> [Beta, Alpha, Gamma, Delta] with activeElement === the same DOM node and its aria-label now "Move Beta down"; press 2 -> back to [Alpha, Beta, Gamma, Delta]; press 3 -> [Beta, Alpha, ...]. Exact oscillation as claimed; a keyboard-only operator can never move a field more than one slot. No guard exists anywhere on the path: the card v-for at FormTemplateEditor.vue:373-375 is :key="fi" (index) and the up/down buttons at 381-382 are rendered unconditionally (contestPii/contestPrivateFiles/contestConditionalFields gate only field TYPES, not reorder); move() at 123-130 emits update:template and sets the aria-live string then returns, with no nextTick refocus anywhere in the file; the only focus-adjacent code is @focusin="activateCard(fi)", which never fires because focus never leaves the node, and the activeIndex watcher (66-75) only calls scrollIntoView. templateFieldMoved really does reorder (contestStages.ts:258-265, splice out then splice in). The existing test at __tests__/FormTemplateEditor.test.ts:92-109 covers only ONE click plus the disabled edge states, so nothing pins consecutive presses or focus. Two minor evidence errors that do not change the verdict: the keyed v-for is at 373-375 not 332, and templateFieldMoved is at contestStages.ts:258-265 not 168-175 (168 is templateFieldLabelChanged); the quoted code itself is accurate. Severity P2 holds: the component's own docblock advertises "keyboard-first" reorder so the broken interaction is the intended primary one and contests are live on deveco, but mouse users are unaffected, the audience is contest operators building a form occasionally rather than a daily end-user path, and no data is lost or leaked.

</details>

#### [P2] Contest banner repositioning is pointer-only with no keyboard equivalent

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestBannerAdjust.vue:121`

In `zoom` mode the framing box is repositioned exclusively by `@pointerdown` + document pointermove; the box has no `tabindex`, no role, and no keydown handler, and only the zoom level has a keyboard-operable control (the range input at line 129).

**Failure:** A keyboard-only organizer edits a contest, uploads a 3000x800 banner and switches to "Zoom" framing. The hint reads "Drag to reposition" (line 124). They can change zoom with the slider, but offsetX/offsetY can only be set by holding a pointer down on the box, so the banner's crop is stuck at its default centre and they cannot frame the image at all.

**Suggested fix:** Give the box `tabindex="0"`, an accessible name and an arrow-key handler that nudges offsetX/offsetY by a step (with Shift for a coarse step), mirroring the slider's role for zoom.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the whole file plus its call site and tests. In ContestBannerAdjust.vue the ONLY write path to meta.x/meta.y after mount is onPointerMove (line 84), reachable exclusively via @pointerdown on the .cpub-ba-box div (line 121); that div has no tabindex, no role, no keydown handler (grep for keydown|tabindex|ArrowLeft returns nothing), and touch-action:none. setMode (l.41-45) and onZoom (l.46-49) both pass pos.x/pos.y through unchanged, so the keyboard-reachable controls (three mode buttons, zoom range at l.129) cannot alter the crop offsets. ContestEditor.vue:555-556 is the sole call site and provides no numeric X/Y alternative. The existing axe test ("has no axe violations") does not guard this — axe cannot flag a missing keyboard handler on a role-less div — so the green suite is not counter-evidence. Git history shows an explicit a11y pass (sessions 223-226) that added .cpub-ba-mode:focus-visible and the zero-rect guard but left the drag box pointer-only, i.e. an omission rather than a documented decision. No feature flag makes it unreachable: contests are live (deveco), and Zoom is a user-selectable mode whose on-box hint reads "Drag to reposition". Severity P2 is right: a normative WCAG 2.1.1 (Level A) keyboard failure against the project's accessibility-first rule, but confined to organizers in one optional non-default mode, with Fill and Fit remaining fully keyboard-operable, so it degrades framing control rather than blocking the feature.

</details>

#### [P2] Blocking terms re-acceptance modal never moves, traps or restores focus

`layers/base/components/TermsReacceptanceGate.vue:53`

This globally-mounted `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` interstitial covers the whole app but does not call `useFocusTrap` (the layer's own helper), does not move focus into the card, does not mark the background inert, and offers no Escape path.

**Failure:** An operator bumps `termsVersion`. A logged-in keyboard user is halfway down /contests/x when the gate appears. Focus stays on the element behind the scrim; because `aria-modal="true"` tells the screen reader to treat everything outside the dialog as inert, the user's virtual cursor is now parked in content the AT reports as nothing at all. To reach "I accept" they must blind-Tab through every focusable element hidden behind the 70%-black overlay — each one still focusable and still clickable — with no visible focus because the scrim covers them.

**Suggested fix:** Add `const cardRef = ref<HTMLElement|null>(null); useFocusTrap(cardRef, () => visible, () => {})` (no-op escape, since the gate is non-dismissible) so focus moves to "I accept" and is trapped, and swap the scrim colour for `var(--color-surface-scrim)`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the code. layers/base/components/TermsReacceptanceGate.vue is 96 lines total; the entire `<script setup>` (lines 1-50) is auth/route/visibility/$fetch logic — there is no `useFocusTrap` import, no template ref on the card, no keydown handler, no `autofocus`, no body-scroll lock, and no focus restoration. Line 53 renders `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` and the plugin at layers/base/plugins/global-overlays.client.ts:31 mounts it into a `document.body` host div (`cpub-terms-gate-host`) on `app:mounted`, so it is a genuine app-wide interstitial that is a DOM sibling of the app root — aria-modal semantics therefore hide the entire rest of the page from AT while focus is still parked out there. No guard exists upstream or downstream: - layers/base/composables/useFocusTrap.ts is the layer's purpose-built helper (trap + initial focus + restore + scroll lock, plus an onUnmounted restore branch written precisely for v-if-unmounted modals). Ten-plus layer modals use it (ShareToHubModal, PublishErrorsModal, ImageCropperModal, RemoteFollowDialog, MirrorDetailModal, ContestSignup, admin layouts overlays…). This gate does not. - layers/base/components/__tests__/termsReacceptanceGate.test.ts pins only visibility, the POST, the logged-out no-op, and the /terms|/privacy suppression. Nothing pins focus behaviour, so no test contradicts the finding. - Nothing sets `inert`/`aria-hidden` on the app root (grep over plugins, app.vue, layouts/default.vue found only unrelated hits). - The flag is NOT off everywhere: `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns `"requireTermsAcceptance":true` (commonpub.io returns false). Session 229/236 handoffs confirm it has been ON on deveco since the lockout fix, and admin/settings.vue exposes `instance.termsVersion` as a runtime bump, so the trigger is a live operator action. Two sub-claims are overstated but do not rescue the finding: (a) background elements are NOT still clickable — `.cpub-trg` is `position:fixed; inset:0` and intercepts pointer events; only the Tab order leaks; (b) "no Escape path" is arguably correct-by-design for a mandatory re-acceptance dialog (APG permits omitting Escape where dismissal is not allowed), so the real fix is initial focus + trap + restore, not an Escape close. The line-74 `background: rgba(0,0,0,0.7)` hardcode is also real — the theme ships `--color-surface-overlay` (packages/ui/theme/dark.css:18 is exactly `rgba(0,0,0,0.7)`) and neighbouring modals use `var(--color-surface-overlay, rgba(0,0,0,0.5))` — but standing rule 3 formally scopes the var-only mandate to @commonpub/ui and @commonpub/docs, so that part is a convention deviation, not a rule violation. Severity P2 is right: a hard-blocking, keyboard-hostile modal on an instance where the flag is live, but reachable only when an operator bumps termsVersion (rare event, though it then hits every logged-in user at once and cannot be bypassed).

</details>

#### [P2] Toast live region is created in the same tick as its content, so success/info toasts may not be announced

`layers/base/components/AppToast.vue:7`

The `aria-live="polite"` container is behind `v-if="toasts.length"`, so the live region and its first message enter the accessibility tree simultaneously — the exact pattern the repo's own PersonaChipGrid docblock warns is dropped by several screen readers. The `role="status"` used for success/info shares that weakness.

**Failure:** An entrant saves a per-stage contest submission. `ContestStageSubmission.save()` routes every outcome through the toast — `toast.error('Please fill: ...')` at line 62, `toast.success(...)` at line 72, `toast.error(extractError(err))` at line 75. On a success the `role="status"` node is inserted together with its brand-new polite container; the screen reader user hears nothing and cannot tell whether their submission was recorded.

**Suggested fix:** Render the container unconditionally (empty when `toasts.length === 0`) and keep only the toast list behind `v-if`, matching the pattern PersonaChipGrid already documents.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. AppToast.vue:7 is `<div v-if="toasts.length" class="cpub-toast-container" aria-live="polite">` — the live region element itself is conditional. useToast.ts makes this worse than a first-toast-only issue: show() pushes onto the array and a setTimeout dismiss() filters it back to empty (3s success/info, 5s error), so toasts.length returns to 0 and the container is destroyed between toasts; EVERY success/info toast therefore gets a live region created in the same tick as its text. AppToast is mounted unconditionally at layouts/default.vue:241, so it is every page with no feature flag. The cited caller is real: ContestStageSubmission.vue save() routes all outcomes through the toast (toast.error on blockingFields, toast.success after the PUT, toast.error(extractError(err)) in catch), and the only other success signal is the plain `Submitted {{ submittedAtLabel }}` badge, which sits in no live region. Refutation attempts all failed: there is no AppToast test and no test anywhere references cpub-toast; git history shows the opposite of deliberate — commit 784ddb06 ("fix(ux): ... a11y live region") changed exactly one line, `role="status"` -> `:role="toast.type === 'error' ? 'alert' : 'status'"`, reasoning about polite-vs-assertive while leaving the v-if on the region untouched, and the v-if dates to the initial commit 00790b98. The house pattern elsewhere contradicts this file: AdminLayoutsAnnouncer.vue mounts two always-present cpub-sr-only role="status" divs bound to reactive text, FormTemplateEditor.vue:328 uses an always-present `<div class="cpub-sr-only" aria-live="polite">{{ announcement }}</div>`, and PersonaChipGrid.vue:69-71 states the rule verbatim. The finding's scoping is honest: error toasts get role="alert", whose dynamic insertion is the canonical APG alert pattern and is generally announced, which is why only success/info are claimed. Severity should be P2, not P3: this is the app-wide confirmation channel on the default layout, hit daily by every save/copy/revoke flow, and an unannounced status message is a WCAG 2.1 SC 4.1.3 Status Messages failure at Level AA, the minimum CLAUDE.md sets. Fix: render the container unconditionally (empty when there are no toasts) so toasts enter an existing region.

</details>

#### [P3] Visually-hidden file input is a focusable stop with no visible focus, duplicating the Choose file button

`layers/base/components/contest/ContestSubmissionField.vue:153`

The real `<input type="file">` is hidden with `.cpub-sr-only` (clip/1px, line 270) rather than `display:none`, so it stays in the tab order alongside the visible "Choose file" button (line 168) that proxies clicks to it.

**Failure:** A sighted keyboard user tabbing a contest form with a file field hits an invisible stop: focus lands on the clipped input, the page shows no focus ring anywhere, and the next Tab lands on "Choose file". Two tab stops open the same picker; the first one looks like focus has vanished.

**Suggested fix:** Add `tabindex="-1"` to the hidden input (the visible button is already the labelled, focusable proxy), or drop the proxy button and style the native input's `::file-selector-button`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. ContestSubmissionField.vue:153-161 renders the real <input type="file"> unconditionally in the file branch with class="cpub-sr-only" and NO tabindex="-1", no aria-hidden, no disabled, and it is not wrapped in a <label> — while line 168 is a separate <button @click="fileInput?.click()">. Line 270 (mirroring packages/ui/theme/base.css:452) hides it with position:absolute + 1px + clip:rect(0 0 0 0), which by design preserves tab order and also clips the element's painted outline, so the global :focus-visible ring at base.css:434 cannot show. Result: an invisible tab stop immediately before the visible "Choose file" button, both opening the same picker. No guard exists: the input renders even when filesEnabled is false (only the button/unavailable note is switched at 168/171) and is not disabled while uploading (the button is). Every other file input in the repo uses the correct single-stop label-wrapping idiom (ArticleEditor.vue:357, ContestEditor.vue:529/546, ProjectEditor.vue:253, JudgesShowcaseBlock.vue:148), so this is a departure, not a house pattern; git history (81839d91 P6, ea6cd020 P6 audit) shows no deliberate decision here. No test pins the behaviour — ContestSubmissionField.test.ts:111-145 covers the file field and runs axe over all types, but axe detects neither duplicate tab stops nor focus visibility. Reachable in production: contestPrivateFiles defaults false in the layer (config/src/schema.ts:52, nuxt.config.ts:112) but apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:27 sets it true and a live curl of https://deveco.io/api/features returns contestPrivateFiles:true; the only remaining gate is an operator template containing a file field. Severity P3 is right: it is a WCAG 2.4.7 AA focus-visible failure on an entrant-facing form (arguing toward P2), but nothing is blocked — the clipped input is still operable by Enter and the visible button works — so the harm is keyboard confusion on a template-dependent field.

</details>

### Performance and scalability

#### [P2] Feed list queries SELECT the entire content_items row including the full content JSONB, then discard it

`packages/server/src/content/content.ts:397`

Both the offset feed (listContent) and the keyset feed (listContentKeyset) select `content: contentItems` — every column of the row — but mapToListItem never reads `content`, `parts`, or `sections`, so every feed page reads and de-TOASTs full article bodies from Postgres and throws them away.

**Failure:** Homepage/explore feed request with limit=24 on an instance whose projects average 50KB of block JSON: Postgres reads and detoasts ~1.2MB of `content` jsonb plus `parts`/`sections` per page, serialises it over the wire to Node, drizzle parses it, and mapToListItem (content.ts:120-150) drops all of it — the returned ContentListItem has no body field. It is worse under federation: listContent sets `localLimit = offset + limit` (content.ts:352), so at offset=200 the query fetches 220 full rows including 220 article bodies to build one 20-item page. queryFederatedAsListItems has the identical shape (`fed: federatedContent`, content.ts:200) and pulls the remote `content` text + `cpubBlocks` jsonb it never maps.

**Suggested fix:** Replace `content: contentItems` with an explicit column list matching mapToListItem's inputs (a shared CONTENT_LIST_SELECT const, mirroring USER_REF_SELECT in query.ts:60). Do the same for queryFederatedAsListItems, listContestEntries and listContests.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code and by dumping the generated SQL. content.ts:394-408 (listContent) and :521-535 (listContentKeyset) both select `content: contentItems`; I compiled that exact drizzle shape and the emitted SQL lists every column explicitly, including "content_items"."content", "parts", "sections" (and "preview_token"). mapToListItem (content.ts:120-150) builds an explicit object that never reads content/parts/sections, and ContentListItem (types.ts:159-190) has no body field, so nothing downstream consumes them. Schema confirms all three are jsonb (packages/schema/src/content.ts:39,57,71). The federated half is the same: queryFederatedAsListItems selects `fed: federatedContent` (content.ts:200) while its mapper (content.ts:221-247) never touches row.fed.content or row.fed.cpubBlocks, both real columns (packages/schema/src/federation.ts:106,122). The amplification is real: content.ts:359-360 sets localLimit = offset + limit with localOffset = 0 on the federated path. No guard exists: these are the hottest read paths, unflagged and uncached — listContentKeyset backs layers/base/server/api/content/feed.get.ts and getUserContent (profile.ts:253); listContent backs api/content/index.get.ts, api/public/v1/content/index.get.ts, api/search/index.get.ts and both feed.xml routes. Git history shows the wide select is convenience, not intent (5cf3100f only added the category join beside an already-wide select); narrowing is blocked only by mapToListItem being typed on ContentItemRow. Secondary sites verified: contest/entries.ts:83 selects `entry: contestEntries` full-row (reading only stageState) while correctly narrowing the joined content/author right beside it, and contest/read.ts:74 is a bare db.select().from(contests) for a listing that maps ~20 scalars. Severity stays P2 — pure over-fetch, no correctness or security impact, but on a path every anonymous visitor hits.

</details>

#### [P2] /api/stats runs 8 uncached aggregates on an unauthenticated route the homepage awaits during SSR

`layers/base/server/api/stats.get.ts:6`

getPlatformStats issues eight aggregate queries — including unfiltered GROUP BYs over `users` (twice) and `content_items` (twice) and unfiltered COUNT(*) over `hubs` and `reports` — with no caching, no auth, and no route rule, and the homepage blocks SSR on it.

**Failure:** Every render of `/` (SSR and every client-side navigation) calls /api/stats. admin.ts:201-227 fires `SELECT role, count(*) FROM users GROUP BY role`, `SELECT status, count(*) FROM users GROUP BY status`, `count(*) FILTER (WHERE email_verified) FROM users`, `SELECT type, count(*) FROM content_items GROUP BY type`, `SELECT status, count(*) FROM content_items GROUP BY status`, `count(*) FROM hubs`, and two counts on `reports`. None of these can be answered from an index-only scan for a GROUP BY over the whole table, so each is a sequential scan. The endpoint requires no authentication, so a trivial loop against /api/stats drives 8 full-table scans per request; and because pages/index.vue:108 uses a blocking `await useFetch('/api/stats')`, homepage TTFB is gated on the slowest of them as content_items grows.

**Suggested fix:** Wrap the handler in `defineCachedEventHandler` with a maxAge of 60-300s (the numbers are display-only), or materialise the counters into a small stats table refreshed by an existing worker. At minimum add a routeRules cache entry for `/api/stats`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every cited file. stats.get.ts is 8 lines: `const stats = await getPlatformStats(db)` with no requireFeature, no requirePermission, and no defineCachedEventHandler — while the sibling api/admin/stats.get.ts calls the SAME function behind requireFeature('admin') + requirePermission(event,'audit.read'), so the unguarded public copy is the anomaly, not a deliberate cheap path. packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:191 getPlatformStats is exactly the eight-way Promise.all described: GROUP BY role over users, GROUP BY status over users, count(*) FILTER (WHERE email_verified) over users, GROUP BY type over content_items, GROUP BY status over content_items, count(*) over hubs, and two counts over reports — six of the eight have no WHERE at all, so none can be answered from an index-only scan. pages/index.vue:108 is a blocking top-level `await useFetch('/api/stats')`, one of six sequential top-level awaits in setup, so it serializes into homepage TTFB. nuxt.config.ts:186 is `routeRules: {}` and the only defineCachedEventHandler hits in the repo are Nitro's generated .nuxt type stubs — nothing is cached. No flag gates it and no upstream guard exists; apps/reference/e2e/api.spec.ts:22 actively PINS the unauthenticated 200. Blast radius is wider than claimed: explore.vue:114 and components/homepage/StatsSection.vue:3 also fetch it (both lazy, so non-blocking), and server/routes/nodeinfo/2.1.ts:13 runs all eight aggregates to read just users.total and content.total on a public federation-discovery endpoint remote instances poll. PARTIAL correction to the failure scenario: production is not un-throttled. layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:70 calls checkRateLimit, and getTierForPath in packages/infra/src/security.ts:274 places /api/stats in the `api` tier at 60 req/min keyed `ip:<ip>:/api/stats`, so a single-source loop is capped at ~480 aggregates/min rather than unbounded (the limiter is also skipped entirely in dev, and its Redis store is explicitly fail-open). The 1-request-to-8-sequential-scans amplification still stands for distributed sources, and the SSR TTFB coupling is untouched by rate limiting. Severity P2 is right: it is on a path every real user hits daily so it is not P3, but at current instance scale these tables are small and seq scans are sub-millisecond, so it is a scaling liability rather than a live outage — not P1. Separately worth noting outside the perf dimension: because the homepage reuses the admin DTO for four numbers, the anonymous response also discloses reports.pending, reports.total, users.byRole and users.byStatus.

</details>

#### [P2] Homepage SSR issues all six blocking fetches whichever of its three render branches is active

`layers/base/pages/index.vue:99`

index.vue awaits /api/homepage/sections, two /api/content calls, /api/stats, /api/hubs and /api/contests unconditionally in <script setup>, but the template picks exactly one of three branches (layout engine / custom sections / legacy) — so in the two non-legacy branches five of those SSR round-trips fetch data nothing renders.

**Failure:** An operator enables features.layoutEngine and seeds a homepage layout, so `layoutEngineActive` is true and the template renders only `<LayoutSlot>` (index.vue:177-189). The editorial fetch (line 99), the featured fetch (line 105), /api/stats (line 108), /api/hubs (line 110) and /api/contests (line 127) still execute and are still awaited during SSR — including the 8-aggregate stats call — even though `stats`, `communities`, `contests`, `featured` and `editorialPicks` are referenced only inside the `v-else` legacy branch (lines 226-438). The same applies to the `hasCustomSections` branch (line 190), where HomepageSectionRenderer fetches its own data per section, duplicating the work.

**Suggested fix:** Move the legacy fetches behind the branch condition (a `useLazyFetch` with `immediate` bound to the branch predicate), or extract the legacy tree into its own component that owns its fetches so Vue only mounts and fetches when that branch renders.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the page, the section components, and the API handlers — and the real reach is broader than the finding claims. 1) The fetches are unconditional and sequential in layers/base/pages/index.vue <script setup>: line 10 `await useFetch('/api/homepage/sections')`, 97-101 editorial (the ONLY guarded one, via `editorialEnabled.value`), 104 featured, 108 `/api/stats`, 110 `/api/hubs?limit=4`, 127 `/api/contests?limit=3`. All top-level `await`, none lazy, so each blocks SSR and they run one after another. `useLayout('/')` at line 69 adds a seventh non-lazy fetch. 2) Every consumer of `stats`, `communities`, `contests`/`activeContest`, `featured`, `editorialPicks` sits inside the legacy `v-else` (lines 226-502): template usages appear only at 238-280, 337-380, 423-469. Nothing in the `layoutEngineActive` branch (177-187) or `hasCustomSections` branch (190-223) reads them. No upstream guard, no route caching (`routeRules: {}` in layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:186; zero `defineCachedEventHandler` under layers/base/server/api). 3) The finding UNDERSTATES the reach. `features.layoutEngine` defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:132), so that branch is rare — but `hasCustomSections` is effectively always true: `getHomepageSections` (packages/server/src/homepage/homepage.ts:82) returns the 6-entry `DEFAULT_SECTIONS` whenever the instance setting is missing or empty, so the array is never length 0 on a healthy instance. The legacy `v-else` is therefore dead on every default instance and all five page-level fetches are wasted on EVERY homepage SSR render, not only behind an off-by-default flag. 4) Duplication confirmed: layers/base/components/homepage/StatsSection.vue:3 `await useFetch('/api/stats', { lazy: true })`, HubsSection.vue:7 `/api/hubs`, ContestsSection.vue:11 and HeroSection.vue:20 `/api/contests`, EditorialSection.vue:8 `/api/content`. Nuxt derives useFetch keys from the call-site autoKey plus URL, so these are distinct keys from the page-level calls — no dedupe. The homepage issues each of those queries twice. 5) Cost is real: `/api/stats` -> `getPlatformStats` (packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts) runs 8 unindexed aggregate COUNTs over users, content_items, hubs and reports (two GROUP BYs on users, two on content_items, plus report counts), executed twice per homepage render, for a sidebar card the active branch never draws. No test pins the current behaviour, and the surrounding comments discuss the layout-engine blank-page trap, not fetch cost — the unconditional fetches are leftover pre-Phase-2 setup that was never moved behind the branch it serves. Severity P2 is fair (latency/DB load only, nothing user-visible breaks), but note it is universal rather than flag-gated, which pushes it toward the top of P2.

</details>

#### [P2] Federation delivery fans out to inboxes serially under a fixed 5-minute lock, causing duplicate delivery

`packages/server/src/federation/delivery.ts:153`

deliverActivity POSTs to each target inbox one at a time with a 30s timeout each, while the activity's claim lock is stamped once at 5 minutes and never renewed — so an activity with more than ~10 slow inboxes outlives its own lock and is re-claimed and re-delivered by the next tick.

**Failure:** A local actor with followers on 15 fediverse domains posts a project. The worker claims the Create at delivery.ts:93 setting `lockedAt = now`; LOCK_EXPIRY_MS is 5 minutes (delivery.ts:17). resolveTargetInboxes returns 15 inboxes and delivery.ts:153 loops them sequentially, each `safeFetchSigned(signed, { timeoutMs: 30_000 })` (delivery.ts:184). If 11 of those domains are unreachable and time out, elapsed time is 330s > 300s, so the candidate query at delivery.ts:78-81 (`lockedAt <= lockExpiry`) matches the still-in-flight row on a later tick, a second delivery pass starts, and every inbox that already accepted the activity receives a duplicate Create — a duplicate post on the remote timeline. The circuit breaker only helps after failures have been recorded, i.e. after the first duplicating pass. The follower query is also unbounded (delivery.ts:328-337 and 380-386 select every accepted follower with no limit).

**Suggested fix:** Renew `lockedAt` between inboxes (the outbox worker's per-chunk lease renewal is the pattern to copy), and/or deliver inboxes with bounded parallelism plus a hard per-activity wall-clock budget well under LOCK_EXPIRY_MS.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. delivery.ts:17 sets LOCK_EXPIRY_MS to 5 minutes; the claim at :93 stamps lockedAt once and no code path anywhere renews it (lockedAt is only written at claim and nulled in markDelivered/markFailed/incrementAttempts). The fan-out at :153 is serial with a real 30s deadline per inbox (safeFetchSigned honours timeoutMs via packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts:320-341). It is actually worse than claimed: the outer loop at :106 is serial too and every row in the batch shares the same `now` lock stamp (:92-101), so with the default batchSize 20 the whole batch's elapsed time counts against rows not yet touched — ten activities with one timing-out inbox each already exceeds 300s. No upstream guard exists: layers/base/server/plugins/federation-delivery.ts:36 is a bare setInterval(30s) calling an un-awaited async runDelivery, with no in-flight flag, no advisory lock, and no FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED (grep found none under federation/ or server/plugins/), so overlapping ticks re-match the in-flight row via the lockedAt <= lockExpiry predicate at :78-81 and :97. The circuit breaker cannot pre-empt it (circuitBreaker.ts:12 threshold is 50 consecutive failures per domain). The unbounded follower selects at :407-415 and :421-429 are real. Tests pin only crash recovery (delivery-retry.test.ts:89), not renewal, and packages/server/src/comms/outbox.ts:158-165 renews lockExpiresAt before each chunk with a comment giving exactly this reason, proving the pattern was known and not applied. Feature reachability holds: features.federation defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:87) but is true in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:31 and federation is live. One overstatement: activity ids are minted once at enqueue and stored in the payload (hubFederation.ts:817, messaging.ts:125), so a re-delivery sends a byte-identical activity id that mainstream AP receivers dedupe — the visible "duplicate post on the remote timeline" is unlikely. The real harms are doubled outbound signed POSTs, concurrent duplicate passes, and a lost-update race in incrementAttempts (:532-554 read-modify-write of attempts) that halves the retry budget and dead-letters activities early. P2 stands: unguarded on a worker that runs every 30s on any federating instance, but only triggered once fan-out includes several unreachable domains.

</details>

#### [P2] syncFederatedHubCollection loops over remote-supplied orderedItems with no cap, 2 queries per item

`packages/server/src/federation/hubMirroring.ts:1536`

The hub-sync worker fetches a remote hub's cpub:resources / cpub:products collection and iterates every entry of the remote-controlled `orderedItems` array, doing a SELECT then an INSERT/UPDATE per item, then reads the entire local table for that hub and DELETEs stale rows one at a time — with no mirrorMaxItems cap, unlike every other mirroring path.

**Failure:** An accepted federated hub serves a cpub:resources OrderedCollection with 200,000 minimal `{id: "..."}` entries (well inside the 10MB MAX_RESPONSE_SIZE cap in packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts:149 — ~30 bytes per entry). refreshFederatedHubMetadata (hubMirroring.ts:227) calls syncFederatedHubCollection, which at hubMirroring.ts:1536 does one SELECT + one INSERT per item: 400,000 sequential queries and 200,000 rows written into federated_hub_resources, holding a pool connection the whole time. The stale-entry pass at hubMirroring.ts:1568-1575 then SELECTs the whole table for that hub and issues one DELETE per stale row. This repeats every hub-sync cycle (default 1h, federation-hub-sync.ts:35) because nothing records how far the sync got. backfillHubFromOutbox by contrast threads `mirrorMaxItems` through precisely to avoid this (hubMirroring.ts:1069-1072).

**Suggested fix:** Slice `items` to `federationConfig.mirrorMaxItems ?? 100` before the loop, batch the upserts (one multi-row INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE keyed on objectUri), and replace the delete pass with a single `DELETE ... WHERE federated_hub_id = $1 AND object_uri <> ALL($2)`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading packages/server/src/federation/hubMirroring.ts:1519-1635. syncFederatedHubCollection accepts no cap parameter; line 1530 takes collection.orderedItems verbatim from the remote and line 1536 loops every entry doing a SELECT(limit 1) plus an INSERT/UPDATE per item, then 1564-1576 SELECTs all rows for the hub and DELETEs stale ones one at a time. Products branch (1579-1626) is identical. No guard exists: safeFetch here is a pure re-export of @commonpub/protocol's (packages/server/src/import/ssrf.ts), bounded only by MAX_RESPONSE_SIZE = 10MB (packages/protocol/src/ssrf.ts:149), which still admits ~200-300k minimal {id} entries; there is no Zod/validator or totalItems check on the path; the local producer layers/base/server/routes/hubs/[slug]/resources.ts inlines every row from listHubResources (packages/server/src/hub/resources.ts:27-44, no limit) so even honest peers serve the whole collection. Reachable from refreshFederatedHubMetadata (:226-231), invoked fire-and-forget by getFederatedHub (:116) on any federated-hub page view with a >1h stale actor cache, and by layers/base/server/plugins/federation-hub-sync.ts:114 on the 1h loop; lastSyncAt is claimed before the work so the full re-scan repeats every cycle. By contrast backfillHubFromOutbox threads mirrorMaxItems (:1069-1072) and caps BACKFILL_MAX_PAGES = 20 (:1033). No test references syncFederatedHubCollection or federatedHubResources, and git history (4ebe57a2) shows the only change here was fetch -> safeFetch, so the uncapped loop is not deliberate. Two corrections to the claim, neither fatal: it does not hold one pool connection for the duration (drizzle/pg acquires per statement; the cost is 2N sequential round-trips repeated hourly), and it is not unique - fetchRemoteHubFollowers (:1242-1261) has the same unbounded per-page item loop bounded only by pages < 10. Gated on features.federateHubs, which defaults to false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:89) but is true in apps/reference and apps/shell configs, and needs an accepted federated hub row; that keeps it off P1 but P2 is correct.

</details>

#### [P3] advanceContestStage locks every entry then issues one UPDATE per row inside the transaction

`packages/server/src/contest/judging.ts:268`

The stage-advance transaction does SELECT ... FOR UPDATE over all of a contest's entries with no limit, then updates them one at a time in a JS loop, holding row locks on the whole cohort for the duration of N sequential round-trips.

**Failure:** An organizer advances a review stage on a 1,000-entry contest. judging.ts:242-246 selects all 1,000 rows `FOR UPDATE`; judging.ts:268-275 then issues 1,000 individual `UPDATE contest_entries SET stage_state = ... WHERE id = ?` statements sequentially inside that transaction. At ~2ms per round-trip that is a ~2s transaction holding write locks on every entry, during which any judge submitting a score (which takes the same row lock, judging.ts:162) blocks, and the HTTP request holds one of the 20 pool connections the entire time. The subsequent notification loop (judging.ts:291) then adds one un-awaited createNotification per entrant on top.

**Suggested fix:** Compute the advanced/eliminated split in JS as now, then write it with two set-based statements: one `UPDATE ... WHERE id = ANY($advanced)` and one for the eliminated set, using a jsonb expression to rebuild stage_state server-side — or a single `UPDATE ... FROM (VALUES ...)` join.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the source. judging.ts:242-246 does `tx.select({...}).from(contestEntries).where(eq(contestEntries.contestId, contestId)).for('update')` with no LIMIT and no eliminated-filter (so it locks even rows discarded by the `isEliminated` filter on line 249), and judging.ts:268-275 loops `eligible` issuing one sequential `await tx.update(contestEntries).set({ stageState: next }).where(eq(contestEntries.id, e.id))` per row inside that transaction. Reachable in production: layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/advance.post.ts:21 calls it from an HTTP route behind `requireFeature('contests')`, which defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:31) but is enabled on deveco, which has run a live contest. No upstream bound on N: `maxEntriesPerUser` (schema/src/validators/contest.ts:389, enforced at server/src/contest/entries.ts:263) caps entries PER USER, not the cohort, so nothing limits total contest entries. Pool `max: 20` confirmed at layers/base/server/utils/db.ts:27. The un-awaited `createNotification(...).catch(()=>{})` fan-out at judging.ts:291-305 is real and unbounded as well. Tests (contest.integration.test.ts:143/167/227, contest-stage-submissions.integration.test.ts:183/247) pin only cut correctness on 2-3 entry fixtures; nothing pins or bounds the write pattern. Strongest counterargument checked and rejected as a full refutation: the FOR UPDATE is deliberate (commit 560f1152 "advance-stage lock", comment at lines 234-240) to prevent a lost update between concurrent organizers and mid-advance judges, and the per-row UPDATE is required because each row's stageState is merged from that row's own prior array (line 270) — that justifies the transaction and the lock, but not the missing bound or the sequential round-trips. Severity downgraded to P3: this is an organizer-only action fired at most once per review stage per contest, blast radius is one contest's rows, and the ~2s lock-hold requires a 1,000-entry cohort no instance currently has; at realistic tens-of-entries scale the transaction is well under 100ms.

</details>

#### [P3] Contest reminder sweep claims + renders every registrant of a contest inside one transaction

`packages/server/src/contest/reminders.ts:196`

sweepContestReminders claims ALL eligible registrants of a contest in a single INSERT...SELECT with no LIMIT, renders one email HTML body per recipient into an in-memory array, and writes them all in one multi-row INSERT inside the same transaction.

**Failure:** A contest reaches its T-7d milestone with, say, 5,000 registrants. The CTE at reminders.ts:196 inserts 5,000 ledger rows and returns 5,000 recipients; reminders.ts:217 builds 5,000 OutboxMessage objects, each carrying a fully rendered HTML body (tens of KB), all live in the heap at once; enqueueEmails (outbox.ts:47) then issues ONE INSERT with 5,000 rows x ~8 columns. If that blows the heap or the driver's statement size, the transaction rolls back, the ledger claim is un-done, and the catch at reminders.ts:252 logs + `continue`s — so the same contest fails identically on every subsequent sweep and its participants silently never receive a deadline reminder. There is no LIMIT anywhere in the claim, so the batch size is exactly the registrant count.

**Suggested fix:** Add `LIMIT n` (e.g. 500) to the claim CTE and loop the claim until it returns fewer than n rows, committing each chunk in its own transaction. Exactly-once still holds because the ON CONFLICT ledger is per (contest,user,milestone).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code, but the severity is overstated. What I verified: (1) packages/server/src/contest/reminders.ts:196-212 — the claim CTE has no LIMIT and no status filter; it inserts one ledger row per row of `contest_registrations` for the contest joined to `users` (only email_verified / unsubscribedAll / legacyGuard predicates) and RETURNs every claimed recipient. (2) reminders.ts:217-247 — `recipients.map(...)` renders a full HTML body per recipient into one in-memory array, and (3) reminders.ts:249 `await enqueueEmails(tx, messages)` → packages/server/src/comms/outbox.ts:45-48 `db.insert(emailOutbox).values(messages.map(toRow))`, a single multi-row INSERT. All three are inside one `db.transaction` (line 195) whose catch at 252-257 logs and `continue`s, so a deterministic failure un-claims the ledger and repeats identically every 10-minute tick (layers/base/server/plugins/contest-reminders.ts INTERVAL_MS = 600_000), losing the milestone permanently. Nothing upstream caps registrations per contest (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:755 has only the (contest_id,user_id) unique). The hard ceiling is real: DB is node-postgres (packages/server/src/types.ts:1), so the extended-protocol 65535-bind-parameter cap breaks at roughly 8k recipients x ~8 columns, before any heap concern. The codebase already recognises this exact hazard on the sibling path: packages/server/src/comms/broadcast.ts:57 `const ENQUEUE_CHUNK = 500` with the comment "Chunk the inserts so a large audience doesn't build one giant statement" — reminders.ts simply never got that treatment (git show d0c944ba added the transaction wrapper without considering batch size). Why not P2: `contestReminders` defaults to false in every config source I read (packages/config/src/schema.ts:60, layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:116, packages/test-utils/src/mockConfig.ts:26) and needs an explicit operator opt-in plus `emailNotifications`; no memory or config record shows it enabled on any live instance. The harmful threshold is low-thousands of registrants on a single contest, which is far outside what these maker-community instances hold today. At realistic sizes (tens to hundreds) the only cost is holding a pooled connection open while rendering templates. Real latent scalability defect, not a live one: P3.

</details>

#### [P3] People search pagination has no unique tiebreaker, so results can be skipped between pages

`layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts:74`

The `type=people` branch orders by `desc(users.createdAt)` alone with LIMIT/OFFSET; created_at is not unique, so rows tied on it may be returned in a different order by the page-1 and page-2 queries.

**Failure:** An instance imports or bulk-creates members (a signup burst, a seed script, a migration) so several users share a created_at value. A visitor searches people with limit=24: users A and B are tied on created_at and straddle the offset-24 boundary. Postgres is free to order the tie differently across the two independent queries, so page 2 either re-shows A or omits B entirely — the searcher never sees that member. This is the exact defect the codebase guards against everywhere else and documents at content.ts:355-361 and registrations.ts:373 ("createdAt is not unique; the id tiebreaker keeps pagination stable"); this branch was missed. The same branch also runs an unconditional COUNT(*) over `users` with a leading-wildcard ILIKE on every request, including deep pages.

**Suggested fix:** Append `desc(users.id)` to the orderBy. Also skip the COUNT(*) when offset > 0 and return it through toPageMeta, as the content branches already do.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts:64-78 orders the type=people page by desc(users.createdAt) alone with LIMIT/OFFSET and no unique tiebreaker; the branch has no desc(users.id). The tie is achievable: packages/schema/src/auth.ts:56 defines created_at as timestamp().defaultNow(), and Postgres now() is the transaction timestamp, so a seed script or bulk import inserting in one transaction gives every row the same value. The path is reachable with no guard: layers/base/pages/search.vue:45 pushes the People pill unconditionally (unlike the flag-gated Communities pill at :42), and :49-66 paginate with pageSize 24 via an offset query; searchQuerySchema accepts any offset; the handler does not re-sort in JS and returns hasMore from rows.length. No test pins the branch (grep for 'people' across layers/base/server, apps/reference/e2e and packages hits only the route line). The convention is documented for this exact column in packages/server/src/persona/directory.ts:887-890 ("created_at alone is not a total order (a seeded instance and a bulk import both produce ties), and an offset page over a non-deterministic order repeats and skips rows silently"), and applied in contentSearch.ts:230-236, content/content.ts:365-370, contest/registrations.ts:392 — this branch was missed, and git history shows it predates those commits rather than being a deliberate exception. Severity lowered to P3: two conditions must coincide (a bulk-created cohort sharing created_at AND a people query matching more than 24 members), and the worst outcome is one member absent from one page of search results, with no privacy, data-loss or availability consequence — unlike the directory path where the same bug mis-discloses. The secondary claim, an unconditional count(*) with a leading-wildcard ILIKE at line 77, is accurate but is a small-table scan, not a P2 perf problem.

</details>

#### [P3] Daily digest scheduler reads the whole users table then issues 2 queries per user

`layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts:136`

runDigest selects every user with a non-null emailNotifications value (no limit, no digest-preference filter in SQL) and then calls listNotifications once per user sequentially — and because that call passes offset 0, each iteration also runs a COUNT(*) it never reads.

**Failure:** At 08:00 UTC on a 50,000-user instance, the query at notification-email.ts:136-149 returns ~50,000 rows into memory. The loop at :154 then filters `prefs.digest` in JS — so a user with digest 'none' (the default) is still fetched — and for every user who does have a digest preference calls listNotifications (:168), which is 2 queries because normalizePagination yields offset 0 and notification.ts:78 runs `countRows` on the first page. That is up to 100,000 sequential round-trips on one connection, serially, while the rest of the instance shares the remaining pool. The `total` returned is discarded at :168 (only `items` is destructured).

**Suggested fix:** Push the digest filter into SQL (`emailNotifications->>'digest' IN ('daily','weekly')` plus the unsubscribedAll guard), page the user read, and replace the per-user notification fetch with one grouped query over `notifications WHERE user_id = ANY($ids) AND read = false AND created_at >= $since`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. notification-email.ts:136-149 selects id/email/username/emailNotifications/emailVerified from `users` with only `isNotNull(users.emailNotifications)` (+ an optional `emailVerified` gate) — no LIMIT, no digest filter in SQL, even though the codebase already filters this same JSONB in SQL elsewhere (packages/server/src/comms/broadcast.ts:35 uses `emailNotifications ->> 'unsubscribedAll'`). The loop at :154 filters `digest` in JS (`if (!prefs?.digest) continue; if (prefs.digest === 'none') continue;`) and at :168 awaits `listNotifications` per user, sequentially. packages/server/src/notification/notification.ts:62-79 confirms that call is two queries on the first page: `Promise.all([select..., offset === 0 ? countRows(db, notifications, where) : COUNT_NOT_COMPUTED])`, and `normalizePagination` yields offset 0 since none is passed — the resulting `total` is discarded (`const { items } =`). No guard makes this unreachable: the only bound is the once-per-day `digest_runs` claim at :127-134, which limits how often the job runs, not the work inside it. The feature flag `emailNotifications` defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:99, layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:129) but is ON on deveco, so the path is live. No test pins the query shape (no __tests__ under server/plugins/), and docs/reference/email-gdpr-scaling-analysis.md:218-227 explicitly flags the digest send path as an unfixed scaling risk — documented, not guarded. Two corrections to the finding's wording, neither fatal: (a) the column has no DB default (packages/schema/src/auth.ts:41 is a nullable jsonb), so users who never opened settings are NULL and already excluded by SQL — the fetched-then-JS-skipped set is users who saved /settings/notifications, which always writes `digest` including 'none' (layers/base/pages/settings/notifications.vue:36); (b) the two per-user queries are concurrent via Promise.all, it is the loop that serializes. Severity P3 is right: correctness is unaffected, it is a once-daily off-peak background job, and current instance user counts are orders of magnitude below the 50k scenario.

</details>

#### [P3] Postgres search fallback uses leading-wildcard ILIKE with no trigram index

`packages/server/src/search/contentSearch.ts:201`

searchWithPostgres — the path actually serving search on instances without MEILI_URL — filters with `title ILIKE '%q%' OR description ILIKE '%q%'`, which no btree index can serve, and pairs it with a COUNT(*) over the same predicate.

**Failure:** A visitor searches on an instance with no Meilisearch configured (deveco is demonstrably on this path — STATUS.md records /api/search returning the COUNT_NOT_COMPUTED sentinel, which only searchWithPostgres emits). Each request runs two sequential scans of content_items evaluating ILIKE on title and description for every published row. packages/schema/src/content.ts:107-132 has btree indexes on author/status/type/published_at and two composite feed indexes, but nothing that can answer a leading-wildcard match, and pg_trgm is not enabled anywhere in the schema. The same pattern is in buildContentConditions (content.ts:314-318) for the feed's `search` filter and in searchProducts (product.ts:317).

**Suggested fix:** Add `CREATE EXTENSION pg_trgm` plus GIN trigram indexes on content_items(title) and content_items(description) in a migration, or add a generated tsvector column with a GIN index and switch the fallback to websearch_to_tsquery.

<details><summary>Verifier (medium confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED on the code, with two corrections to the framing. What I verified by reading: - `packages/server/src/search/contentSearch.ts:198-207` — `const pattern = '%' + query + '%'` then `or(ilike(contentItems.title, pattern), ilike(contentItems.description, pattern))`. Exactly as claimed. (Note the file's own header comment at line 5 says the fallback "uses Postgres full-text search (to_tsvector/websearch_to_tsquery)" — that comment is stale; the implementation is ILIKE.) - `contentSearch.ts:263-271` — the paired `count(*)::int` over the identical `where`, on `offset === 0` only. Confirmed. - `packages/schema/src/content.ts:105-134` — the full index list on `content_items` is btree only: author_id, status, type, published_at, is_editorial, category_id, two partial composite feed indexes (`idx_content_items_feed_recency`, `idx_content_items_feed_popular`), `author_status`, `type_status_published`. Nothing that can serve a leading-wildcard match. - No pg_trgm anywhere: `grep -rl trgm packages/schema/migrations/` returns nothing across all 49 migrations (0000–0048), and the only GIN index in the schema is `idx_conversations_participants_gin`. - `packages/server/src/federation/timeline.ts:493` — inline `to_tsvector(...) @@ websearch_to_tsquery(...)` with no supporting expression index. Confirmed. - `packages/server/src/product/product.ts:268` and `:317` — `ilike(products.name, '%'+escapeLike(search)+'%')`. Confirmed. Reachability — no guard, and the class is BROADER than the cited location. `layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts` is a public unauthenticated GET. Its only query guards are `z.string().max(200)` and an empty-string early return — there is no minimum length, so `q=a` is accepted and matches nearly every row. Meili is used only when `process.env.MEILI_URL` is set (line 110-114), and `searchContent` (contentSearch.ts:291-298) falls through to `searchWithPostgres` otherwise. Critically, the branch at line 137 (`config.features.seamlessFederation && !usesSearchOnlyFilters`) deliberately OUTRANKS Meilisearch and delegates to `listContent`, whose `buildContentConditions` at `packages/server/src/content/content.ts:316-319` runs the SAME unindexed `title ILIKE ... OR description ILIKE ...`. So on a federation-enabled instance the default search path never reaches contentSearch.ts:201 at all — it hits the identical pattern in content.ts. Either way the predicate is unindexable. Two corrections to the reporter's evidence: 1. "COUNT_NOT_COMPUTED ... which only searchWithPostgres emits" is wrong. `listContent` skips COUNT past page 1 too (the route comment at index.get.ts:151-152 says so explicitly), so the STATUS.md sentinel does not prove deveco is on searchWithPostgres. It proves nothing about which of the two ILIKE paths is live — but both are ILIKE, so the conclusion stands. 2. "two sequential scans" overstates the items half.
