With `ORDER BY published_at DESC NULLS LAST, id DESC` + LIMIT and the matching partial index `idx_content_items_feed_recency`, the planner can walk that index in order and filter on ILIKE, stopping early for common terms. It is the page-1 `COUNT(*)` that genuinely must evaluate ILIKE against every published+public row, with no early exit. Severity: P3 is right, keep it. This is a scalability characteristic, not a live failure. It is on a daily-hit path, which argues up, but the mitigating facts argue back down: `content_items` on the three live instances is small (hundreds to low thousands of rows), where a filtered scan costs single-digit ms; Meilisearch is the documented production answer and the Postgres path is explicitly labelled a fallback in-file; and production has a per-IP rate limit (`layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts`, Redis-backed when `NUXT_REDIS_URL` is set) bounding the abuse case. It becomes a real problem at tens of thousands of rows or under concurrency, not today. Confidence is medium rather than high only because the perf IMPACT is a projection I could not measure against a real table — the code facts themselves I confirmed at high confidence.

</details>

#### [P3] Unread-message count scans full message history, re-run every 30s per SSE connection

`packages/server/src/messaging/messaging.ts:266`

getUnreadMessageCount counts over every message in every conversation the user participates in, anti-joined against message_reads, with no time or watermark bound — and the SSE stream calls it on a 30-second timer for every connected user plus on every pub/sub nudge.

**Failure:** A user with 20 conversations totalling 30,000 messages keeps a tab open. layers/base/server/api/realtime/stream.get.ts:99-102 calls getUnreadCount and getUnreadMessageCount every 30 seconds (and again on every notification/message event). The message query joins conversations via the GIN `participants @> [userId]` predicate, walks all 30,000 messages in those conversations, and does an anti-join probe into message_reads for each — so the work is proportional to lifetime history, not to the (usually zero) unread count. With 500 such users connected that is ~17 executions/second of a 30,000-row anti-join, permanently, against the same 20-connection pool.

**Suggested fix:** Store a per-(conversation,user) last_read_at watermark and count `messages.created_at > watermark`, which an index on messages(conversation_id, created_at) answers directly; or at minimum bound the scan with `messages.created_at > now() - interval '90 days'`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. (1) packages/server/src/messaging/messaging.ts:262-282 — getUnreadMessageCount selects count(*) from messages innerJoin conversations, filtered only by `participants @> [userId]`, `senderId != userId`, and a notExists probe into message_reads; there is no createdAt bound, no watermark, no limit. (2) packages/schema/src/social.ts:174-201 — no read-watermark column exists to bound on: `messages` has no readAt, and message_reads stores one row per (message,user), confirmed by markMessagesRead (messaging.ts:320-348) which inserts one row per unread message. So the anti-join must consider every message in every conversation the user participates in, for the life of the account. (3) layers/base/server/api/realtime/stream.get.ts:99-102 and 132-138 — Promise.all([getUnreadCount, getUnreadMessageCount]) runs on connect, on every pub/sub nudge, and on a 30000ms setInterval. (4) layers/base/server/utils/db.ts:27 — `max: 20`, so the cited 20-connection pool is accurate. No guard makes this unreachable: layers/base/layouts/default.vue:90-98 calls connectMessages()/connectNotifications() in onMounted for every authenticated user on every page, gated only on isAuthenticated with NO feature flag (useFeatures() is destructured in that file but messaging/realtime is not among the gates). There is no caching — the handler comment states the pub/sub payload is only a nudge and is never trusted as source of truth, so every trigger refetches from the DB. The only mitigations are partial and do not address the finding: MAX_CONNECTIONS_PER_USER=10 caps per-user amplification, and the sending/pendingSend coalescing collapses bursty pub/sub triggers into one in-flight query — neither bounds the 30s timer nor the rows scanned. getConversationUnreadCounts (messaging.ts:285-314) has the identical unbounded shape, so this is a pattern rather than a considered trade-off. Severity P3 is correct: work is proportional to lifetime history rather than unread count and the path runs for every logged-in user with a tab open, but the absolute cost only becomes material at message volumes the deployed instances do not have, and there is no correctness impact.

</details>

### Input validation and error handling across the HTTP surface

#### [P1] /api/search difficulty is an unvalidated string bound to the `difficulty` pg enum — unauthenticated 500

`layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts:11`

`difficulty` is declared `z.string().optional()` and then cast straight to the enum type on both search paths, so any value reaches `eq(contentItems.difficulty, <value>)` against a Postgres enum column.

**Failure:** Anonymous `GET /api/search?q=a&difficulty=zzz`. With `seamlessFederation` on (the live config on commonpub.io) the route takes the listContent branch and passes `difficulty: params.difficulty as ContentFilters['difficulty']` (line 144) into `listContent`, which does `eq(contentItems.difficulty, filters.difficulty)` (packages/server/src/content/content.ts:314). Postgres answers `invalid input value for enum difficulty: "zzz"` (22P02) → unhandled → 500. With the flag off and no Meili, the same value lands on contentSearch.ts:177 with an identical result. Note `/api/content` gets this right (`contentFiltersSchema` uses `difficultySchema`); only `/api/search` redefines the filter loosely.

**Suggested fix:** Replace `difficulty: z.string().optional()` with the shared `difficultySchema` (already exported from @commonpub/schema and used by `contentFiltersSchema`), and add a whitelist guard next to the existing `validContentTypes` check in `searchWithPostgres`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full call graph and by an empirical DB check. layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts:11 declares `difficulty: z.string().optional()` with no whitelist, and both branches pass it straight to a pg-enum comparison: line 144 -> resolveContentQuery (layers/base/server/utils/contentQuery.ts, which only rewrites status/visibility and never touches difficulty) -> listContent -> buildContentConditions `eq(contentItems.difficulty, filters.difficulty)` at packages/server/src/content/content.ts:314 (canMergeFederated returns false when difficulty is set, so the local enum query definitely executes); line 167 -> packages/server/src/search/contentSearch.ts:177 identical. No upstream guard: parseQueryParams only enforces the loose Zod shape; middleware/features.ts explicitly skips /api and has no search entry; the route uses getOptionalUser, never requireAuth; grep for `22P02`/`invalid input value for enum` across layers+packages finds no error handler; no test file exists under server/api/search/__tests__. Verified the DB behaviour against the live dev Postgres (commonpub-postgres-1): `PREPARE p AS select id from content_items where difficulty = $1; EXECUTE p('zzz')` returns `ERROR: invalid input value for enum difficulty: "zzz"`, and `\dT+ difficulty` confirms the 3-label pg enum; driver is `pg ^8.13` in both packages/server and layers/base. The finding actually understates reach: layers/base/pages/search.vue:62 sends `difficulty: diffFilters.value.join(',')` and layers/base/components/SearchFilters.vue renders three independent checkboxes with a toggleDiff that appends, so checking TWO difficulty boxes in the shipped UI sends `difficulty=beginner,intermediate` and 500s the public search page for an ordinary logged-out user. The neighbouring `type` filter IS whitelisted (index.get.ts:140 CONTENT_TYPES, contentSearch.ts:170 validContentTypes) and /api/content uses difficultySchema (packages/schema/src/validators/content.ts:19,31), so this is a miss rather than a deliberate design. Severity P1 stands: unauthenticated, trivially triggerable, on a primary public surface, and hit by normal UI use.

</details>

#### [P2] /api/users?ids= splits a raw string straight into a uuid `inArray` — unauthenticated 500

`layers/base/server/api/users/index.get.ts:26`

The `ids` query param is validated only for length (`z.string().max(2000)`), then split on commas and passed to `inArray(users.id, idList)`, where `users.id` is a `uuid` column — a non-uuid element makes Postgres reject the bind.

**Failure:** Anonymous `GET /api/users?ids=abc` (no auth, no feature flag on this route). `idList = ['abc']` → `inArray(users.id, ['abc'])` → `invalid input syntax for type uuid: "abc"` → unhandled → 500. `?ids=` with 50 junk values behaves the same. This is the exact class `layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts` documents on `isUuid` ("a non-uuid string reaching the bind throws an unhandled 500") — the helper exists and is used by `/api/events`, but not here.

**Suggested fix:** Filter the split list through the existing `isUuid` helper (drop non-uuids, or 400 the request) before building the `inArray` condition.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code and reproducing the DB error. layers/base/server/api/users/index.get.ts has no auth, no requireFeature, no try/catch: line 9 validates `ids` only as `z.string().max(2000)`, lines 24-26 split on commas and pass the raw strings to `inArray(users.id, idList)`. packages/schema/src/auth.ts:6 confirms `id: uuid('id')`, and Drizzle's PgUUID driver mapper is identity, so junk reaches the bind. Reproduced against the running dev Postgres: `SELECT id FROM users WHERE id IN ('abc')` -> `ERROR: invalid input syntax for type uuid: "abc"`. Refutation attempts all failed: csrf.ts:46 exempts GET; public-api-auth.ts:31 only guards /api/public/; auth.ts attaches an optional session but requires none; nuxt.config.ts:186 is `routeRules: {}`; there is no Nitro error hook in server/plugins/; the only test dir file is persona-profile-route.test.ts, which does not cover this. `grep -rn isUuid layers/base/server/api/` returns exactly one call site (events/index.get.ts:29), so the helper genuinely is not applied here, and its own docstring names this hazard. Git history (374e5cc3 "fix(security): replace raw SQL with Drizzle inArray") shows the inArray was an injection fix, not deliberate omission of validation. Severity lowered to P2: grep across layers/packages/apps finds ZERO callers passing `ids=` (all /api/users hits are /api/users/:username/*), so no real user or UI path triggers it; it needs a deliberately crafted anonymous request, and the impact is a caught 500 with log/alert noise, not a data leak, outage, or process crash.

</details>

#### [P2] /api/search dateFrom/dateTo are unvalidated strings that reach a timestamp bind — unauthenticated 500

`layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts:14`

The search route declares `dateFrom`/`dateTo` as bare `z.string().optional()`, and `searchWithPostgres` wraps them in `new Date(...)` before handing the value to Drizzle's timestamp column, whose `mapToDriverValue` calls `.toISOString()` — which throws `RangeError: Invalid time value` on an Invalid Date.

**Failure:** Anonymous `GET /api/search?q=a&dateFrom=x`. `usesSearchOnlyFilters` goes true (search/index.get.ts:135), so the route skips the listContent branch and calls `searchContent`. In `searchWithPostgres` (packages/server/src/search/contentSearch.ts:178) `gte(contentItems.publishedAt, new Date('x'))` builds a param from an Invalid Date; drizzle-orm/pg-core/columns/timestamp.js:34 does `value.toISOString()` → RangeError → unhandled → 500 to an unauthenticated caller. With Meilisearch configured the Meili path builds `publishedAtTs >= NaN` (contentSearch.ts:100), Meili rejects the filter, `searchContent` catches it and falls back to the same Postgres path, so the 500 happens either way. The general-tier limiter allows 60/min per IP, so this is a cheap repeatable error-rate spike.

**Suggested fix:** Validate the domain, not the shape: `dateFrom: z.string().datetime().optional()` (or a `^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$` regex plus a `Number.isFinite(new Date(v).getTime())` refine) in `searchQuerySchema`, and defensively drop a non-finite date inside `searchWithPostgres`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code and reproducing the throw. layers/base/server/api/search/index.get.ts:14-15 declares dateFrom/dateTo as bare z.string().optional(), and parseQueryParams (layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts:82) only safeParses — no coercion or refine. Both branches reach the bug: with seamlessFederation off, control falls through to searchContent (line 179); with it on, usesSearchOnlyFilters (line 135) is true precisely BECAUSE dateFrom is set, so the listContent branch at line 137 is skipped — the flag is not a guard. packages/server/src/search/contentSearch.ts:178 does gte(contentItems.publishedAt, new Date(opts.dateFrom)) unchecked, and searchContent (286-299) try/catches only the Meili call, so the Meili path's `publishedAtTs >= NaN` (line 100) falls back into the same Postgres call. packages/schema/src/content.ts:102 uses timestamp(..., {withTimezone:true}) = drizzle default 'date' mode, whose PgTimestamp.mapToDriverValue calls value.toISOString(). I ran this in packages/server against the real schema: d.select().from(contentItems).where(gte(contentItems.publishedAt,new Date('x'))).toSQL() → THREW: RangeError Invalid time value. Nothing catches it → unauthenticated 500. grep for dateFrom across packages/ and layers/ finds no validator anywhere; the only test (packages/server/src/__tests__/content-search.integration.test.ts:138) passes a valid '2020-01-01' and does not pin the invalid case. Reachability is BROADER than claimed and not attacker-only: layers/base/components/SearchFilters.vue:87-88 renders the date range as free-text type="text" inputs emitting on every @input keystroke, and layers/base/pages/search.vue:64-77 feeds them into a computed that useFetch watches with no debounce, so typing "2020-01-01" passes through "20", "2020-0" and "2020-01-0" — all Invalid Date in V8 — firing three 500s mid-typing for an ordinary logged-out visitor; the panel is behind no flag. Severity corrected P1→P2: the RangeError is thrown at query-BUILD time before any DB round trip, so it is cheaper than a successful search — no amplification, no resource exhaustion, no data exposure, no auth bypass. The durable harm is 500-instead-of-400 on an unauthenticated route plus unhandled-error log noise, matching the project's own precedent at packages/server/src/query.ts:275-300 where decodeCursor rejects unparseable dates for this exact reason.

</details>

#### [P2] /api/events/:slug/attendees casts an arbitrary `status` query value to the attendee pg enum

`layers/base/server/api/events/[slug]/attendees.get.ts:44`

`query.status` is taken from `getQuery` with no schema at all and type-asserted to `AttendeeStatus`, then bound with `eq(eventAttendees.status, opts.status)` against the `event_attendee_status` enum column.

**Failure:** Anonymous `GET /api/events/<published-slug>/attendees?status=zzz` on an instance with the `events` flag on. `listEventAttendees` pushes `eq(eventAttendees.status, 'zzz')` (packages/server/src/events/events.ts:287) → `invalid input value for enum event_attendee_status: "zzz"` → 500. Repeating the param (`?status=a&status=b`) makes `getQuery` return an array, which fails the bind the same way. The sibling route `/api/events` gets this right — it whitelists `status` through `PUBLIC_STATUSES` before use.

**Suggested fix:** Parse the query with `parseQueryParams` and a `z.enum(['registered','waitlisted','cancelled','attended']).optional()` for `status` (also giving `limit`/`offset` a schema instead of bare `Number()`).

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED — I read the whole route and reproduced the failure against the live dev Postgres. Route (`layers/base/server/api/events/[slug]/attendees.get.ts`): the only gates before the query are `requireFeature('events')`, a `canReadHubById` check that is skipped entirely when `existing.hubId` is null, and a draft gate that passes for `status === 'published' | 'active'`. Then line 37 `const query = getQuery(event)` and line 44 `status: (query.status as AttendeeStatus) || undefined` — a bare TypeScript assertion, no zod, no whitelist, no `isUuid`-style door check. `AttendeeStatus` is just a union alias (`packages/server/src/events/events.ts:8`), so the cast erases at runtime. Sink: `listEventAttendees` (events.ts:281-288) does `if (opts.status) conditions.push(eq(eventAttendees.status, opts.status))`. The column is `eventAttendeeStatusEnum('status')` (`packages/schema/src/events.ts:45`) over `pgEnum('event_attendee_status', [...])` (`packages/schema/src/enums.ts:171`). Drizzle emits an untyped placeholder with no cast. Empirical proof against `commonpub-postgres-1`: `PREPARE t2 AS SELECT count(*) FROM event_attendees WHERE status = $1` infers `parameter_types = {event_attendee_status}`, and executing it with 'zzz' gives `ERROR: invalid input value for enum event_attendee_status: "zzz"` (22P02). Nothing catches it: `layers/base/server/utils/errors.ts` only exports throw-helpers, there is no nitro `error` hook or error middleware, and no events route wraps the call — so it surfaces as an unhandled 500. The repeated-param variant is the same: `getQuery` yields an array, which pg serialises to `{a,b}` and fails the identical bind. Refutation attempts that failed: (1) the `events` flag defaults to `false` in `packages/config/src/schema.ts:84`, but the session-182 live `curl /api/features` snapshot records events ON on all three instances (commonpub / deveco / heatsync), so this is anonymously reachable in production; (2) no test pins the behaviour — grep found no attendee-status test anywhere in `layers/base` or `packages/server`; (3) git history shows no deliberate decision — the security passes (`df0486f3` P-1b, `1e41350e`) added the hub/draft gates above and never touched the query params; (4) the codebase itself treats this exact class as a bug — `events/index.get.ts:29` guards `hubId` with `isUuid` under the comment "a non-uuid string reaching the bind throws an unhandled 500", and whitelists `status` through `PUBLIC_STATUSES`. The attendees route simply never got that treatment. Severity note: P2 is defensible but slightly generous. No real user path sends `status` (the only caller, `layers/base/pages/events/[slug]/index.vue:10`, fetches with no query), there is no data exposure, no privilege escalation, and no amplification — the connection survives, each crafted request just returns 500 and logs noise. It is an attacker-only robustness/validation defect, so P3 is arguable; I left the severity as filed since the repo's own precedent guards the identical class inline.

</details>

#### [P2] Nav item `route` has no scheme guard and renders as a raw href via NuxtLink's external branch

`layers/base/server/api/admin/navigation/items.put.ts:24`

`navHref` deliberately denies script-executing schemes on `href`, but the sibling `route` field is `z.string().max(255).optional()` with no scheme check — and `NavLink` feeds `route` to `<NuxtLink :to>`, which for any string carrying a protocol renders a plain `<a href=…>` with the value verbatim.

**Failure:** A holder of the granular `navigation.manage` permission (a grantable non-admin RBAC key, packages/schema/src/permissions.ts:58) PUTs `{ items: [{ id:'x', type:'link', label:'Docs', route:'javascript:fetch("/api/admin/users")…' }] }`. Zod accepts it. On every page, `NavLink` hits the `v-else-if="item.route"` branch; Nuxt 3.21's `useNuxtLink` sets `isAbsoluteUrl` via `hasProtocol` and returns `to.value` unchanged as `href` (nuxt-link.js:44-46, 68-72), so the header renders `<a href="javascript:…">` for every visitor including admins. CSP does not save this — `script-src` carries `'unsafe-inline'`, which permits `javascript:` URLs. Result: stored XSS in the global nav, escalating `navigation.manage` to full instance compromise.

**Suggested fix:** Constrain `route` to an internal path — e.g. `z.string().max(255).regex(/^\/[^\/\\]/).optional()` (plus allowing bare `/`) — so it can never carry a protocol, matching how `sectionConfigs.ts` guards CTA hrefs with `URL_LINK_STRICT`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the whole chain. (1) items.put.ts:24 `route: z.string().max(255).optional()` has no scheme guard while the sibling navHref (10-16) refines to http(s)/mailto/tel; git show 7242f24d shows the security pass hardened href only and left route untouched, so the comment's "internal destinations use route" is an unenforced assumption. (2) packages/server/src/navigation/navigation.ts setNavItems/getNavItems store and return the array verbatim, no sanitization. (3) layers/base/server/api/navigation/items.get.ts is a fully PUBLIC route (no guard) and layers/base/layouts/default.vue:47 fetches it for every visitor, feeding NavRenderer -> NavLink/NavDropdown/MobileNavRenderer. (4) NavLink.vue:26-27 binds `:to="item.route"`; in nuxt 3.21.2 nuxt-link.js isAbsoluteUrl = hasProtocol(path,{acceptRelative:true}) and ufo 1.6.3 PROTOCOL_REGEX /^[\s\w\0+.-]{2,}:([\/\\]{2})?/ matches `javascript:`, so `href` returns to.value unchanged and the render falls through to h("a",{href:href.value}) with onClick returning early for external — the raw javascript: URL lands in the DOM/SSR HTML. (5) packages/infra/src/security.ts:98 sets script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' in prod, which permits javascript: URLs. (6) The admin editor (pages/admin/navigation.vue:215,258) is a free-text input with no pattern. One correction to the reporter's scenario: navigation.manage is grantable to a non-admin custom role, but that grant only resolves when features.rbac is on — config/src/schema.ts:136 defaults rbac to false and rbac/resolver.ts:80 returns an empty set with the flag off, so on a default-config instance only an admin (admin floor in requirePermission) can perform the write. The stored XSS is real regardless; the non-admin privilege-escalation variant requires an operator who enabled rbac. Severity P2 stands: genuine stored-XSS sink on a path every visitor hits, but the write requires an admin-level permission on default configs.

</details>

#### [P3] /api/public/v1/metrics/timeseries accepts a shape-valid but non-existent date and throws RangeError

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/metrics/timeseries.get.ts:36`

`from`/`to` are validated only against `/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/`, so a syntactically well-formed but impossible date passes; the clamp arithmetic then calls `.toISOString()` on an Invalid Date.

**Failure:** A holder of any API key with `read:analytics` calls `GET /api/public/v1/metrics/timeseries?metric=users.total&to=2026-13-45`. The regex passes. Line 35's `from > to` string comparison does not trip (`'2026-08-20' < '2026-13-45'`). Line 36 evaluates `new Date('2026-13-45T00:00:00Z').getTime()` → NaN, then `new Date(NaN).toISOString()` → `RangeError: Invalid time value` → unhandled 500 rather than the 400 the route's own schema was meant to produce.

**Suggested fix:** Add `.refine((v) => Number.isFinite(new Date(`${v}T00:00:00Z`).getTime()))` to both date fields, or guard the clamp with a finite check before calling `toISOString`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code and executing the arithmetic. timeseries.get.ts:10-11 validates `to` with only /^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/ (shape, not domain). Line 35's `from > to` is a STRING compare, so it clamps a bad `from` but never fires for a bad `to`. Line 36 then evaluates new Date('2026-13-45T00:00:00Z').getTime() -> NaN and new Date(NaN).toISOString() -> RangeError (verified in node). No guard exists anywhere on the path: layers/base/server/middleware/public-api-auth.ts only does flag/key/rate-limit/CORS, layers/base/server/utils/requireScope.ts only checks the scope, and grep of layers/base/server/plugins/ shows no Nitro error hook remapping thrown errors, so this is an unhandled 500 rather than the 400 the schema promised. Not deliberate: the same regex is paired with an explicit NaN check elsewhere in this codebase (packages/server/src/persona/values.ts:356 and packages/server/src/contest/validation.ts:135); the route was added in fb560cf5 and never revisited. Reachable in production: `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns "publicApi":true and the endpoint answers 401 (mounted), not 404. A related path exists too: from='2026-00-00' passes the regex, does not trip the string clamp, and is greater than minFrom, so it reaches the SQL date bind in getMetricsTimeseries for a Postgres-side 500. Severity P3 is right: it needs an operator-issued API key with read:analytics, is per-key rate limited, leaks no data, and only a malformed client request triggers it.

</details>

#### [P3] Contest judge/stakeholder DELETE routes bind an unvalidated :userId to a uuid column

`layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/judges/[userId].delete.ts:22`

These two routes read `userId` with bare `getRouterParam` and check only for emptiness, bypassing `parseParams(event, { userId: 'uuid' })`, so a non-uuid path segment reaches a uuid equality bind.

**Failure:** A contest owner (or `contest.manage` holder) requests `DELETE /api/contests/<slug>/judges/not-a-uuid` — e.g. a UI that accidentally sends a username. The ownership check passes, then `removeContestJudge` runs `eq(contestJudges.userId, 'not-a-uuid')` (packages/server/src/contest/judges.ts:109) → `invalid input syntax for type uuid` → 500 instead of the 400 the shared helper would produce. `contests/[slug]/stakeholders/[userId].delete.ts:21` has the identical shape. Both are on the live-contest surface on deveco.io.

**Suggested fix:** Replace the manual `getRouterParam` pair with `const { slug, userId } = parseParams(event, { slug: 'string', userId: 'uuid' });` in both files.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code and verifying against the live dev DB. Both `layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/judges/[userId].delete.ts:12-13` and `stakeholders/[userId].delete.ts:12-13` read `userId` via bare `getRouterParam` with a truthiness-only check, then pass it to `removeContestJudge`/`removeContestStakeholder` (packages/server/src/contest/judges.ts:100-113, stakeholders.ts:120-133), which bind it with `eq(contestJudges.userId, userId)` against `userId: uuid('user_id')` (packages/schema/src/contest.ts:589, :615). I empirically confirmed the bind fails: `PREPARE t(uuid,uuid) ... EXECUTE t('00000000-...','not-a-uuid')` -> `ERROR: invalid input syntax for type uuid`, both literal and parameterized. No guard exists: I enumerated layers/base/server/plugins/ and found no error hook mapping 22P02 to a 4xx; no validator sits between route and query; the only test touching these paths (privileged-route-keys.test.ts) checks authz keys, not param shape. Not deliberate: these are the ONLY two routes under server/api/contests/ using bare getRouterParam for a uuid param (every sibling uses parseParams with 'uuid'), and layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts:12-14 documents this exact hazard in its own doc comment ("a non-uuid string reaching the bind throws an unhandled 500"). Git history shows both predate the parseParams migration (f700e635, 3d75d7c0). TWO CORRECTIONS to the finding: (1) the claimed trigger "a UI that accidentally sends a username" is not real -- ContestJudgeManager.vue:102 and ContestStakeholderManager.vue pass `judge.userId` sourced from the server's own listing, always a valid uuid; the only reachable trigger is a hand-crafted request. (2) `ownerOrPermission(event, contest.createdById, 'contest.manage')` runs BEFORE the bind, so anonymous/unprivileged callers get 403 first -- this is not an unauthenticated DoS or info-leak vector. Net: a contest owner or contest.manage holder sending a malformed path segment gets an unhandled 500 (DB error in logs) instead of the clean 400 parseParams would produce. Real defect, wrong status code, no security consequence, off the daily user path -- P3 stands.

</details>

#### [P3] Hub resource PUT echoes the raw internal error message in a 500 response

`layers/base/server/api/hubs/[slug]/resources/[id].put.ts:17`

The catch-all rethrows any non-matching error as `createError({ statusCode: 500, statusMessage: message })` where `message` is the raw `Error.message` — including Postgres driver text.

**Failure:** An authenticated hub member PUTs a resource update that trips a DB-level constraint or a driver error (e.g. a value too long for `varchar`, or a unique-index violation). Nitro's default handler masks *unhandled* errors to "Server Error", but this error is deliberately re-wrapped in `createError`, so it is not marked unhandled and the message is returned verbatim in both the status line and the JSON body — leaking table/constraint/column names to the client. The two branches above it are also substring-matched on error prose (`message.includes('not found')`), which silently reclassifies any future error whose text happens to contain those words.

**Suggested fix:** Log the real message server-side and return a fixed `statusMessage: 'Update failed'`; have `updateHubResource` throw typed errors (or return a discriminated result) instead of relying on message substrings.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading code, though the claim's named failure vectors are wrong and a different one is right. Mechanism verified end to end: 1. layers/base/server/api/hubs/[slug]/resources/[id].put.ts:17 rewraps the raw Error.message as statusMessage on a 500. requireAuth/parseParams/parseBody are all outside the try, so only updateHubResource errors reach the catch. 2. The masking defence does NOT apply. nitropack@2.13.2 dist/runtime/internal/error/prod.mjs defaultHandler: `const isSensitive = error.unhandled || error.fatal` gates ONLY `message` and `data`; `statusMessage` goes into the response body and setResponseStatus unconditionally. h3 1.15 createError never sets unhandled/fatal, so isSensitive is false and BOTH fields leak (createError sets err.message = input.message ?? input.statusMessage, so `message` carries the same raw string). 3. sanitizeStatusMessage (h3 dist/index.mjs:578) only strips control chars via .replace(DISALLOWED_STATUS_CHARS,"") — no truncation or redaction. 4. No override: no nitro errorHandler in layers/base/nuxt.config.ts, apps/reference/nuxt.config.ts, or layers/base/server/plugins/; nuxt@3.21.2 dist has no nitro error-handler module. 5. Zero tests reference this route/function. It is the only route under server/api/hubs/ using err.message; siblings [id].delete.ts and reorder.post.ts use the result-object pattern. Reachability of a raw driver error CONFIRMED via a vector the claimant did not name: updateHubResourceSchema = createHubResourceSchema.partial(), and `sortOrder: z.number().int().min(0).optional()` has NO upper bound, while hub_resources.sort_order is `integer()` (int4, max 2147483647) at packages/schema/src/hub.ts:269. An authenticated author/mod PUTs {"sortOrder": 3000000000}, passes zod, and gets the node-postgres DatabaseError text echoed back. Also reachable: PG `permission denied for table hub_resources` fails the `.includes('permissions')` test (singular vs plural) and falls through to the 500, leaking the table name; connection failures leak internal host/port/user. The claim's two stated vectors are BOTH unreachable: (a) varchar overflow — title is varchar(255) against z.string().max(255) (JS length <= 255 implies <= 255 code points), and url/description are unbounded `text` columns against max 2048/2000; (b) unique-index violation — hub_resources has only two plain indexes plus a PK on a server-generated defaultRandom() uuid the update never sets. Severity P3 is correct: authentication plus hub authorship/mod required; information disclosure only, no authz or data impact; the most reachable message ("value ... is out of range for type integer") is near-worthless; and HubResources.vue only calls POST and DELETE — the PUT has no first-party caller anywhere in the repo, so it is not a daily user path. Higher-value leaks require a concurrent DB misconfiguration or outage.

</details>

#### [P3] /api/notifications?type= binds an arbitrary 64-char string to the notification_type enum

`layers/base/server/api/notifications/index.get.ts:6`

`type` is validated as `z.string().max(64)` and then cast to `NotificationType`, reaching `eq(notifications.type, filters.type)` against a Postgres enum column.

**Failure:** Any authenticated user issues `GET /api/notifications?type=zzz`. `listNotifications` pushes `eq(notifications.type, 'zzz')` (packages/server/src/notification/notification.ts:55) → `invalid input value for enum notification_type: "zzz"` → unhandled 500 instead of a 400. Blast radius is narrower than the public routes (auth required, own rows only), but it is the same unhandled-bind class and the enum vocabulary is already enumerated one import away.

**Suggested fix:** Use `z.enum(notificationTypeEnum.enumValues)` (or the exported `NotificationType` union) in `notificationsQuerySchema` so an unknown type is a clean 400.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the full call path and reproducing the error against the live dev database. (1) layers/base/server/api/notifications/index.get.ts:6 declares `type: z.string().max(64).optional()` with no enum constraint, and line 20 casts it unchecked as `query.type as NotificationType | undefined`. (2) packages/server/src/notification/notification.ts:53-57 pushes `eq(notifications.type, filters.type)` with no guard. (3) packages/schema/src/social.ts:106 is `notificationTypeEnum('type').notNull()` — a genuine pgEnum, not text. Guards searched for and absent: parseQueryParams (layers/base/server/utils/validate.ts:82-93) only runs the already-permissive Zod schema; no feature flag on the route; no Nitro error hook in layers/base/server/plugins/ or middleware/; utils/errors.ts holds passive helpers unused here; no try/catch; no test anywhere references notificationsQuerySchema. Empirical proof: db.ts:2 uses drizzle-orm/node-postgres, which sends parameters untyped. My first psql probe used an explicit `text` param and produced a misleading "operator does not exist" error; re-running with an untyped $1 (`PREPARE p2 AS SELECT count(*) FROM notifications WHERE type = $1; EXECUTE p2('zzz')`) yielded exactly `ERROR: invalid input value for enum notification_type: "zzz"` — the claimed message verbatim. Not deliberate: git log -S dates line 6 to the bulk layer commit 00790b98, and the codebase's own established pattern for enum-backed query filters is z.enum([...]) — see layers/base/server/api/admin/reports.get.ts:6 for report_status. SEVERITY CORRECTED P2 -> P3. requireAuth gates the route (line 13) and `eq(notifications.userId, user.id)` is unconditionally the first condition, so there is no cross-user exposure and no way to widen the row set. Impact is strictly a 500 where a 400 belongs, on the caller's own request: no data leak, no corruption, no process crash. It is also unreachable from the UI — layers/base/pages/notifications.vue:6-11 maps a fixed tab list to only like/comment/follow/system — so it requires a hand-crafted request and no real user hits it daily. The residual argument for P2 is that any authenticated user can trivially spray 500s into error budgets and alerting, but that is log noise rather than user harm. Additional drift worth fixing alongside: NotificationType at notification.ts:21 omits 'event', which IS present in the pgEnum (enums.ts:67-79). The hand-written TS union has already diverged from the DB, so the fix should derive the validator from notificationTypeEnum.enumValues rather than hand-writing a third copy of the vocabulary.

</details>

### Configuration, feature flags and admin surfaces

#### [P2] instance.maxUploadSize is dead in both config file and DB setting; validateUpload uses a hardcoded table

`packages/infra/src/storage.ts:422`

`validateUpload()` resolves the size cap purely from the module-level `MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES` map keyed by purpose, and never consults `config.instance.maxUploadSize`. That config key is validated by Zod, defaulted to 10MB, surfaced as an editable admin setting labelled "Max Upload Size (MB)", and consumed by nothing.

**Failure:** An operator on a small VPS sets `instance.maxUploadSize: 2 * 1024 * 1024` in commonpub.config.ts and, seeing no effect, also sets `instance.maxUploadSize` to `2` via /admin/settings. Both are ignored: a member still uploads a 100MB `attachment`-purpose file (MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES.attachment) and fills the disk. Conversely an operator who wants to raise the cap cannot. The admin field also displays the byte value `10485760` under a label that says megabytes, so any value typed there is unit-ambiguous even if it were read.

**Suggested fix:** Thread the effective cap into `validateUpload(mimeType, sizeBytes, purpose, maxOverride?)` from the calling Nitro route (`min(MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES[purpose], effectiveMaxUploadSize)`), reading the DB setting with a config fallback the way `getEffectiveTermsVersion` does — and fix the MB-vs-bytes unit on the admin row.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/infra/src/storage.ts:413-428 defines validateUpload(mimeType, sizeBytes, purpose) with no config parameter, and :422 resolves the cap solely from the module-level MAX_UPLOAD_SIZES const record. The sole multipart upload route (layers/base/server/api/files/upload.post.ts — the only file besides server/utils/validate.ts that calls readMultipartFormData) calls validateUpload directly, never calls useConfig(), and adds only a hardcoded MAX_UPLOAD_BODY_BYTES = 128MB Content-Length pre-check; purpose 'attachment' (100MB) is reachable by any authenticated user with no feature flag (only 'contest' is gated by requireFeature('contestPrivateFiles')). packages/server/src/storage.ts is a pure re-export, so no wrapper injects config. Repo-wide grep for maxUploadSize outside node_modules/dist yields only write/declare sites: packages/config/src/schema.ts:253 (Zod .default(10*1024*1024)), types.ts:314, config.ts:26, test-utils/mockConfig.ts, two test fixtures, layers/base/server/api/admin/settings.get.ts:21 (display echo only) and layers/base/pages/admin/settings.vue:42 (label 'Max Upload Size (MB)'). No read site anywhere. The DB path is dead too: settings.put.ts stores the key but only theme.default / instance.name / instance.description invalidate any cache, so the admin edit returns 200 and is never consulted. No upstream guard, no off-by-default flag, no test pins the correct behaviour — instead packages/infra/src/__tests__/storage.test.ts:134-149 pins the hardcoded table (attachment = 100MB), locking the defect in. git log -S maxUploadSize shows the key present since the earliest config/layer commits with a consumer never added, so this is an omission rather than a deliberate deprecation. Severity stays P2: not higher because the hardcoded caps plus the 128MB body guard do enforce a real bound (no unbounded-upload security hole), and not lower because an operator cannot tune the cap in either supported surface and the admin field displays a byte value (10485760) under a megabytes label while silently discarding any value saved.

</details>

#### [P2] config.instance.domain is unreachable dead code — the layer's non-empty public.domain default always wins

`apps/reference/server/utils/config.ts:55`

`getBaseConfig()` resolves the instance domain as `runtimeConfig.public.domain || config.instance.domain`, but `layers/base/nuxt.config.ts` declares `public.domain: 'localhost:3000'` — a non-empty string — so the `||` fallback can never fire and the value an operator writes in `commonpub.config.ts` is silently ignored. Neither `apps/reference/nuxt.config.ts` nor the scaffolder's generated `nuxt.config.ts` wires `public.domain` from the config file (both DO wire `siteName`/`siteDescription`).

**Failure:** An operator moves their instance to a new domain and updates the required `instance.domain: 'makers.example'` in commonpub.config.ts (the documented source of truth — it is `z.string().min(1)`, so it looks load-bearing) but does not also change `NUXT_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` in the container env. Boot succeeds, config validation passes, no warning is emitted. Every federation-visible string is then built from `localhost:3000` (or the stale old host): `content-ap.ts:34` and `hub-ap.ts:29` mint AP object/actor ids, `registry-heartbeat.ts:30` signs the heartbeat with `https://localhost:3000/actor#main-key`, `public/v1/openapi.json.get.ts:21` publishes `https://localhost:3000/api/public/v1`, and `auth/federated/login.post.ts:41` builds an OAuth redirect_uri on the wrong host. Remote instances persist those unresolvable actor ids permanently.

**Suggested fix:** Set `domain: siteConfig.config.instance.domain` and `siteUrl` from it in the app's `runtimeConfig.public` (as siteName already is), so the config file is the default and the env var is the override. Add a boot assertion that logs loudly when `NUXT_PUBLIC_DOMAIN` and `config.instance.domain` disagree.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. apps/reference/server/utils/config.ts:55 is the ONLY useConfig() implementation (layers/base/server/utils/ has no config.ts, so every layer route resolves through the app util), and it does `runtimeConfig.public.domain || config.instance.domain`. layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:91 declares `domain: 'localhost:3000'` in runtimeConfig.public, and apps/reference/nuxt.config.ts wires siteName/siteDescription/instanceCookies/requireEmailVerification/analytics/cookiePolicyVersion from siteConfig but never domain or siteUrl — so merged public.domain is always a non-empty string unless NUXT_PUBLIC_DOMAIN is set, making the right-hand fallback reachable only by explicitly setting that env var to an empty string. No guard exists: plugins/identity-startup.ts asserts only features.identity.*/CPUB_FED_TOKEN_KEY, nothing validates domain against the serving host or rejects a localhost domain in production, and no test covers the resolution (the instance.domain tests are in packages/config, packages/schema and packages/test-utils, exercising the factory/validator, not the runtime merge). Consumers use the resolved value directly: routes/actor.ts calls buildInstanceActor(config.instance.domain, …), plus content-ap.ts:34, hub-ap.ts:29, registry-heartbeat.ts:30, public/v1/openapi.json.get.ts:21, auth/federated/login.post.ts:41. Failure is silent and asymmetric because routes/.well-known/webfinger.ts derives everything from getRequestURL(event).host, so discovery keeps working at the real host while AP ids carry localhost:3000. Docs make it reachable: docs/building-with-commonpub.md:129 instructs operators to set instance.domain in commonpub.config.ts, docs/deployment.md's env reference documents INSTANCE_DOMAIN/INSTANCE_NAME/INSTANCE_DESCRIPTION (read by no .ts/.vue anywhere) and never mentions NUXT_PUBLIC_DOMAIN, and both docker run examples pass only NUXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL. Two corrections to the finding: (1) the siteUrl half is inert by design — NUXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL is documented required and set in every path (app-spec.yaml:34, do-one-click.sh:241, .env.prod.example:9, scaffolder, both docker examples); (2) severity is P2, not P1, because every automated install path DOES set NUXT_PUBLIC_DOMAIN (app-spec.yaml:28, do-one-click.sh:242, .env.prod.example:42, template.rs:479/546/625), so no live instance is currently mis-minting ids, and the blast radius is the undocumented standalone-docker path plus later-edit drift, gated behind the per-instance features.federation flag.

</details>

#### [P2] create-commonpub pins are 30 layer minors stale, and the loose server caret splits @commonpub/server in two

`tools/create-commonpub/src/template.rs:47`

The scaffolder's version constants were last synced at session 196 (2026-06-10) and now trail the published packages badly. Worse, the four pins are inconsistent: layer/config/schema use 0.x carets (which cannot cross a minor) while `@commonpub/server` uses `^2.113.0`, which floats across every 2.x minor — the exact hoisting hazard the comment block above it warns about.

**Failure:** A new operator runs `create-commonpub` today. package.json gets `@commonpub/layer: ^0.106.0` → resolves 0.106.x, not the current 0.136.1 (30 minors, ~2 months of fixes missing, including the whole persona/data-sharing feature set and the 0.136.1 roll). `@commonpub/schema: ^0.59.0` → the drizzle migrate step (`./node_modules/@commonpub/schema/migrations`) ships migrations only through 0.59, so 0046/0047/0048 never apply. Meanwhile `@commonpub/server: ^2.113.0` resolves to 2.132.1, while layer 0.106.0's own `workspace:*` publishes as an EXACT pin on server 2.113.x — so pnpm installs two copies of @commonpub/server and the layer's subpath imports resolve against the older nested one.

**Suggested fix:** Bump the four constants to the current published versions and pin `@commonpub/server` the same tight way (`~2.132.0` or an exact pin) so it cannot float ahead of the layer's own exact dependency. Add a CI check that fails the release when the constants diverge from the workspace package versions.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED by reading the code, with the second half of the title overstated. tools/create-commonpub/src/template.rs:47-50 holds ^0.33.0/^0.106.0/^0.59.0/^2.113.0 against live workspace versions config 0.39.1 / layer 0.136.1 / schema 0.65.0 / server 2.132.1 — 30 layer minors of drift. No guard exists anywhere: tools/create-commonpub/tests/cli.rs::package_json_has_correct_dependencies asserts only that the package NAMES appear (pkg.contains("@commonpub/layer")) and never checks a version, so nothing can fail on drift; there is no build-time derivation from the workspace manifests. The migration half checks out factually: packages/schema/migrations/ runs to 0048, release commit 4aba0443 shows schema 0.59 shipped through mig 0042, and ^0.59.0 cannot cross a 0.x minor, so the scaffold's ./node_modules/@commonpub/schema/migrations really does stop at 0042. The escape hatch is closed too — the generated README (template.rs:882) says `pnpm update @commonpub/layer`, which also cannot cross a 0.x minor, so the operator is stuck on July code without hand-editing package.json. Independently corroborated at docs/STATUS.md:146 as a known-open item ("pins are ~22 minors stale"), now grown to 30. CORRECTIONS to the finding: (1) The header comment "Last synced: 2026-06-10 (session 196) — layer 0.73.0" is itself stale; the constants were actually last synced in e8c5ec2a on 2026-07-15, so the drift is ~5 weeks, not ~2 months. (2) The "splits @commonpub/server in two" mechanism is misdescribed. layers/base/package.json:36-47 uses workspace:* for every dep, and per the verified exact-pin behaviour that publishes as an EXACT pin, so layer 0.106.0 pins server exactly 2.113.0. Two copies do land on disk, but the layer resolving its nested 2.113.0 is CORRECT — that is what it was built against — and exact pins already prevent the hoisting hazard the template.rs:45 comment warns about (pnpm hoisting an OLDER server so subpath imports go undefined). grep of template.rs shows generated code imports only @commonpub/config and reaches @commonpub/schema by explicit path; nothing generated imports @commonpub/server, so the floated root 2.132.1 is wasted install weight, not a resolution break. (3) "Migrations 0046/0047/0048 never apply" is true but not a failure: the layer pins schema 0.59.x as well, so the stack is internally consistent — the instance is old, not broken. Net: a real, unguarded config-admin defect on the documented primary onboarding path (cargo install create-commonpub, docs/STATUS.md:848) that yields a working but 5-week-stale instance an operator cannot update without manual edits. P2 is the right severity — real user path, no breakage or security impact.

</details>

#### [P2] 15 of 46 flags on /admin/features render as a raw camelCase key with an empty description

`layers/base/pages/admin/features.vue:20`

`flagMeta` covers 31 of the 46 boolean flags. The template falls back to `flagMeta[key]?.label ?? key` and `flagMeta[key]?.description ?? ''`, so the uncovered flags show the identifier and a blank line — including several with real security, privacy and delivery consequences.

**Failure:** An admin scrolls /admin/features and sees an unlabeled row reading `publicApi` with no description, next to `emailUnverified`, `rbac`, `contestPrivateFiles`, `analytics` and `announceToRegistry`, all equally blank. They toggle `emailUnverified` on to "fix" bounced mail without any indication it starts sending to unconfirmed addresses (the sender-reputation caveat documented in packages/config/src/schema.ts), or toggle `announceToRegistry` off/on with no idea it controls discovery heartbeats to commonpub.io. The toggle applies immediately via the DB override.

**Suggested fix:** Add metadata for the 15 missing flags, and add a unit test asserting `Object.keys(flagMeta)` covers every boolean key of `featureFlagsSchema.parse({})` — reading the schema from source, not dist (see the parity-guard finding).

'' }}` (line 157). (3) No downstream guard: grep shows flagMeta is referenced ONLY inside features.vue; no test pins coverage, and its type is Record<string,...> rather than Record<keyof FeatureFlags,...>, so adding a flag can never fail typecheck. (4) The 'it is deliberate' defense is refuted by git history: commit 5ccc575e ('polish(admin): label featuredHub + hubGovernance flags in /admin/features') states 'Both new flags were rendering as raw camelCase keys (no label/description/icon). Add flagMeta entries so operators see proper names + descriptions.' The maintainer treated this exact condition as a bug and fixed it one-off for two flags without adding an exhaustiveness guard, so the fallback is a stopgap and the gap regrew to 15. (5) Supporting details verified: the emailUnverified schema comment does carry the 'can hurt sender reputation' caveat, and announceToRegistry defaults true and controls discovery heartbeats to federation.registryUrl — neither reaches the UI. Severity P2 is correct and I would not raise it: the toggles still apply the right value and the PUT validator is sound, so this is an operator-comprehension/UX defect rather than a correctness or security bypass, and it is reachable only by a settings.manage holder. #### [P2] The Persona flag's admin description states the opposite of the actual privacy default

`layers/base/pages/admin/features.vue:48`

The /admin/features copy for `persona` says "Answers show on their public profile unless you turn a question off." The implemented default is the reverse: an answer reaches the public profile only where the operator explicitly sets `showOnProfile: true`, and no built-in field does. The copy predates the session-255 privacy-model correction.

**Failure:** An operator evaluating whether to enable Persona reads the admin description, concludes that switching it on will publish every member's answers (industry, tech stack, what they're building) to public profiles, and either refuses a feature that is in fact private-by-default, or enables it and then writes a privacy notice to members describing a public disclosure that is not happening. Either way the one screen where the decision is made misdescribes what personal data leaves the instance — the exact question a GDPR-relevant toggle has to answer correctly.

**Suggested fix:** Rewrite to match the implementation: "Optional profile questions people answer about themselves. Answers stay private unless you mark a question as shown on profile. Sections are defined in commonpub.config.ts or at /admin/persona."

The implemented rule is the exact inverse: layers/base/server/api/users/[username]/persona.get.ts:257-259 skips every field with `showOnProfile !== true` under a comment labelled "THE INVERSION (R3.1 D1)"; packages/persona/src/schemas.ts:92 declares `showOnProfile: z.boolean().optional()` with no default ("Opt IN, absent means not shown"); packages/persona/src/persona.ts:478 states "NOT ONE FIELD HERE SETS showOnProfile: true, and that is the point", pinned by packages/persona/src/__tests__/persona.test.ts:247 (`expect(f.showOnProfile).toBeUndefined()` for every built-in field); packages/config/src/types.ts:219-221 and packages/config/src/schema.ts:174-176 both document "an answer appears on the public profile only where the operator set showOnProfile: true ... and no built-in field does". The /admin/persona field editor (layers/base/pages/admin/persona.vue:1554-1556) is likewise an opt-in checkbox that patches to `undefined` when cleared, not the "turn a question off" the copy implies. No guard rescues it: the description is a static literal in flagMeta, nothing derives it from the persona registry, and grep finds no test asserting anything about flagMeta copy. Git history shows it is stale rather than deliberate: the line came in with b4ed0a1c (persona PR #79, 2026-08-14) and the privacy-model correction be8bede7 ("feat(persona)!: questions are private, statistics are not a consent", #85) rewrote the config docblocks, /settings/privacy, /privacy, PersonaPublicDisplay.vue and purposes.ts but never touched features.vue — `git log -- layers/base/pages/admin/features.vue` still ends at b4ed0a1c. The only shred of truth is that `link` fields render in the profile hero regardless (persona.get.ts:253-254), but those are pre-existing users.social_links, not persona answers. Severity kept at P2 but at its soft end: it is admin-only copy on an infrequently visited screen and the error direction is fail-safe (it over-states publicity, so an operator acting on it under-enables rather than over-exposes), yet it states the exact inverse of the shipped model on the one screen where a GDPR-relevant collection toggle is decided. Fix is a one-line copy change to match the types.ts wording.
