#### [P3] defineCommonPubConfig computes operator warnings that nothing ever reads

`packages/config/src/config.ts:92`

`defineCommonPubConfig` builds a `warnings: ConfigWarning[]` array for three real misconfigurations and returns it in `ConfigResult`, but `.warnings` is never accessed anywhere in the monorepo — not logged at boot, not surfaced in the admin panel, not asserted in a startup check.

**Failure:** An operator enables `features.federation: true` without any `auth.trustedInstances`. `defineCommonPubConfig` produces the warning "Federation is enabled but no trusted instances are configured. AP Actor SSO (Model B) requires at least one trusted instance." and it is discarded on the spot. Boot is silent, the admin panel says nothing, and the operator discovers the gap only when federated sign-in fails. Same for the `auth.sharedAuthDb` database-coupling warning and the learning-without-explainers warning.

**Suggested fix:** In the Nitro startup plugin (or `getBaseConfig()`), iterate `siteConfig.warnings` and `console.warn` each one once at boot, and optionally expose them on `/api/admin/features` so /admin/features can show a banner.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

CONFIRMED on the facts, with caveats that weaken the stated failure scenario but do not eliminate the defect. What I verified by reading code: - packages/config/src/config.ts:63-92 builds `warnings: ConfigWarning[]` for exactly the three conditions described (auth.sharedAuthDb, federation-without-trustedInstances, learning-without-explainers) and returns `{ config, warnings }`. - A repo-wide grep for `defineCommonPubConfig|ConfigResult|ConfigWarning|warnings` (excluding node_modules/dist/.nuxt/.output) returns only: config.ts itself, packages/config/src/__tests__/config.test.ts:239-277 (which pins that the warnings are *produced*, never that anything consumes them), and a comment at layers/base/server/api/admin/__tests__/features-override-cap.test.ts:20. There is no runtime consumer. - apps/reference/server/utils/config.ts:53 is `const { config } = siteConfig;` — warnings dropped at the single startup choke point. No boot log, no admin endpoint, no startup assertion. - The condition is live in-repo: apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts sets `federation: true` with an `auth` block containing no `trustedInstances`, so it trips the federation warning today and it is discarded. (apps/reference declares `trustedInstances: ['deveco.io']`, so it does not.) - No upstream guard makes it unreachable: configSchema (packages/config/src/schema.ts:245) makes `trustedInstances` merely `.optional()` and does not reject the combination; `features.federation` is on in both in-repo app configs, so this is not a flag-off-everywhere path. Caveats found while trying to refute (they cap severity, and matter if someone "fixes" this by naively logging the array): 1. The federation warning would be a FALSE POSITIVE on a correctly configured instance. packages/server/src/federation/oauth.ts:432-488 stores trusted instances in the DB (`getStoredTrustedInstances`/`addTrustedInstance`), and `isDomainTrusted` accepts either source. An operator who registers peers at /admin/federation has an empty `config.auth.trustedInstances` and a fully working SSO path. 2. The warning cannot reflect runtime state anyway. apps/reference/server/utils/config.ts merges FEATURE_* env vars and DB `features.overrides` on top of the file, so federation can be enabled without the file ever tripping the check — the array is computed once against the file literal at module evaluation. 3. The operator is not entirely blind: layers/base/server/api/admin/federation/trusted-instances.get.ts returns `{ configDomains, storedDomains }` and layers/base/pages/admin/federation.vue renders it, so the empty list is visible in the admin panel — just not flagged as a warning. 4. This is a documented public API of a published package: packages/config/README.md has a "## Warnings" section and its usage example destructures `warnings`, so a downstream thin app could legitimately consume it. "Unused in this monorepo" is weaker than "dead code" for a library export — though the repo's own two apps both ignore it, and the README example never uses the value it destructures either. Net: the code path exists, is reachable, and the diagnostic is genuinely discarded. It is an unwired-diagnostic / dead-return-value issue, not a correctness or security bug. Severity P3 is correct and if anything generous.

</details>

### EMAIL, NOTIFICATIONS AND SCHEDULED WORK

#### [P2] Digest claims the day BEFORE sending, so one failure loses every digest for that date

`layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts:134`

The cross-replica claim row in digest_runs is committed before any message is built or enqueued, and the whole fan-out is one unbounded INSERT; any failure after the claim is swallowed by the outer catch and the day can never be retried by any replica.

**Failure:** At 08:00 UTC the winning replica inserts digest_runs('2026-08-20') and proceeds. It builds one OutboxMessage per digest user in memory (each carrying full rendered HTML) and issues a single `enqueueEmails(db, messages)` at line 200. With a few thousand digest users this is one multi-megabyte INSERT; if it exceeds a statement/packet limit, or the pod is restarted mid-loop (deploy, OOM kill), or any single listNotifications query errors, control lands in the catch at line 203 which only console.errors. digest_runs already holds the row, so every replica's next tick returns at line 134 (`claimed.length === 0`) and the in-process `lastDigestDate` guard short-circuits too - nobody's daily/weekly digest goes out that day, with no retry and no alert. Separately the hourly interval is phase-locked to process start, so a restart at 08:05 pushes the first tick to 09:05 and hour===8 is never observed that day (line 125 `if (!isDigestHour) return`), losing the digest for a different reason.

**Suggested fix:** Mirror the reminder sweep: run the claim and the enqueue in one transaction, or move the digest_runs write to AFTER a successful enqueue with a reclaim window. Chunk enqueueEmails at 500 like broadcast. Widen the trigger to `hour >= 8` (the digest_runs claim already guarantees once-per-day) so a restart inside hour 8 doesn't skip the day.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading /Users/obsidian/Projects/ossuary-projects/commonpub/layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts in full. (1) Claim-before-work is real. Lines 127-134: the `digest_runs` row for `todayKey` is inserted and COMMITTED (no surrounding transaction — `db` here is the plain `useDB()` handle), then `lastDigestDate = todayKey` is set unconditionally, before a single recipient is queried. All the actual work (lines 136-200) happens after the claim: the `users` scan, one `listNotifications` round-trip per digest user, template rendering, and finally one `await enqueueEmails(db, messages)`. (2) The failure is swallowed and unrecoverable. The only error handling is the catch at 204-206, which does `console.error` and returns — no rethrow, no alert, no compensating delete. I grepped the whole repo for `digestRuns|digest_runs`: the only writes are this INSERT (schema def at packages/schema/src/social.ts:138 and a concurrency test). Nothing ever DELETEs a digest_runs row, so once the row is committed the date is burned for every replica forever, and the in-process `lastDigestDate` guard at line 124 short-circuits the winning replica too. (3) The unbounded insert is real. packages/server/src/comms/outbox.ts:45 `enqueueEmails` is a single `db.insert(emailOutbox).values(messages.map(toRow))` with no chunking — ~8 bind params per row means the pg extended-protocol 65535-parameter ceiling is hit around ~8k recipients, and each row carries full rendered HTML. Simpler triggers are far more likely: one `listNotifications` throw mid-loop, or a pod restart during a multi-thousand-user sequential loop (deploy/OOM), both of which land in the same catch with the claim already committed. (4) The codebase's own contrast confirms this is an oversight, not a deliberate design. packages/server/src/comms/broadcast.ts:57,91-92 chunks the identical `enqueueEmails` call at `ENQUEUE_CHUNK = 500`. packages/server/src/contest/reminders.ts:187-190 wraps claim + `enqueueEmails` in ONE `db.transaction` with a comment naming exactly this bug: "if enqueueEmails throws, the ledger claim rolls back so a later sweep retries the milestone, instead of committing the claim and permanently dropping the whole batch's reminder (the documented exactly-once silently degrading to at-most-once/zero)." The digest path has neither the transaction nor the chunking. Git history (593e142a "atomic cross-replica claims for digest + hub-sync workers") shows the claim was added for multi-replica dedupe; crash-safety was never considered. (5) No guard makes it unreachable. `process.env.NODE_ENV === 'test'` early-return (line 27) only affects tests. The `features.emailNotifications` flag defaults to false in packages/config/src/schema.ts:99, but I verified empirically: `curl https://deveco.io/api/features` returns `"emailNotifications":true` (and `"emailUnverified":true`, which widens the fan-out to unverified addresses), so the scheduler is live on a real instance today. No test pins crash-safety — packages/server/src/__tests__/concurrency.integration.test.ts:162 only pins the exactly-one-winner property, i.e. the very behaviour that makes the loss permanent. (6) The secondary phase-lock claim also holds: the interval is started 5s after boot (line 31/90) and fires every 3600s, so tick minutes are pinned to process start; a restart at 08:05 means `hour === 8` is never observed that calendar day and line 121 returns every tick. It self-heals the next day (the 08:05 tick then sees hour 8), so it costs one day rather than being permanent — a real but lesser gap than the burned claim. Severity P2 is right: silent, no-alert loss of one day's daily digests (and a whole week's weekly digests if it lands on a Monday) on a live-flag instance, with no data loss, corruption, or security impact, and no operator-visible signal beyond a console line.

</details>

#### [P2] Contest email-test mails any user by id, ignoring global unsubscribe and unverified address

`layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/email-test.post.ts:41`

The test-send resolves an arbitrary toUserId to that user's address and sends directly through the adapter without consulting getNotificationEmailTarget, so unsubscribedAll and the emailVerified gate are both bypassed, and the recipient need not be connected to the contest at all.

**Failure:** User V clicked the one-click List-Unsubscribe in an earlier mail, so users.emailNotifications.unsubscribedAll === true (unsubscribe.post.ts sets it). A contest organizer (contest creator, any user added via isContestEditor, or any member on an instance configured `instance.contestCreation: 'open'`) POSTs /api/contests/<slug>/email-test with `{ template:'reminder', copy:{ subject:'...', bodyBlocks:[...] }, toUserId:'<V uuid>' }`. The handler looks V up by id filtered only on `isNull(users.deletedAt)` (lines 38-40), then calls `adapter.send({ to: V.email, ... })` at line 100 - no getNotificationEmailTarget, no unsubscribedAll check, no emailVerified check, no requireFeature('emailNotifications'). V receives organizer-composed mail after having globally unsubscribed. The route sits in the generic /api/* tier (60/min), so it is also a 60-msg/min outbound sender for organizer-chosen text from the instance's sending domain.

**Suggested fix:** For the toUserId branch, route through getNotificationEmailTarget(db, toUserId, config.features.emailUnverified) and 4xx when it returns null; restrict toUserId to the contest's own organizers/editors; add requireFeature('emailNotifications'); and apply a tight dedicated rate-limit tier as resend-verification.post.ts:34 already does.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. `layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/email-test.post.ts` gates only on `requireFeature('contests')`, `requireFeature('contestEmailEditor')`, `requireAuth`, and an organizer check (`ownerOrPermission(event, contest.createdById, 'contest.manage') || isContestEditor(...)`). For the `toUserId` branch it selects `{ email, username }` from `users` filtered ONLY by `eq(users.id, toUserId)` and `isNull(users.deletedAt)` (lines 35-42) and then calls `useEmailAdapter().send({ to: recipientEmail, ... })` at lines 100-106. It never imports or calls `getNotificationEmailTarget`, and there is no `requireFeature('emailNotifications')`. No upstream/downstream guard makes this impossible: (a) `getNotificationEmailTarget` (packages/server/src/notification/emailPrefs.ts:68-90) is the only place `emailVerified` / `emailNotifications.unsubscribedAll` are enforced, and its own doc comment claims to be "the single mailability gate ... so every caller ... honors it by default" — the other contest send paths do call it (`packages/server/src/contest/registrations.ts:96`, `layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts:59`); this route is the outlier. (b) The adapters (`packages/infra/src/email/adapters.ts`) carry no suppression list — `send()` goes straight to nodemailer/Resend. (c) The schema `contestEmailTestSchema` (packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:105-115) only checks `uuid()` and exactly-one-recipient; it does not scope the user to the contest. (d) The only route test, `layers/base/server/api/__tests__/contest-email-preview-route.test.ts`, covers `email-preview.post.ts` only — no test pins email-test behaviour at all. Reachability is real, not theoretical: `contestEmailEditor` defaults false in `packages/config/src/schema.ts:66`, but `docs/STATUS.md:71-72` records that deveco.io runs with `emailNotifications`/`emailUnverified`/`contestReminders`/`contestEmailEditor` all ON (commonpub.io has the editor OFF). The recipient need not be contest-connected: the picker route `user-search.get.ts` calls `searchUsers()` (packages/server/src/profile/profile.ts:21-33), an instance-wide ilike over all non-deleted users, and the API accepts any uuid regardless of the picker. `instance.contestCreation` does default to `'admin'` (packages/config/src/schema.ts:261), so on a default instance the caller must be staff/admin or an added editor; the `'open'` variant in the scenario is a supported setting, not the default. Rate tier is confirmed: `/api/*` falls to `DEFAULT_TIERS.api = { limit: 60, windowMs: 60_000 }` (packages/infra/src/security.ts:252, 274-275). Git history shows the route was added deliberately (864bb63f "send-a-test-email in the contest email editor") and this exact gap was already logged as an open item: `docs/reviews/production-readiness-audit-2026-07-23.md:87` — "Lock down `email-test.post.ts:92` (admin-only or own verified address, route through throttled outbox)." Known-but-unfixed, not intentional-and-safe. One correction/addition to the finding: the route's own doc comment says for a userId "the server resolves that user's email; a client-supplied address is never trusted" and implies it is "never exposed", but line 108 returns `{ sent: true, to: recipientEmail }` and `ContestEmailEditor.vue:200` toasts it — so any contest organizer can also read any instance user's email address via this endpoint. That strengthens rather than weakens the finding. Severity: P2 is right. It needs an authenticated privileged caller plus a non-default flag, so not P1; but it is live on a production instance today, bypasses a hard one-click-unsubscribe opt-out (compliance + sender-reputation risk) and leaks addresses, so not P3.

</details>

#### [P2] Soft-deleted and suspended accounts keep receiving digest, instant and reminder email

`packages/server/src/notification/emailPrefs.ts:88`

getNotificationEmailTarget, the digest recipient query, and the contest-reminder claim SQL all gate on emailVerified + unsubscribedAll but none filters users.status / users.deletedAt - while sendBroadcast does, and its comment claims that is the shared definition.

**Failure:** An admin sets a user to status='deleted' via updateUserStatus (packages/server/src/admin/admin.ts:409), which stamps deletedAt and kills sessions but leaves users.email and users.emailNotifications intact. (a) That user has 3 unread notifications and digest:'daily'; the 08:00 UTC sweep selects them at notification-email.ts:136 (`where isNotNull(emailNotifications)` plus optional emailVerified only) and mails them a digest every day forever. (b) If they were registered for an active contest, the reminder claim at reminders.ts:196-206 joins users with only `u.email_verified = true` and the unsubscribedAll check, so they get every 7d/48h/24h/1h reminder. (c) A suspended account likewise keeps receiving instant notification mail via getNotificationEmailTarget. A user who asked to be deleted continues to be mailed by the instance - the exact outcome the soft-delete is supposed to prevent.

**Suggested fix:** Extract the mailability predicate broadcast.ts:audienceWhere already encodes into one shared helper in comms/, and use it in getNotificationEmailTarget, the digest query, and the reminder claim SQL.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. emailPrefs.ts:68-92 (getNotificationEmailTarget) selects only email/username/emailVerified/emailNotifications keyed on users.id with no status or deletedAt predicate; notification-email.ts:145-149 gates the digest sweep on only isNotNull(emailNotifications) plus the flag-conditional emailVerified; reminders.ts:199-206 joins users with only the flag-conditional u.email_verified and the unsubscribedAll check. broadcast.ts:29-36 is the only email path that adds eq(users.status,'active') + isNull(users.deletedAt), and git log -L on that hunk shows the guard was added in ed8f6847 (2026-07-17 audit batch) with a comment assuming no soft-delete path existed yet, while admin.ts:388-427 updateUserStatus already stamps deletedAt and leaves email + emailNotifications intact. The path is reachable: layers/base/server/api/admin/users/[id]/status.put.ts accepts adminUpdateStatusSchema z.enum(['active','suspended','deleted']) (validators/admin.ts:91). No downstream guard exists — outbox.ts enqueue/drain never re-checks the user (its 'deleted user's queued mail cascades away' comment refers to the FK cascade on the separate HARD-delete route api/admin/users/[id].delete.ts). grep of users.deletedAt across packages/server/src shows filters only in auth/identity.ts, comms/broadcast.ts, profile/profile.ts and publicApi/serializers.ts. No test pins status/deletedAt in notification-email.test.ts. A soft-deleted user's local content is not hidden and a suspended user's profile is still resolvable (profile.ts:39 filters deletedAt only), so new like/comment/follow notifications keep being generated. Two overstatements in the scenario that do not refute it: the digest filters n.createdAt >= since (24h/7d window), so it is not 'every day forever' from three stale notifications but needs continuing activity; and all three paths are inert unless features.emailNotifications is on (default false; on deveco it is on but against a console sink). Severity P2 is correct: reachable, privacy/GDPR-relevant, but gated behind a default-off flag and requiring an admin action.

</details>

#### [P2] Notification dedup collapses the row but still enqueues an email each time

`packages/server/src/notification/notification.ts:225`

createNotification() calls the registered email sender unconditionally at the end, including on the UNIQUE-violation path where it merely UPDATEs an existing notification row, so the de-duplication the schema exists to provide never reaches the email path.

**Failure:** Instance has features.emailNotifications on; victim V has emailNotifications = { likes: true } (no digest). Attacker A repeatedly POSTs /api/social/like on V's project, toggling like -> unlike -> like. Each `liked:true` reaches social.ts:89 -> createNotification -> INSERT hits uq_notif_user_type_actor_link (same user/type/actor/link) -> the catch at notification.ts:187 UPDATEs the single row (correct), but line 225 still fires registeredEmailSender -> notification-email.ts:76 enqueueEmail. The social rate tier is 30 req/min/user (packages/infra/src/security.ts:250), so one attacker account sustains ~15 emails/min (~900/hour) into V's inbox from the instance's verified sending domain, with no per-recipient throttle anywhere in the outbox (drainEmailOutbox throttles provider CALLS, not per-recipient volume). The same path is reachable without any dedup at all from federation: notifyRemoteInteraction (inboxHandlers.ts:125) passes no actorId, so actor_id is NULL, Postgres treats NULLs as distinct, and every remote Like activity id creates a brand-new row plus a new email.

**Suggested fix:** Have createNotification report whether the row was INSERTed or UPDATEd (e.g. return `{ notification, created: boolean }`), and only call registeredEmailSender when created===true, or when the existing row's createdAt is older than a cooldown. Independently add a per-(userId, category) enqueue throttle in enqueueEmail, and pass a stable actorId from notifyRemoteInteraction so federated interactions dedup too.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. notification.ts:224-229 calls registeredEmailSender unconditionally after the try/catch, and the 23505 UPDATE branch (lines 173-193) leaves no "was this new?" signal on the returned NotificationItem, so the email path genuinely cannot see the dedup that the schema comment (packages/schema/src/social.ts:118-122) says the constraint exists to provide. The loop is reachable: toggleLike (social/social.ts:30-58) deletes the like row on unlike, so a re-like re-enters createNotification (social.ts:89) with the same (user,type,actor,link) tuple. Downstream there is no suppression: the plugin sender (layers/base/server/plugins/notification-email.ts:110-142) gates only on shouldEmailNotification (per-type pref, digest mode, unsubscribedAll) and getNotificationEmailTarget (verified + global opt-out), then enqueueEmail; enqueueEmail is a bare INSERT (comms/outbox.ts:39-41) into email_outbox, which has only idx_email_outbox_claim and no unique key (schema/comms.ts:40); drainEmailOutbox throttles provider CALLS via maxBatchesPerSecond only, with no per-recipient cap anywhere in comms/. The 30/min social tier is real and applied (infra/security.ts:246-268 via layers/base/server/middleware/security.ts:69), so ~15 emails/min per attacker account is the correct bound. The settings page defaults to likes=true, digest='none' (layers/base/pages/settings/notifications.vue:7,11), so the vulnerable pref combo is what a user gets by saving that page once. No test pins the correct behaviour: notification.integration.test.ts:256-294 exercises the exact like->unlike->like loop but registers no email sender, and notification-email.test.ts:149 uses a notification with no actor/link so it never hits the dedup branch. Git history (2dbddaa7, session 103) shows the email hook was appended to an already-dedup-aware function, not a deliberate design. One correction to the report: the federation claim's mechanism is wrong in detail — onLike IS idempotent per (actorUri, objectUri, inbound) at inboxHandlers.ts:1183-1200 — but onUndo deliberately DELETEs that activity row to clear the idempotency marker (inboxHandlers.ts:375-380), so a remote like->undo->like loop re-fires notifyRemoteInteraction each cycle, and since it passes no actorId (inboxHandlers.ts:114-125) NULL-distinct means a brand-new row plus a new email every time, i.e. no dedup at all. Same conclusion. Severity lowered to P2: features.emailNotifications defaults false (config/schema.ts:99) and is not enabled in apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts; on the one instance where it is on (deveco) the adapter resolves to ConsoleEmailAdapter (layers/base/server/utils/email.ts:26-60), so nothing reaches an inbox today, and exploitation additionally requires the victim to have saved notification prefs plus a verified address. Genuine unguarded defect that bites the moment an operator turns on email with a real provider, but not P1.

</details>

#### [P2] Admin broadcast has no idempotency; a mid-send failure double-sends on retry

`packages/server/src/comms/broadcast.ts:91`

sendBroadcast enqueues N chunks in a loop outside any transaction and writes the broadcasts audit row only after all chunks succeed, so a failure on chunk k leaves chunks 1..k-1 already queued for delivery while the caller sees a 500 and no broadcast row.

**Failure:** Admin sends a broadcast to audience 'all' on an instance with 3,000 verified users. The loop at line 91 issues 6 inserts of 500 rows each. Chunk 4 fails (connection reset / statement timeout - each row carries a full ~5KB HTML body, so a chunk is several MB). The exception propagates out of sendBroadcast, past the route (layers/base/server/api/admin/broadcast/index.post.ts:22), and the admin gets a 500. Meanwhile 1,500 rows are already `pending` in email_outbox and the worker delivers them within seconds. No `broadcasts` row was written (line 96 never runs), so the admin history shows nothing was sent. The admin clicks Send again - those first 1,500 users receive the broadcast twice. Additionally the whole recipient set plus rendered HTML is materialised in one array at line 66 before any chunking, so 'all' on a large instance is an OOM risk the chunk loop does not mitigate.

**Suggested fix:** Insert the `broadcasts` row FIRST (status 'sending'), stamp its id on every outbox row, and enqueue with ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING on (broadcastId, userId) so a retry is idempotent; stream recipients in keyset pages rather than materialising the whole audience.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/server/src/comms/broadcast.ts:59-107: sendBroadcast selects the full audience with no limit, materialises every OutboxMessage with the full rendered HTML, loops enqueueEmails in 500-row chunks with no transaction and no try/catch, and inserts the `broadcasts` audit row only after the loop completes. enqueueEmails (outbox.ts:44-47) is a bare autocommit insert, and email_outbox (packages/schema/src/comms.ts:20-40) has no unique constraint or dedupe/request-id column, so a retry inserts duplicate deliverable rows. The route (layers/base/server/api/admin/broadcast/index.post.ts:22) adds no idempotency. The UI (layers/base/pages/admin/broadcast.vue:97-111) guards a rapid double-click via :disabled="sending" plus a confirm step, but on error it just toasts "Broadcast failed" and re-enables, and because the broadcasts row was never written the history list shows nothing sent, which invites the retry. No test pins atomicity: broadcast.integration.test.ts covers only audience filtering, unsubscribe headers and the happy-path audit row. The issue is already documented as unfixed in docs/plans/federation-email-audit-fixes.md:310-313 ("Broadcast runs inline in the request + no idempotency ... add a submit idempotency guard"); git history shows only the chunking half ever landed. I tried to refute on the feature flag: adminBroadcast defaults false (config/src/schema.ts:120, layers/base/nuxt.config.ts:133) and docs/STATUS.md:918 claims it is OFF in prod, but that doc is stale - live https://deveco.io/api/features returns adminBroadcast:true and emailNotifications:true (commonpub.io returns both false), so the path is live and admin-reachable. Two corrections to the claim as written: (1) the multi-chunk partial send needs >500 recipients, since below that there is exactly one atomic INSERT - but the finding survives anyway because a failure of the audit-row insert after a successful chunk, or a proxy/client timeout mid-loop, produces the same "emails sent, history empty, admin retries, everyone double-sent" outcome at any audience size; (2) the OOM sub-claim is overstated - bodyText is capped at 5000 chars (validators/comms.ts:39), so ~6-8KB per row and ~20MB for 3,000 recipients, only a real memory concern in the tens of thousands. Severity P2 holds: admin-only path, needs an infra failure or timeout to trigger, consequence is duplicate mass email with no audit trail (deliverability/spam-complaint risk), not data loss or a security break.

</details>

#### [P3] email_outbox is never pruned; message bodies retained indefinitely and hidden from DSAR export

`packages/server/src/comms/outbox.ts:217`

Rows are marked status='sent' and left in place forever; there is no purge worker, no retention window, and no migration that deletes them, so the table grows without bound and permanently stores each recipient's address alongside the rendered body of every notification they received.

**Failure:** On an instance with emailNotifications on, every instant notification, digest, broadcast and contest reminder writes a row carrying to_email, the full HTML body (which embeds the recipient's username, the notification text, and their per-user unsubscribe HMAC token in the headers jsonb), and never removes it. At ~5KB/row and a few thousand emails a day this is multiple GB per year of pure retention. It also creates a privacy inconsistency: packages/server/src/profile/export.ts:52 explicitly excludes email_outbox from the GDPR subject export as an "operational queue (not subject data)", yet the rows hold that subject's address and message content; and because updateUserStatus soft-deletes rather than hard-deletes (admin.ts:409), the ON DELETE CASCADE on user_id never fires for a 'deleted' account, so their sent mail bodies persist after deletion.

**Suggested fix:** Add a purge pass to the email-outbox worker (or a plugin modelled on disclosure-purge.ts): delete status='sent' rows older than N days and status='failed' rows older than a longer window, driven by a runtimeConfig retention setting; optionally null out html/text on send and keep only metadata.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The core gap is real and I confirmed it by reading code: outbox.ts:215-218 is the only terminal write (status='sent'), and repo-wide the only delete(emailOutbox) calls are in three integration tests (contest-email-copy/reminders/registrations setup). No production DELETE, no purge plugin (disclosure-purge.ts names only disclosure_events), no retention key in packages/config/src, and migration 0036_stale_toro.sql creates the table with one claim index and no TTL. Rows retain the full rendered html plus a non-expiring HMAC unsubscribe bearer token in headers (unsubscribe.ts:33-41). BUT two of the three legs of the stated failure scenario are refuted. (1) "Bodies persist after deletion" is FALSE: both erasure paths - self-service layers/base/server/api/auth/delete-user.post.ts:57 and admin layers/base/server/api/admin/users/[id].delete.ts:13 - call deleteUser, which ends in `tx.delete(users).where(eq(users.id, userId))` (admin.ts:696), a hard delete firing the ON DELETE cascade. updateUserStatus (admin.ts:388-412) is a moderation status change, not an erasure endpoint, so no deletion claim rests on it; and every producer (notification-email.ts:75-82, broadcast.ts:80-88, contest/registrations.ts:130-138, contest/reminders.ts:240) sets userId, so the cascade has full reach with no orphan rows. (2) The "privacy inconsistency" with the DSAR export is a deliberate, pre-audited decision, not an oversight: docs/plans/session-231-remediation-roadmap.md:100-102 explicitly lists email_outbox under "MUST NOT export (security/third-party)", and the underlying subject data is exported via its own tables (profile email, notifications, consents) - the outbox row is a rendered derivative. Severity drops to P3: all producers are gated on features.emailNotifications which defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:99), so most instances write nothing; the one instance with it on is deveco on a console sink at maker-community volume, far from the assumed few-thousand-a-day. What survives is a hygiene gap - a table the roadmap calls "transient" that nothing makes transient - fixable with a bounded purge worker modeled on disclosure-purge.ts.

</details>

#### [P3] Stage advancement can tell a multi-entry participant the wrong outcome

`packages/server/src/contest/judging.ts:292`

The advancement notification de-dupes by userId and keeps whichever entry appears FIRST in an unordered result set, so the message reflects an arbitrary entry rather than the participant's best outcome.

**Failure:** User U has two eligible entries in a staged contest: entry A (eliminated at this review cut) and entry B (advanced). advanceContestStage's notification loop walks `eligible` - the rows returned by the unordered `select ... .for('update')` at judging.ts:243-246 - and at line 292 skips any userId already `seen`. If A precedes B in the result set, U is notified "Your entry wasn't selected to continue in <contest>" and never learns B advanced; the notification link is the same /contests/<slug> for both branches so no second row appears. Postgres row order here is not stable, so the outcome flips between runs.

**Suggested fix:** Group by userId and compute `adv = eligible.filter(e => e.userId === u).some(e => advancedIds.has(e.id))` before notifying, so any advancing entry produces the 'You advanced!' message.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. packages/server/src/contest/judging.ts:243-246 selects entry rows with `.for('update')` and NO ORDER BY; `eligible = rows.filter(r => !isEliminated(r))` preserves that arbitrary order. The notify loop at judging.ts:289-303 keeps the FIRST row per userId (`if (seen.has(e.userId)) continue`) and derives the whole message from that one row's `advancedIds.has(e.id)`. Nothing prevents a user holding two eligible entries with split outcomes: packages/schema/src/contest.ts:577 uniques only `(contestId, userId, contentId)`, and `maxEntriesPerUser` (contest.ts:474) is a nullable integer defaulting to NULL = unlimited, enforced only where an organizer sets it (packages/server/src/contest/submissions.ts:446-452, entries.ts:263-285). In topN mode the sort is by score, so one entry can land inside the cut and the other outside; manual mode is the same. I also verified the sub-claim that no second row appears: createNotification (packages/server/src/notification/notification.ts:141-200) dedupes against uniqueIndex `uq_notif_user_type_actor_link` on (user_id, type, actor_id, link) (packages/schema/src/social.ts:131), and both branches pass a non-null actorId (the organizer) and the identical link `/contests/<slug>`, so even without the `seen` set the second insert would only overwrite the first row's title/message. Either way the participant gets exactly one notification whose outcome is arbitrary; a user whose entry B advanced can be told "Your entry wasn't selected to continue". No feature-flag rescue beyond `requireFeature('contests')` in layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/advance.post.ts, and contests are live. git log shows the block was added verbatim in d97b1dbf (Phase B2) and only moved by the 0ccd55c4 decomposition, so the de-dup was a deliberate anti-spam measure that was never made outcome-aware. No test pins it: packages/server/src/__tests__/contest.integration.test.ts asserts notifications only for winner/runner-up on completion (lines 1172-1210), never for advanceContestStage. Severity P3 is right: narrow precondition (multi-entry participant plus a split outcome at one cut), copy-only impact, and the per-entry Advanced / Not-advanced badges on the linked contest page show the true state. Fix is one line: group by userId and use `entries.some(e => advancedIds.has(e.id))` so the surviving entry wins the slot.

</details>

#### [P3] Rich-text links in a contest email body are silently stripped of their href

`packages/server/src/emailBlocks.ts:71`

blockText() reduces a paragraph/heading/quote/callout block to plain text via stripTags before escaping, so any link mark an organizer adds inside a text block loses its URL entirely - the anchor text survives with nothing to click.

**Failure:** An organizer writes a reminder body paragraph "Submit your entry on the contest page" with "contest page" hyperlinked in the block editor (the palette at layers/base/components/contest/ContestEmailEditor.vue:74-77 offers paragraph/heading/quote as TipTap rich-text blocks). renderEmailBlocks hits case 'text'/'paragraph' and calls blockText(content), which for an `html` payload does `decodeEntities(stripTags(html))` - the <a href> is removed, only the label text remains. The reminder that goes to every registrant contains dead copy referring to a link that isn't there. The only block type that can emit a working URL in an email body is registrationLink.

**Suggested fix:** Either preserve anchors in the email-safe subset (whitelist <a href> through the same absolutizeHref + http(s) guard already used for registrationLink and images), or append stripped hrefs as a footnote; at minimum warn in the editor when a text block contains a link mark that will not survive the send.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the whole path. BlockCanvas.vue (packages/editor/vue/components/BlockCanvas.vue:218-236, 494-520) renders an unconditional floating-toolbar "Insert link" button + URL prompt that applies a TipTap link mark; TextBlock.vue:52 registers Link and emits {html: getHTML()}, so a contest email paragraph is stored as '<p>… <a href="https://…">contest page</a></p>'. contestEmailTemplateCopySchema (packages/schema/src/validators/contest.ts:75-80) types bodyBlocks as z.array(z.array(z.unknown())) and strips/rejects nothing. renderEmailBlocks line 71 then does decodeEntities(stripTags(html)) with stripTags = s.replace(/<[^>]*>/g,'') (line 40), and the 'text'/'paragraph', 'heading', 'quote', 'callout' cases emit esc(t) with no link reconstruction — only case 'registrationLink' builds an <a>. This renderer is the single choke point for preview (email-preview.post.ts:55), test send (email-test.post.ts:68) and real send (packages/server/src/contest/emailCopy.ts:36). No flag hides the link button (the contests flag gates the whole feature and contests are live on deveco), no test pins link preservation (emailBlocks.test.ts:35-39 only pins tag stripping as an injection guard), and git history (5be98816, dc0c062b, a86286aa, b64449b0, b9e83b42) shows the strip was a deliberate anti-injection policy whose authors reasoned about unsupported block TYPES being dropped but never about the inline link mark. Severity stays P3: the live preview is rendered by the same function so the organizer can see the unlinked text before sending, registrationLink covers the main CTA, and TipTap autolink means a bare typed URL survives as text that mail clients relinkify — the loss only bites when custom anchor text hides a distinct URL.

</details>

### FRONTEND ROBUSTNESS — SSR/hydration, responsive, and design-system compliance

#### [P2] Contest entry detail page server-renders wall-clock times — mismatches for every viewer outside the server's timezone

`layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId].vue:74`

`fmtDate` renders `toLocaleString` with `hour`/`minute` from `useLazyFetch` data. `useLazyFetch` still fetches on the server (`options.server !== false` in nuxt/dist/app/composables/asyncData.js:73-79 registers `onServerPrefetch`), so the submitted-at timestamps are in the SSR'd HTML in the server's timezone.

**Failure:** deveco.io runs in UTC with a live contest. Entry submitted `2026-08-19T21:05:00Z`. Server HTML: "Entered Aug 19, 2026, 9:05 PM". A judge or entrant in US/Central hydrates and Vue re-renders "Aug 19, 2026, 4:05 PM" — a mismatch on every load of every entry page for every non-UTC viewer, not just near a date boundary. Same shape at ContestStageSubmission.vue:85 (`Submitted {{ submittedAtLabel(...) }}`) on the contest page itself.

**Suggested fix:** Gate `fmtDate` on a `mounted` ref exactly as ContestSidebar.vue:57 does (`if (!mounted.value) return null`) and keep `<time :datetime="iso">` in the SSR markup.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. (1) layers/base/pages/contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId].vue:74-76 defines fmtDate with toLocaleString including hour/minute, and calls it unguarded at line 130 and line 149; the file contains no onMounted and no mounted flag. (2) The data really is server-rendered: nuxt@3.21.2 asyncData.js computes `fetchOnServer = options.server !== false && nuxtApp.payload.serverRendered` and registers `onServerPrefetch(() => promise)` when `import.meta.server && fetchOnServer && options.immediate`; line 15's useLazyFetch passes no options, so `entry` is resolved before the server render. The same page file's sibling (contests/[slug]/index.vue:24) explicitly passes `{ server: false }` for per-viewer data, showing the opt-out exists and was not used here. (3) No upstream guard: nuxt.config.ts has `routeRules: {}` and no `ssr: false`; the endpoint layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/entries/[entryId]/index.get.ts uses getOptionalUser and always returns submittedAt for a published entry on a public contest, so the timestamp is in the SSR HTML for every viewer. The only flag is requireFeature('contests'), which is on (deveco has a live contest). (4) The codebase treats this as a known bug class and guards it elsewhere: ContestSidebar.vue:57-62 carries the explicit comment about server-vs-viewer TZ mismatch and gates on a mounted ref, ContestSignup.vue:65-67 does the same, and ContestEntryPrivateData.vue:49 has the identical toLocaleString but is safe because privateData is only fetched client-side (loadPrivate returns early when `typeof window === 'undefined'`). The entry-detail page is the outlier, not a deliberate exception. (5) The second cited location is also real: ContestStageSubmission.vue:85 submittedAtLabel is rendered in the template ("Submitted {{ submittedAtLabel(existing.submittedAt) }}"), gated by myEntries which filters on user.value?.id, and layers/base/plugins/auth.ts populates the auth-user useState ON THE SERVER from event.context.auth, so an entrant's block does render during SSR. (6) No test pins it: layers/base/components/contest/__tests__/contestEntryDetailPage.test.ts has zero references to fmtDate/toLocale/mounted. Severity should be P2, not P1: Vue patches mismatched text on hydration, so the impact is a brief wrong-time flash, a console hydration warning, and wrong times in crawler-visible HTML on a daily-traffic path. No data loss, privacy, or functional breakage, which is what P1 would require here.

</details>

#### [P2] AuthorRow formats a locale date unguarded — a live homepage hydration mismatch session 252 missed

`layers/base/components/AuthorRow.vue:22`

`AuthorRow` renders `new Date(date).toLocaleDateString('en-US', {...})` straight in its template with no mounted guard, and it is used inside the homepage's SSR-blocking featured card as well as every content detail page.

**Failure:** `pages/index.vue:104` does a blocking `await useFetch('/api/content', { featured: true })`, and line 367 renders `<AuthorRow :date="featured.items[0].publishedAt || createdAt" />` whenever the instance has no editorial picks. Content published at `2026-08-11T02:00:00Z`: server (UTC) emits "Aug 11, 2026", an LA browser re-renders "Aug 10, 2026" -> "Hydration completed but contains mismatches" on the homepage of any instance on the legacy featured branch. This is a strong candidate for the unidentified per-load mismatch STATUS.md:150 records for heatsynclabs.io. Same component fires on `/u/:user/:type/:slug` (index.vue:124) and `/learn/:slug` (index.vue:136).

**Suggested fix:** Apply the ContentCard.vue:63-74 pattern: `const mounted = ref(false); onMounted(() => mounted.value = true)`, emit `<time :datetime="isoDate">` always and the human string only when `mounted`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The component defect is real and I confirmed it in live SSR output, but the claimed blast radius is largely wrong, so severity drops to P2. CONFIRMED: AuthorRow.vue:22 formats `new Date(date).toLocaleDateString('en-US', {...})` inline with no onMounted guard (no onMounted anywhere in the 81-line file) and no `datetime` attribute on the `<time>`; git log shows the file untouched since the initial layer commit 00790b98, so it never got the session-252 fix that ContentCard.vue:56-73 has (mounted ref + ISO datetime fallback, with a comment describing precisely this timezone mismatch). Live proof: https://commonpub.io/learn/test SSRs `<div class="cpub-author-meta"><time data-v-b98f7e35>Apr 1, 2026</time>` — server-timezone text, no machine-readable ISO. REFUTED PARTS: (1) The homepage scenario is unreachable. index.vue:367 sits under `<template v-else>` (line 226), reached only when layoutEngineActive is false AND hasCustomSections is false; hasCustomSections = `!!homepageSections.value?.length` fed by /api/homepage/sections -> getHomepageSections (packages/server/src/homepage/homepage.ts:82), which returns the 6-entry DEFAULT_SECTIONS whenever the DB setting is absent and can never yield an empty array. Verified in production HTML: heatsynclabs.io, deveco.io and commonpub.io homepages all contain zero `class="cpub-author-row"`; the `cpub-featured-card` string in heatsync's HTML is only inside the inlined stylesheet. So this is NOT the heatsynclabs.io per-load mismatch from STATUS.md:150 — heatsync also has zero learning paths (/api/learn returns {"items":[],"total":0}). (2) The content-detail call site u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:124 is inside the `v-else` generic fallback (line 109), reached only for a content type outside project|article|blog|explainer — and contentTypeEnum (packages/schema/src/enums.ts:10) holds exactly those four, all handled by ViewsProjectView/ViewsArticleView/ViewsExplainerView at lines 104-106. Only pages/learn/[slug]/index.vue:136 is live (useLazyFetch without server:false, so it SSRs; `learning` defaults true in packages/config/src/schema.ts:85). Real SSR-correctness bug plus a lost machine-readable date, cheap to fix with the existing ContentCard pattern, but confined to learning-path detail pages currently holding one test path across all three instances — P2, not P1.

</details>

#### [P2] Events pages render times of day during SSR — guaranteed mismatch, not just at date boundaries

`layers/base/components/EventCard.vue:37`

`EventCard.formatTime` and the event detail page's `formatTime` render `toLocaleTimeString` in SSR'd markup. Unlike a date, a formatted time differs for every viewer whose UTC offset is not the server's, so this mismatches on essentially every load rather than only near midnight.

**Failure:** `pages/events/index.vue:33` uses a blocking `await useFetch('/api/events')` and renders `<EventCard>` per item (line 133). Event starting `2026-09-02T18:00:00Z`: server HTML says "2:00 PM" (if TZ=UTC, "6:00 PM"), a Berlin viewer's client renders "8:00 PM". Same on `pages/events/[slug]/index.vue`, which awaits `useFetch` at lines 9-10 and formats at :58 (`weekday/month/day/year`) and :67 (`hour/minute`) — so a Tokyo viewer can also see a different weekday than the crawler indexed.

**Suggested fix:** Guard both with a mounted ref and keep `<time :datetime="iso">` for SSR/crawlers, matching CountdownTimer.vue:22,39.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code and by fetching the live SSR'd HTML. Code: `layers/base/components/EventCard.vue:28-41` defines `formatDate` (toLocaleDateString) and `formatTime` (toLocaleTimeString), both called directly in the template at line 56 (`{{ formatDate(event.startDate) }} at {{ formatTime(event.startDate) }}`). There is no `onMounted`/mounted guard anywhere in the file, no `<ClientOnly>`, and no `<time datetime>` machine-readable fallback. `layers/base/pages/events/[slug]/index.vue:57-71` repeats the same two functions and renders them at template lines ~108 and ~110 (`{{ formatTime(event.startDate) }}, {{ formatTime(event.endDate) }}`), also unguarded. Both pages SSR the data: `pages/events/index.vue:33` and `pages/events/[slug]/index.vue:9-10` are top-level `await useFetch(...)` in `<script setup>`, so `data` is populated during SSR. No upstream guard exists. `nuxt.config.ts:186` has `routeRules: {}`, nitro preset `node-server`, no `ssr: false`, and neither events page calls `definePageMeta({ ssr: false })`. The feature flag does NOT make this unreachable. `packages/config/src/schema.ts:84` defaults `events: false`, but I checked empirically: `GET /api/features` returns `events: true` on both commonpub.io and deveco.io. And the SSR'd HTML proves the failure: `curl https://deveco.io/events` returns `<div class="cpub-event-date">... Sep 1, 2026 at 5:30 PM`, and `curl https://deveco.io/events/breaking-breadboards-brighton-uk` returns `<div class="cpub-event-info-sub">5:30 PM, 7:30 PM`. Those strings are computed in the server process's timezone; any viewer in a different UTC offset hydrates a different string, which is a mismatch on essentially every load rather than only near midnight — exactly as claimed. The repo already recognises this bug class and has a fix pattern the events code skipped: `ContentCard.vue:56-73` carries an explicit comment ("`toLocaleDateString` formats in the RENDERER's timezone... a hydration mismatch... invisible in local dev") plus a `mounted` ref set in `onMounted`, and `components/contest/ContestSidebar.vue:58-67` and `pages/settings/privacy.vue:540` do the same. So this is not deliberate design — EventCard predates/ignores the established guard (its only commit is `09b47b4c feat(events): events system with RSVP + admin curation (Phase 5)`). No test pins the current behaviour; there is no EventCard test file. Severity P2 is right. It is on a live, flag-on, publicly reachable path, but Vue patches mismatched text nodes during hydration, so the practical harm is a wrong time in the SSR HTML (crawlers, no-JS, first paint) plus a flash, not a broken page — not P1. It is not P3 because the events index and detail are ordinary daily-traffic pages on two live instances and the wrong value is the event's start time, the single most load-bearing field on the card.

</details>

#### [P2] All three content views plus CommentSection format dates during SSR — every project/blog/explainer page

`layers/base/components/views/ArticleView.vue:130`

ArticleView, ProjectView, ExplainerView and CommentSection all call `toLocaleDateString` directly in SSR-rendered markup with no mounted guard, so the byline date and every comment timestamp can differ between server and client.

**Failure:** `/u/:user/blog/:slug` is server-rendered (the page's `useLazyFetch` at index.vue:13 still runs on the server). A post published `2026-08-11T01:30:00Z` renders "Aug 11, 2026" in the SSR HTML and "Aug 10, 2026" for a Los Angeles viewer. CommentSection does the same for every comment and every reply, so a page with 20 comments can produce 20 mismatched text nodes. ProjectView's `formattedDate` computed (line 118-123) is rendered at line 254 `Published {{ formattedDate }}`.

**Suggested fix:** Extract the ContentCard guard into a shared composable (e.g. `useClientDate`) and route all four through it; `layers/base/utils/datetime.ts` already has `formatLocalDate`, so only the mounted gate is missing.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading every cited line plus the call graph. ArticleView.vue:130 renders `new Date(...).toLocaleDateString('en-US', {...})` in a bare <span> with no mount guard and no <time :datetime>; ProjectView.vue:121 (formattedDate computed) is rendered unguarded at line 254; ExplainerView.vue:266/292 are inside <time :datetime> but the visible text is unguarded; CommentSection.vue:195/228 are unguarded AND their <time> elements carry no datetime attribute at all. Grepping all three views for `mounted|ClientOnly|isHydrated|import.meta.client|typeof window` returns zero hits (only an unrelated onUnmounted keydown cleanup at ExplainerView:171), so there is no guard upstream or downstream. SSR reachability verified rather than assumed: pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/index.vue:13 uses useLazyFetch with no `server: false` (lazy only skips client-navigation blocking; on the server Nuxt registers onServerPrefetch so the data resolves and renders), nuxt.config.ts has `routeRules: {}` and no `ssr:` key so SSR is on, and CommentSection is rendered inside all three views (ArticleView:295, ProjectView:452, ExplainerView:351) via `await useFetch(..., {lazy: true})` with no `server: false`, so comment timestamps are SSR'd too. No feature flag gates any of it; the 'en-US' locale is pinned but no timeZone option is passed, and no TZ= appears in any Dockerfile/compose/workflow, so the container formats in UTC while viewers do not. Git history supports the finding: commit d1e99dc4 'fix(hydration): stop SSR rendering timezone- and order-dependent content' touched only ContentCard.vue, homepage/ContestsSection.vue and pages/index.vue; ContentCard.vue:56-73 carries the canonical fix (mounted ref + :datetime="dateIso") with a comment naming this exact hazard, and ContestHero.vue:46,152 uses the same guard, so these four files are an unfixed remainder of a known, deliberately-fixed defect class rather than an intentional design. No test pins the behaviour (the view tests stub CommentSection: true). Additional extent not in the finding: components/AuthorRow.vue:22 has the identical unguarded call and is used by the page's fallback branch. Severity P2 is right: it is on the highest-traffic content path and the wrong day lands in the SSR HTML that crawlers index, but Vue repairs text-node mismatches during hydration so JS viewers see a flash rather than a persistent wrong value.

</details>

#### [P2] Every hub surface formats locale dates during SSR (nine call sites)

`layers/base/components/hub/HubFeed.vue:142`

The hub page tree — feed posts, announcements, discussions, member cards, the hub list, the members page and post detail — all format dates in SSR'd markup with no mounted guard. `useLazyFetch` on these pages still runs server-side, so the values are in the HTML.

**Failure:** `pages/hubs/[slug]/index.vue:22` fetches posts with `useLazyFetch` (server fetch runs; see asyncData.js:73-79) and renders `<HubFeed>` at line 350. A post created `2026-08-11T03:00:00Z` renders "Aug 11" server-side and "Aug 10" for a Los Angeles member, mismatching on every hub page view near a UTC date boundary. `pages/hubs/index.vue:34` builds `foundedLabel` inside a computed over SSR'd data, so the whole hub-card list can shift month at a month boundary.

**Suggested fix:** Same shared mounted-gated formatter; the hub tree is the largest single cluster, so fixing it in `FeedItem`/`MemberCard`/`DiscussionItem`/`AnnouncementBand` covers most of the feed.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. HubFeed.vue:142 and :165 format dates inline in the template with toLocaleDateString and no hydration gate; the same unguarded pattern is in FeedItem.vue:38, AnnouncementBand.vue:11, DiscussionItem.vue:22, MemberCard.vue:20, pages/hubs/index.vue:34, pages/hubs/[slug]/index.vue:68, members.vue:174 and :198, and posts/[postId].vue:139. No onMounted, no mounted ref, and no ClientOnly appears in any hub component or hub page. The SSR mechanism is real: nuxt/dist/app/composables/asyncData.js:73-79 computes fetchOnServer = options.server !== false && payload.serverRendered and calls onServerPrefetch(() => promise) without consulting `lazy`, so useLazyFetch data is resolved before the server render; pages/hubs/index.vue additionally uses blocking `await useFetch`, so its foundedLabel is unambiguously in the SSR HTML. No guard exists upstream: nuxt.config.ts:186 has routeRules: {} and no ssr:false, and the hubs flag is z.boolean().default(true) in packages/config/src/schema.ts:28. HubFeed renders on SSR for every hub because initialTab evaluates `!hub.value?.hubType` while hub.value is still null at setup, yielding 'feed'. The code is not deliberate: components/contest/ContestSidebar.vue:58-62 carries the exact fix with a comment naming this hazard ("toLocaleDateString is timezone-dependent, so it would mismatch between the server's TZ and the viewer's on hydration. Gate it on a client mounted flag"), so the hub surfaces are an unmigrated omission. No test pins the behaviour and there is no shared date composable. P2 is correct: public, default-on, daily path, but the impact is a hydration text mismatch and an off-by-one day in SSR markup near a UTC boundary, not data loss or a security issue; note the <time> elements also lack a datetime attribute, so crawlers see only the wrong day. Scope note for the parent: 44 files under layers/base/{components,pages} call toLocaleDateString/toLocaleTimeString, so the hub set is one cluster of a broader class.

</details>

#### [P2] Remaining SSR locale-date sites — the full inventory is ~31, not the ~10 STATUS estimates

`layers/base/pages/u/[username]/index.vue:209`

Beyond the clusters above, eleven more SSR-reachable surfaces format clock- or timezone-derived text with no mounted guard. STATUS.md:152 records "~10 other components"; the actual count across components and non-admin pages is about 31 call sites in 28 files (plus seven admin pages).

**Failure:** Public profile `/u/:username` is SSR'd via `useLazyFetch` (index.vue:25-27). `relTime` at line 204 uses `Date.now()` and falls through to `toLocaleDateString` at line 209, and lines 347/474/535 format join and certificate dates — so a profile joined `2026-08-01T02:00:00Z` renders "Joined Aug 2026" on the server and "Joined Jul 2026" for an LA viewer at a month boundary, plus every "2h/3d" relative label differs by whatever wall-clock time elapsed between render and hydration.

**Suggested fix:** Land one guarded helper in `layers/base/utils/datetime.ts` (a `useClientDate()` composable wrapping the existing `formatLocalDate`) and add a repo guard test that greps `.vue` files for `toLocale*` inside `<template>` without a mounted ref — otherwise this list will regrow.

Confirmed by reading code. pages/u/[username]/index.vue has no mount guard and no ClientOnly (grep for onMounted/isMounted/ClientOnly returns only the date sites themselves); its data is server-fetched because useLazyFetch still registers onServerPrefetch on the server in Nuxt 3.16, so profilePending is false and the v-else-if="p" branch at :300 renders during SSR. The five cited sites are real and unguarded: relTime at :204 uses Date.now() and falls through to toLocaleDateString at :209 (rendered at :419), join dates at :347 and :535 are unconditional in the hero and About tab, and the certificate date is at :474. No ssr:false, routeRules is empty ({}) in layers/base/nuxt.config.ts, no feature flag gates the affected markup, and no test pins the behaviour (the only hydration-adjacent e2e assertion, apps/reference/e2e/smoke.spec.ts:146-157, filters console noise and does not cover /u/:username). The fix pattern exists deliberately elsewhere, in components/ContentCard.vue:56-74 with a written-out mounted guard from session 252, which shows the project treats this as a bug rather than an accepted tradeoff. The inventory claim also checks out: docs/STATUS.md:152 records "~10 other components", but a scan of pages/ + components/ finds 97 raw locale/clock hits across 61 files, of which roughly 20 component sites and ~25 non-admin page sites have zero guard references, plus ~12 sites across 7 admin pages. Spot-checked evidence: federated-hubs/[id]/posts/[postId].vue:19 formats hour and minute (its only onMounted at :42 is fetchLikedState), and FederatedContentCard.vue:40-51 computes Date.now() inside timeAgo, rendered unguarded from pages/search.vue:348, pages/federation/index.vue:71 and pages/federation/users/[handle].vue:165. Severity P2 stands: public SSR pages with user-visible wrong text and hydration mismatches, but no data loss or security impact, and the class is already tracked as open.
