#### [P3] Five orphaned components still in the tree a month after the readiness audit flagged them

`layers/base/components/PostVoteButtons.vue:1`

`PostVoteButtons`, `TimelineItem`, `SkillBar`, `FormatToggle`, `ProgressTracker` and additionally `CpubEditor` have zero call sites — no PascalCase tag, no kebab tag, no path-prefixed name, no dynamic `:is` string, no import.

**Failure:** Not a runtime failure: dead surface area that still gets typechecked, linted, bundled into the component manifest and audited. `docs/reviews/production-readiness-audit-2026-07-23.md:50` listed the first five as "5 orphaned components (~345 L), zero references" a month ago and they are unchanged; `CpubEditor` is a sixth the audit missed (its only mentions are in layers/base/README.md:58 and archived plan docs, plus BlockContentRenderer.vue:4 which says it *replaces* CpubEditor).

**Suggested fix:** Delete them (or move the vote UI behind a tracked issue) and re-run `pnpm typecheck`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. All six components have zero call sites anywhere: a full-repo grep for PascalCase, kebab-case, and quoted-string forms across layers/, apps/, packages/, test-site/, scripts/, tools/ returned only generated .nuxt/components.d.ts declarations, CHANGELOG.md, codebase-analysis/, and docs/ prose. No dynamic resolution reaches them: the only resolveComponent string tables are BlockContentRenderer.vue:116 (block types) and pages/u/[username]/[type]/[slug]/edit.vue:141-144 (Editors*), and no plugin registers components by name, so the TermsReacceptanceGate counter-pattern does not apply. The most plausible live wiring is dead: docs/sessions/124 records PostVoteButtons wired into DiscussionItem/HubDiscussions, but DiscussionItem.vue:31-43 now hand-rolls its own .cpub-vote-btn markup and emits upvote/downvote, and HubFeed.vue:198 uses PollDisplay only. I also grepped every sibling consumer repo (deveco-io, heatsynclabs-io, hack-build, etc.) since layers/base is a published Nuxt layer whose components are consumer-facing API; no consumer uses any of the six (deveco-io's .cpub-editor-topbar/-shell-inner CSS targets the editor-shell chrome, not CpubEditor.vue's .cpub-editor, and there is no bare .cpub-editor rule there). git log -1 on all six shows the newest touch is 2026-06-24, all predating the 2026-07-23 audit, so they are unchanged as claimed, and docs/reviews/production-readiness-audit-2026-07-23.md:50 reads exactly as quoted. CpubEditor is genuinely a sixth the audit missed (archived session 043 records its swap for BlockContentRenderer across all four views). Two framing corrections that do not refute: (1) the "bundled" impact is overstated, since Nuxt resolves auto-import candidates per-usage at compile time so unused ones never enter the client bundle; the real cost is manifest declarations, typecheck, lint, and re-audit noise; (2) CpubEditor differs from the other five in that layers/base/README.md:58 advertises it as the published layer's content editor, so removing it is a small public-surface change needing a README edit in the same commit. Severity P3 is correct, arguably generous: docs/sessions/archived/056 already graded orphaned ProgressTracker as P4 "Not harmful", and there is zero runtime reachability across ~432 lines.

</details>

### Public API

#### [P1] GET /api/public/v1/events and /events/{slug} throw 500 on every non-empty result (startAt does not exist)

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/events/index.get.ts:41`

The route casts `EventListItem`/`EventDetail` to `PublicEventRow` with `as unknown as`, but the internal shape uses `startDate`/`endDate` while the serializer reads `row.startAt.toISOString()`, so the serializer dereferences undefined.

**Failure:** Instance with `features.events` on and at least one event whose status is published/active/completed. `GET /api/public/v1/events` with a valid `read:events` key -> `listEvents` returns rows with `startDate` -> `toPublicEvent` runs `row.startAt.toISOString()` -> TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toISOString') -> 500 for every request. Same for `/events/{slug}` (line 14). The endpoint can only return 200 when the list is empty.

**Suggested fix:** Map explicitly in the route (or add a `toPublicEvent` overload taking the internal `EventListItem`) instead of casting: `startAt: r.startDate, endAt: r.endDate, coverImageUrl: r.coverImage, host: ...`. Add one integration test per public list endpoint that seeds a row and asserts a 200 body against the OpenAPI schema.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading source and dist. packages/schema/src/events.ts:15-16 defines startDate/endDate; grep for "startAt" in packages/schema/src returns nothing. mapEventListItem (packages/server/src/events/events.ts:460-479) emits startDate/endDate/coverImage, and listEvents (line 94-156) returns those rows verbatim. The route casts them `as unknown as PublicEventRow[]` (index.get.ts:39), and toPublicEvent does an unconditional `row.startAt.toISOString()` (packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:438; same in packages/server/dist/publicApi/serializers.js:150) -> TypeError -> 500. No guard exists: the `.filter(r => !r.deletedAt && PUBLIC_STATUSES.has(r.status))` passes because `events` has no deletedAt column (undefined is falsy) and status is a real column; isPublicEvent on the [slug] route only inspects status/deletedAt. Feature gating does not make it unreachable: I checked live flags empirically, deveco.io/api/features returns "events":true AND "publicApi":true (commonpub.io has events:true, publicApi:false). requireApiScope only checks the key scopes. The only test, packages/server/src/__tests__/publicApi.test.ts:213-229, hand-builds `const row: any = { startAt: new Date(...) }` — a shape no producer emits — so it passes vacuously and pins nothing. Git history shows route and serializer landed in the same commit (1e509132), so this is not a deliberate later rename. Secondary non-crashing mismatches: coverImageUrl, waitlistCount, host are undefined on internal rows; endAt becomes null despite end_date being NOT NULL. Severity P1 is right — a permanent total outage of two documented endpoints on a live instance, but reachable only via an admin-issued API key rather than an ordinary user path, which keeps it below P0.

</details>

#### [P1] GET /api/public/v1/videos and /videos/{id} throw 500 on every non-empty result (row.author is undefined)

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/videos/index.get.ts:25`

`VideoListItem`/`VideoDetail` carry flat author columns (`authorId`, `authorName`, `authorUsername`, `authorAvatarUrl`) but `toPublicVideo` reads a nested `row.author.id`.

**Failure:** Instance with `features.video` on and at least one video row. `GET /api/public/v1/videos` with a `read:videos` key -> `toPublicVideo` evaluates `row.author.id` where `row.author` is undefined -> TypeError -> 500. Same crash on `/videos/{id}` (line 17). Also `description` is absent from `VideoListItem`, so even after the crash is fixed the list would report `description: null` for every video.

**Suggested fix:** Build the nested `author` in the route from the flat fields, or give `listVideos` a `USER_REF_SELECT`-shaped `author`. Same integration-test gap as the events routes.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading source, built dist, and an empirical repro. packages/server/src/video/video.ts:9-27 defines VideoListItem with flat author columns (authorId/authorName/authorUsername/authorAvatarUrl) and no nested `author`; listVideos (95-112) and getVideoById (137-155) emit exactly that shape. packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:593-598 reads `row.author.id` unguarded. The route at layers/base/server/api/public/v1/videos/index.get.ts:23 launders the mismatch through `as unknown as PublicVideoRow[]`, so vue-tsc cannot catch it; [id].get.ts:15 does the same. Running the shipped packages/server/dist/publicApi/serializers.js with the exact row listVideos produces yields `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'id')`. The isPublicVideo filter does not save it: VideoListItem has no `deletedAt`, so `!undefined` is true and every row reaches the serializer. Refutation attempts all failed: features.video defaults to TRUE (packages/config/src/schema.ts:30); `read:videos` is a real scope (packages/schema/src/validators/publicApi.ts:12) and both endpoints are published in the OpenAPI doc (openapi.json.get.ts:395,398); requireApiScope only does 401/403 on the key with no shape adaptation; the only test (packages/server/src/__tests__/publicApi.test.ts:278-293) hand-builds a `row: any` with a nested author, pinning the serializer in isolation while never exercising listVideos, so it passes vacuously. Git history (1e509132) shows routes and serializers landed together with mismatched row types - not deliberate. Secondary claim holds with a nuance: on the list path `description` is undefined, so JSON.stringify OMITS the key rather than emitting null. Two further unlisted defects in the same code: `category` is always null on both endpoints (flat categoryName/categorySlug never read), and `duration` is a string in the DB but typed number in PublicVideo. P1 is correct: hard 500 on two documented versioned public-API endpoints for any default-configured instance with at least one video; empty result sets return 200 with items:[], which is why it stays hidden until real content exists.

</details>

#### [P2] GET /api/public/v1/docs/{slug} throws 500 for every existing docs site (wrapper object passed to serializer)

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/docs/[slug].get.ts:14`

`getDocsSiteBySlug` returns `{ site, versions }`, but the route casts that wrapper straight to `PublicDocSiteRow` and serializes it, so every field resolves to undefined and `row.createdAt.toISOString()` throws.

**Failure:** Instance with `features.docs` on and a docs site with slug `handbook`. `GET /api/public/v1/docs/handbook` with a `read:docs` key -> `row` is `{ site: {...}, versions: [...] }` -> `isPublicDocSite` passes (undefined deletedAt is falsy) -> `toPublicDocSite` runs `row.createdAt.toISOString()` on undefined -> TypeError -> 500. A missing slug correctly 404s, so the endpoint returns 404 or 500 and never 200.

**Suggested fix:** `const result = await getDocsSiteBySlug(db, slug); ... toPublicDocSite({ ...result.site, versionCount: result.versions.length, defaultVersion: result.versions.find(v => v.isDefault)?.version ?? null }, domain)`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading source, built dist, schema and guards. packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:57 (and dist/docs/docs.js:60-63) returns the wrapper `{ site: {...row.site, owner}, versions }`. The route layers/base/server/api/public/v1/docs/[slug].get.ts:12 launders it with `row as unknown as PublicDocSiteRow`, a double cast that defeats typecheck. `isPublicDocSite` (packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:631) is only `!row.deletedAt`, and docs_sites has no deletedAt column at all (packages/schema/src/docs.ts:7-16), so that guard is vacuous and cannot reject the wrong shape. `toPublicDocSite` (serializers.ts:644) then calls `row.createdAt.toISOString()` on an undefined field -> TypeError -> 500. No guard exists: features.docs defaults to true (packages/config/src/schema.ts:29) and is true in all three in-repo commonpub.config.ts files; no validator inspects the shape; the only test in the route's __tests__ dir is openapi-route-parity.test.ts, a filesystem source sweep that never invokes a handler. Evidence it is an oversight rather than intent: the sibling docs/index.get.ts flattens correctly via listDocsSites, and contests/[slug].get.ts uses the identical blanket-cast pattern against getContestBySlug, which returns a flat ContestDetail — the pattern is correct everywhere except the one function with a wrapper return. Single commit in history (1e509132). Severity corrected P1 -> P2: the entire /api/public/ tree is gated by config.features.publicApi in layers/base/server/middleware/public-api-auth.ts:34, and that flag defaults to false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:124) and is enabled in none of the in-repo configs; reaching it also requires an API key holding read:docs. So it is a guaranteed, 100%-reproducible outage of a documented endpoint, but only on an instance that opted into the public API — not P3 (no working response ever), not P1 (not a daily user path, off by default everywhere in repo).

</details>

#### [P2] GET /metrics/tags/trending always returns an empty list: tags.usage_count is never written

`packages/server/src/publicApi/metrics.ts:250`

`getTrendingTags` filters `where usageCount > 0`, but no code in the repo ever writes `tags.usage_count`, so it is 0 for every tag created through the app.

**Failure:** Instance with hundreds of tagged articles. `GET /api/public/v1/metrics/tags/trending` with a `read:analytics` key returns `{ items: [], limit: 20 }` forever, while `GET /api/public/v1/tags` returns the same tags with real counts. Two endpoints publish the schema-identical `PublicTag.usageCount` and disagree. Separately, the `/tags` recomputation counts every `content_tags` row including draft, private and soft-deleted content, contradicting docs/public-api.md's 'Public entities only ... status=published, visibility=public, non-deleted, filtered at the SQL level'.

**Suggested fix:** Make one of them authoritative: either maintain `tags.usage_count` on tag attach/detach/publish, or have `getTrendingTags` compute the same aggregate as `/tags`. Whichever wins, join `content_items` and restrict to `status='published' AND visibility='public' AND deleted_at IS NULL` so the public count cannot reveal unpublished content.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading code. getTrendingTags (packages/server/src/publicApi/metrics.ts:246-253) filters/orders on tags.usageCount, but the only production writer of tags — syncTags in packages/server/src/content/content.ts:1032-1063 — inserts tags with only {name, slug} and never touches usageCount; it also never recomputes it on content_tags insert/delete. Repo-wide grep for usageCount|usage_count (excluding node_modules/dist/.output/.nuxt) yields only the column definition (packages/schema/src/content.ts:190, integer default 0 notNull), migration snapshots + 0000 baseline SQL, docs, the two read sites, and tests. No CREATE TRIGGER exists in any packages/schema/migrations/*.sql, so there is no DB-side maintenance either. No upstream guard makes it unreachable: the route layers/base/server/api/public/v1/metrics/tags/trending.get.ts only validates limit and calls requireApiScope(event,'read:analytics'); the public-api-auth middleware 404s only when features.publicApi is off, and although that flag defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:124), I verified live that https://deveco.io/api/features returns "publicApi":true (commonpub.io returns false), so the endpoint is served on a real instance and will return {items: [], limit} permanently. The existing test does not pin correct behaviour: packages/server/src/__tests__/metrics.integration.test.ts:77-80 seeds tags with literal usageCount 50/10/0, exercising the sort/filter over hand-written counters and passing vacuously with respect to the write path. The secondary claim also holds: layers/base/server/api/public/v1/tags/index.get.ts:26-32 computes count(content_tags.content_id) via a leftJoin to content_tags with no join to content_items and no status/visibility/deleted_at predicate, so drafts, private and soft-deleted content inflate the count, contradicting docs/public-api.md's public-entities-only guarantee; both endpoints serialize the same PublicTag.usageCount field via toPublicTag (serializers.ts:674) with divergent semantics. Prior art, not a refutation: docs/sessions/203-full-codebase-audit.md:124 already logged tags.usage_count as a dead counter at P3; it was never fixed and the Phase-2 metrics endpoint was later built on it. P2 is the right severity: it is a documented public endpoint that is permanently empty and self-contradictory against /tags, reachable today on deveco, but it sits behind a non-default flag and needs a read:analytics key, so it is not a path a regular user hits daily.

</details>

#### [P2] GET /api/public/v1/hubs always returns items: [] while reporting the real total and hasMore: true

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/hubs/index.get.ts:33`

The route filters `row.deletedAt === null`, but `HubListItem` (what `listHubs` returns) has no `deletedAt` property, so the comparison is `undefined === null` and every row is discarded.

**Failure:** Instance with 12 public hubs. `GET /api/public/v1/hubs` with a `read:hubs` key returns `{ items: [], total: 12, hasMore: true, limit: 20, offset: 0 }`. A consumer paging on `hasMore` loops forever and never sees a hub; the OpenAPI `paginated(PublicHub)` contract is violated (items empty with a non-zero total). No test covers this route.

**Suggested fix:** Delete the `.filter((row) => row.deletedAt === null)` - the SQL already excludes deleted and non-public hubs - and either widen `HubListItem` with `categories`/`website` or drop those from the serializer input for the list path.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full call graph. packages/server/src/hub/hub.ts:65-79 builds listHubs' return rows as an explicit 13-key object literal (no spread), and HubListItem (packages/server/src/types.ts:238-252) declares exactly those 13 fields with no deletedAt. layers/base/server/api/public/v1/hubs/index.get.ts:32-34 double-casts via `as unknown as PublicHubRow[]` (defeating the typecheck that would have caught this) and then filters `row.deletedAt === null`, which evaluates `undefined === null` -> false for every row, so `items` is unconditionally []. The filter is also redundant: hub.ts:36 already applies isNull(hubs.deletedAt) and eq(hubs.privacy,'public') in SQL. Attempts to refute all failed: (1) no test covers the route -- layers/base/server/api/public/v1/__tests__/ contains only openapi-route-parity.test.ts with zero 'hubs' references, and publicApi.test.ts:168-182 exercises only the serializer with a hand-built row that does include deletedAt; (2) not a deliberate shared idiom -- the sibling hubs/[slug].get.ts uses a truthy check `(hub as {deletedAt?: Date|null}).deletedAt` which is safe with undefined, and `deletedAt === null` appears exactly once under layers/base/server/api/public/; (3) the route never passes includeFederated, so no differently-shaped federated rows can rescue it; (4) not unreachable by flag -- features.publicApi defaults to false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:124) and commonpub.io reports "publicApi":false, but I curled deveco.io/api/features and it reports "publicApi":true and "hubs":true, so the route is live on a real instance. Two claimed details are overstated but do not rescue the finding: toPageMeta (packages/server/src/query.ts:227-242) computes hasMore as `offset + returned < total` using the UNFILTERED returned, so with 12 hubs at limit=20 hasMore is false (12 < 12), not true -- hasMore:true only when total > limit; and a consumer advancing offset terminates normally rather than looping forever, it just walks the whole set receiving zero hubs. Secondary issue for the fix: HubListItem also lacks `categories` and `website`, which toPublicHub reads (serializers.ts:226-244), so even after removing the filter the route would emit categories:null and website:undefined until listHubs selects those columns. Severity corrected to P2: the endpoint is totally broken for every consumer, but the blast radius is bounded -- opt-in surface off by default and off on commonpub.io, reachable only with an admin-issued key holding read:hubs, and it fails closed (empty result, no data leak or corruption).

</details>

#### [P2] docs/public-api.md states a consent INNER JOIN three of the four persona endpoints no longer do

`docs/public-api.md:344`

The published contract says every audience aggregate INNER JOINs the consent table and that 'there is no code path that counts a person without passing through that join', but since the 0.135 privacy-model correction fields/distribution/links count on legitimate interest with an Art. 21 objection anti-join.

**Failure:** An operator or DPO reading the API contract for deveco.io (which has all four persona flags ON per STATUS.md) concludes that a member who never granted a purpose is not counted in `/metrics/persona/distribution`. They are counted, unless they filed a statistics objection. The doc also lists only `features.persona` + `features.personaAnalytics` as required for 'all four', while `/metrics/persona/audience` additionally requires `features.dataSharingConsents`. The same stale wording is published to consumers in the OpenAPI summary for `/metrics/persona/distribution` ('consent-joined').

**Suggested fix:** Rewrite the three bullets to distinguish the two bases: `/audience` counts current grants (consent join); `/fields`, `/distribution`, `/links` count non-objecting members (legitimate interest, Art. 21 anti-join) still scoped by digest, public profile, active and not deleted. Update the OpenAPI summary string at the same time, and add the third flag to the requirement list for `/audience`.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code and the doc; every prong of the finding holds. 1. The stale text is really there. docs/public-api.md:344-347: "**Consent is a join, not a check.** Every aggregate INNER JOINs the consent table on a current grant whose recorded scope digest equals the live one... there is no code path that counts a person without passing through that join." The same claim is repeated in the summary bullet at docs/public-api.md:610 ("Persona aggregates are consent-joined... Answers count only where the member's grant is current AND its recorded scope digest matches"). 2. The code contradicts it. `grep -n "innerJoin\|countedUserWhere"` over packages/server/src/persona/metrics.ts shows exactly ONE consent join, at line 938 (`.innerJoin(consents, currentGrant(consents, offered.purpose, offered.scopeDigest))`), inside the `/audience` path. The fields query (line 519), both distribution queries (541, 565) and the link-presence CTE (762) call only `countedUserWhere()`, which at metrics.ts:464-470 is `and(isNull(users.deletedAt), eq(users.status,'active'), eq(users.profileVisibility,'public'), notObjected())`. `notObjected()` (metrics.ts:434-437) is `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM user_statistics_objections WHERE user_id = users.id)` — an anti-join, not a consent join. The file header at metrics.ts:42 states it outright: "The consent INNER JOIN became an objection ANTI-JOIN." So three of the four endpoints count a member who never granted anything. 3. The flag-ladder claim is also wrong. docs/public-api.md:337-338 says all four endpoints require "features.persona and features.personaAnalytics". audience.get.ts:38-40 requires a third, `requireFeature('dataSharingConsents')`; distribution.get.ts:40-48 deliberately omits it with a comment explaining why. So the doc understates audience's gating and the four endpoints do not share one ladder. 4. The stale wording is machine-published. layers/base/server/api/public/v1/openapi.json.get.ts:442 still summarises the distribution endpoint as "One persona field distribution, consent-joined, floored to the k-anonymity quantum". Not a deliberate state, and not guarded. `git show be8bede7` (the 0.135 privacy-model correction, "feat(persona)!: questions are private, statistics are not a consent") updated metrics.ts and parts of public-api.md (lines 397, 449-467 carry the new legitimate-interest/Art. 21 wording), but the diff contains no hit for "Consent is a join" and does not touch openapi.json.get.ts at all — the bullet and the OpenAPI summary were simply missed, leaving the doc self-contradictory within one file (line 344 says every aggregate joins consent; line 397 says "the first three count answers the instance holds, and `audience` counts consent grants"). The commit message itself confirms the code moved: "The six aggregate joins change from 'INNER JOIN a current, digest-matching grant' to 'exclude anyone who objected'." The failure scenario is live, not hypothetical. docs/STATUS.md:23-26 states "**deveco.io has all four ON** — an operator override saved via /admin/features... members previously uncounted without a `profile_analytics` grant are now counted in k-anonymous aggregates unless they object." That is precisely the behaviour docs/public-api.md:344 denies, so a DPO reading the published contract for deveco.io draws the wrong conclusion, and the doc errs in the privacy-overstating direction. Severity P2 is right. No runtime defect and no data leak — k-anonymity flooring, whole-field suppression and the objection anti-join all work as intended, so nobody is identifiable. But it is a published privacy contract that is materially false about the lawful basis on a live instance with the flags on, and the stale phrasing is also served to API consumers in the OpenAPI document. Too consequential for P3, well short of P1.

</details>

#### [P2] GET /api/public/v1/contests reports judgingDeadline, prizeDescription and communityVotingEnabled wrong

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/contests/index.get.ts:24`

`ContestListItem` carries none of `judgingEndDate`, `prizes` or `communityVotingEnabled`, so the optional reads in `toPublicContest` fall through to their defaults, while `/contests/{slug}` (fed by `ContestDetail`) returns the real values.

**Failure:** deveco.io's live contest has community voting enabled and prizes. `GET /api/public/v1/contests` returns `communityVotingEnabled: false, prizeDescription: null, judgingDeadline: null`; `GET /api/public/v1/contests/{slug}` returns `true` and the real prize summary. One OpenAPI schema (`PublicContest`) documents both, so an integrator building a contest listing publishes wrong facts about a live contest.

**Suggested fix:** Add the three columns to `ContestListItem`'s map (they are already on the `contests` row `listContests` selects with `db.select()`), or document a separate `PublicContestSummary` schema in the OpenAPI document that omits fields the list cannot answer.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the call graph. listContests (packages/server/src/contest/read.ts:93-110) maps rows to ContestListItem field-by-field and deliberately omits judgingEndDate, prizes and communityVotingEnabled (the full row IS fetched via db.select(), then discarded). The route index.get.ts:22-24 casts that array `as unknown as PublicContestRow` — the cast compiles only because those three are declared OPTIONAL on PublicContestRow (serializers.ts:488-491) — so toPublicContest's reads at serializers.ts:532-535 fall through to null/null/false on every list item, always. The detail route feeds getContestBySlug -> toContestDetail, which does map all three (read.ts ~156-160), so /contests/{slug} returns the real values. openapi.json.get.ts:388-392 documents both endpoints with the same PublicContest schema (fields defined at 226-245), so the contract claims identical meaning. No guard prevents it: requireFeature('contests') is off by default (config/src/schema.ts:31) but on where a live contest exists; requireApiScope only narrows the audience; isPublicContest merely filters status/visibility and cannot repopulate fields. No test pins the real path — publicApi.test.ts:253 ('maps real contest fields ... not null placeholders') asserts against a hand-built row that already carries judgingEndDate/prizes, so it passes while the route is wrong, and the only route-level test dir (layers/base/server/api/public/v1/__tests__/) holds just openapi-route-parity.test.ts, which mentions no contest fields. Git history (50bfb804) added the serializer fields plus that test without touching the list mapper, so this is an oversight, not a deliberate summary shape. Severity P2 holds: wrong data on a documented public contract, silently wrong because false/null look like legitimate values, but key-gated and flag-gated with no privacy or availability impact. Adjacent note for the fix: ContestDetail.prizes items have `title?: string` (contest/types.ts:12-18) while PublicContestRow declares `title: string`, so summarizePrizes can emit the literal 'undefined' on the detail endpoint for a description-only prize.

</details>

#### [P2] GET /api/public/v1/docs returns pageCount 0, versionCount 0 and defaultVersion null for every site

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/docs/index.get.ts:23`

`listDocsSites` returns the raw `docs_sites` row plus an owner; it computes no page or version counts, so all three documented aggregate fields fall through to their defaults.

**Failure:** A docs site with 3 versions and 40 pages. `GET /api/public/v1/docs` with a `read:docs` key returns `{ pageCount: 0, versionCount: 0, defaultVersion: null }`, which docs/public-api.md explicitly advertises ('Returns docs sites with pageCount, versionCount, defaultVersion, owner').

**Suggested fix:** Add grouped count subqueries for versions and published pages to `listDocsSites` (one grouped query for the page, never per row), or remove the three fields from the v1 schema and the doc.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the full path. layers/base/server/api/public/v1/docs/index.get.ts:19-23 calls listDocsSites then maps straight through toPublicDocSite with no aggregate step. packages/server/src/docs/docs.ts:29-52 selects only `{site: docsSites, owner}` via one innerJoin(users) and returns `{...row.site, owner}` — no join to docs_versions or docs_pages and no isDefault lookup. packages/schema/src/docs.ts:7-20 shows docs_sites has only id/name/slug/description/ownerId/themeTokens/createdAt/updatedAt; a grep for pageCount|versionCount|defaultVersion over packages/schema/src returns zero hits, so those keys can never be present and the `?? 0`, `?? 0`, `?? null` fallbacks in packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:641-643 always fire. Guards checked and none save it: requireFeature('docs') passes (config default true, and true on both live instances), and although features.publicApi defaults false I verified empirically that https://deveco.io/api/features returns "publicApi":true with "docs":true, so the endpoint is live on a real instance. The only related test, packages/server/src/__tests__/publicApi.test.ts:295-308, hand-feeds a synthetic row already containing pageCount:10/versionCount:2/defaultVersion:'v2' and asserts only canonicalUrl and no-private-leak — it never runs the query, so it masks rather than pins the behaviour. The contract is advertised in docs/public-api.md:214 and in the served OpenAPI at layers/base/server/api/public/v1/openapi.json.get.ts:274-276 (pageCount/versionCount typed integer, defaultVersion nullable string). It is an oversight rather than a deliberate stub: the sibling tags route (layers/base/server/api/public/v1/tags/index.get.ts:21-32) computes its usageCount aggregate inline with count(...)::int + groupBy, showing the established pattern is route-level aggregation; git history shows the docs route landed whole in commit 1e509132 and was never revisited. Severity stays P2: live on one production instance behind an issued read:docs key, and a silent 0 is worse than an absent field because clients cannot distinguish "empty site" from "not computed" — but it is metadata-only with no leak, crash, or data loss, and only one of the two instances has publicApi enabled.

</details>

#### [P2] GET /api/public/v1/learn documents lessonCount and publishedAt that can never carry a value

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/learn/index.get.ts:29`

`toPublicLearningPath` reads `row.lessonCount` and `row.publishedAt`, but the internal list item exposes `moduleCount` and the `learning_paths` table has no `published_at` column at all.

**Failure:** A published path with 6 modules. `GET /api/public/v1/learn` returns `lessonCount: 0, publishedAt: null` for it, and `/learn/{slug}` does the same. A consumer rendering 'N lessons' shows 0 for every path on the instance, and any consumer sorting or filtering on `publishedAt` gets nothing usable, though the OpenAPI schema lists both fields.

**Suggested fix:** Map `lessonCount` from `moduleCount` in the route, and either drop `publishedAt` from `PublicLearningPath` and the OpenAPI schema or add a real `published_at` column.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code. layers/base/server/api/public/v1/learn/index.get.ts:29 does `result.items as unknown as PublicLearningPathRow[]` — the double cast is what lets this compile. The producer, listPaths in packages/server/src/learning/learning.ts:84-100, emits LearningPathListItem (packages/server/src/types.ts:418-433) which has `moduleCount` and NO `lessonCount` and NO `publishedAt`. The serializer at packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:360 reads `row.lessonCount ?? 0` and :358 `row.publishedAt?.toISOString() ?? null`, both optional/nullable, so they silently degrade to 0 and null instead of throwing. The `learning_paths` table genuinely has no published_at column — verified against packages/schema/src/learning.ts:19-38 AND the live migration snapshot packages/schema/migrations/meta/0048_snapshot.json (columns: author_id, average_rating, completion_count, cover_image_url, created_at, description, difficulty, enrollment_count, estimated_hours, id, review_count, slug, status, title, updated_at). So publishedAt is structurally always null on every instance. /learn/[slug].get.ts has the identical cast over LearningPathDetail, which extends LearningPathListItem, so it has the same two gaps. Both fields are published in the OpenAPI doc at layers/base/server/api/public/v1/openapi.json.get.ts:193,195 and in docs/public-api.md:198. Refutation attempts that failed: (1) The only test, packages/server/src/__tests__/publicApi.test.ts:195-212, builds `const row: any` with fabricated `lessonCount: 5, publishedAt: new Date()` and asserts only private-field omission plus canonicalUrl — it tests a row shape no producer creates, so it masks the mismatch rather than pinning it. (2) Flag gating is real but not total: layers/base/server/middleware/public-api-auth.ts:34-36 404s all of /api/public/ when features.publicApi is false (default false per packages/config/src/schema.ts:124), but live checks show deveco.io reports publicApi True / learning True, and `curl https://deveco.io/api/public/v1/learn` returns 401 not 404 — the surface is live and needs only a read:learn API key. commonpub.io has it off. (3) Not deliberate: moduleCount is not just a rename of lessonCount (a correct count needs an aggregate over learning_lessons joined through learning_modules), and publishedAt has no backing column, so it must be dropped from the contract or added via migration. Severity P2 is right: reachable on a live instance with two permanently-wrong documented fields, but no crash, no data leak, and it affects API-key consumers of two learn endpoints rather than a daily interactive user path.

</details>

#### [P2] ?sort=popular and ?sort=featured are silently ignored on /content when seamlessFederation is on

`packages/server/src/content/content.ts:270`

`canMergeFederated` does not consider `filters.sort`, so any request without an author/tag/category/difficulty filter takes the federated merge path, which forces the chronological `mergeLocalOrder` and discards the requested sort.

**Failure:** On an instance with `features.seamlessFederation` on (federation has been live since session 188), `GET /api/public/v1/content?sort=popular` returns the same rows in the same order as `sort=recent` - ordered by publishedAt DESC, id DESC, never by viewCount. The OpenAPI document advertises `sort: recent|popular|featured` as an enum parameter with no caveat, and the request succeeds with 200, so a consumer has no signal that its sort was dropped.

**Suggested fix:** Add `&& (!filters.sort || filters.sort === 'recent')` to `canMergeFederated` so a non-chronological sort stays local-only, or document in the OpenAPI parameter that non-recent sorts apply to local content only.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Confirmed by reading the code end to end; I tried three refutation angles and all failed. 1. The mechanism is exactly as claimed. `packages/server/src/content/content.ts:270-279` — `canMergeFederated` tests only `authorId`, `followedBy`, `featured`, `editorial`, `categoryId`, `difficulty`, `tag`, `status`; `filters.sort` is never consulted. At line 362 `const willFederate = !!options?.includeFederated && canMergeFederated(filters)`, and at lines 381-391 `const orderBy = willFederate ? mergeLocalOrder : filters.sort === 'popular' ? ... : filters.sort === 'featured' ? ... : recencyOrder`. `mergeLocalOrder = [desc(publishedAt), desc(id)]`, so a requested `popular`/`featured` sort is discarded whenever the federated path is taken. The downstream JS merge (lines ~438-447) also sorts purely by publishedAt then id, so nothing restores the sort later. Note the local SQL is re-ordered even when zero federated rows come back — `willFederate` depends only on the flag plus the filter set, not on federated content existing. 2. No upstream guard. `layers/base/server/api/public/v1/content/index.get.ts:41` passes `sort: filters.sort` and line 42 passes `includeFederated: config.features.seamlessFederation` — no interaction between the two. The internal path is the same: `layers/base/server/utils/contentQuery.ts` returns `includeFederated: config.features.seamlessFederation` with no sort handling, so `/api/content?sort=popular` is affected identically. The Zod schema accepts `recent|popular|featured` and returns 200, and `layers/base/server/api/public/v1/openapi.json.get.ts:323` advertises the enum with no caveat, so the consumer gets no signal. 3. Not unreachable and not off-by-default-everywhere. `packages/config/src/schema.ts:88` defaults `seamlessFederation` to false, but `apps/reference/commonpub.config.ts:33` and `apps/shell/commonpub.config.ts:22` ship it true, and `docs/STATUS.md:1207-1211` ("Live flags per instance") records seamless as ON for commonpub.io and deveco.io, OFF only on heatsynclabs.io. So it is live on two of three real instances. 4. It is deliberate, but deliberate about the ordering, not about the contract. Commit 2d99c742 ("byte-align federated-merge ordering") and the surviving comment "Local-only keeps the requested sort" show the author forced recency on purpose so `merge(localTop-K, fedTop-K)[0:K] == globalTop-K` and offset pages stay disjoint. The only test that combines the two — `packages/server/src/__tests__/content.integration.test.ts:296`, `listContent(db, {sort:'popular',...}, {includeFederated:true})` — asserts only `dupes === 0`; it pins the no-dup property and would still pass if the sort were honoured, so nothing pins the current (wrong) ordering as correct. `docs/plans/pagination-scalability.md:45` even says the popular index is for "non-federation instances", acknowledging the tradeoff internally while the public OpenAPI discloses nothing. Severity P2 is right: no security or data impact, results are still valid published content, but it is a silently-wrong documented public API response on two live instances, and the same drop hits the in-app "popular" feed. The correct fix is arguably to disclose or explicitly reject the combination (e.g. suppress the merge for non-recency sorts, or document the degradation) rather than to restore the sort, since restoring it reintroduces the load-more dup bug 2d99c742 fixed.

</details>

#### [P3] GET /api/public/v1/events/{slug} has no private-hub gate, unlike the internal route it mirrors

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/events/[slug].get.ts:13`

The public detail route checks only `isPublicEvent` (status + deletedAt) and never calls `canReadHubById`, so a published event belonging to a private hub is served to any `read:events` key.

**Failure:** A private hub has a published event `members-only-build-night` with a location and locationUrl. `GET /api/events/members-only-build-night` correctly 404s for a non-member (canReadHubById). `GET /api/public/v1/events/members-only-build-night` with any `read:events` key (or `read:*`) returns title, description, location, locationUrl, startAt, capacity and attendeeCount. Today this is masked by the `startAt` 500 above - fixing that crash exposes the leak.

**Suggested fix:** Mirror the internal route: after fetching, `if (row.hubId && !(await canReadHubById(db, row.hubId, undefined))) throw 404`. A public API key has no viewer identity, so the correct check is 'hub privacy is public'.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

The missing gate is real. layers/base/server/api/public/v1/events/[slug].get.ts is 15 lines and does only requireApiScope('read:events') -> requireFeature('events') -> getEventBySlug -> isPublicEvent -> toPublicEvent; isPublicEvent (packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:422-425) tests only deletedAt and PUBLIC_EVENT_STATUSES. The internal twin layers/base/server/api/events/[slug].get.ts:18-24 does call canReadHubById with the P-1b comment, and listEvents (packages/server/src/events/events.ts:131-141) applies the private-hub exclusion. `grep -rn canReadHubById layers/base/server/api/public/v1/` returns ZERO hits, so no upstream or downstream hub gate exists on this path; hubs.privacy ('public'|'unlisted'|'private', packages/schema/src/hub.ts:43) is the only visibility signal and events has no visibility column. No test pins the behaviour (the only test under public/v1/__tests__ is openapi-route-parity.test.ts, which does not cover hub privacy). HOWEVER the failure scenario as written cannot occur today, and I am downgrading severity for that reason plus two upstream gates: 1) Currently unreachable: getEventBySlug returns EventDetail, whose fields are startDate/endDate/coverImage (events.ts:10-28 and mapEventListItem at 460-479). The route casts it `as unknown as PublicEventRow` and toPublicEvent does the unguarded `row.startAt.toISOString()` (serializers.ts:437; same in dist/publicApi/serializers.js:150). row.startAt is undefined, so the handler throws a TypeError -> 500 for EVERY event, private hub or not. No title/location/locationUrl is ever serialized. The finding concedes this masking, but it means the leak is latent, not live. 2) features.publicApi defaults false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:124) and no repo config enables it; middleware/public-api-auth.ts 404s all of /api/public/ when the flag is off. 3) Even with the flag on it requires an issued API key holding read:events (or read:*), not an anonymous request. So: keep the finding, fix the gate at the same time as the startAt mapping, because fixing the crash alone ships the leak. Worth flagging alongside it: layers/base/server/api/public/v1/events/index.get.ts passes `hubId` straight into listEvents without canReadHubById, which violates the assumption stated verbatim in the events.ts:133-135 comment ("The `?hubId=` path is route-authorized via canReadHubById"), so the same class of leak exists on the list route the moment the identical startAt crash is fixed there.

</details>

#### [P3] The OpenAPI parity test proves only that a path string appears in the file

`layers/base/server/api/public/v1/__tests__/openapi-route-parity.test.ts:74`

The sweep asserts `openapiSource.toContain("'/path'")` for each `*.get.ts` file. It cannot detect a documented path with no route, a wrong documented scope, an undocumented query parameter, or a response body that does not match the schema it claims - which is why every finding above shipped green.

**Failure:** Concretely today: `/contests`, `/videos`, `/docs`, `/tags`, `/learn`, `/events` are documented with no `limit`/`offset`/filter parameters at all though every route accepts them; `/content/{slug}` is documented as returning `PublicContentSummary` while the route returns `toPublicContentDetail` (adds `content` and `tags`); and four endpoints return bodies that violate the `paginated(...)` schema or crash. All pass. A future `foo.post.ts` under v1 would also be invisible to the walk (`.get.ts` only) while breaking the 'read-only in v1' contract. Separately, no public response sets `Cache-Control` or `Vary: Authorization` (only `Vary: Origin` in public-api-auth.ts:57,103), so a shared cache in front of the instance can serve one key's `/members/open-to/recruiters` body to another key.

**Suggested fix:** Import the handler's returned document rather than reading the source, then (1) assert set equality between `Object.keys(doc.paths)` and the walked routes, (2) grep each route file for its `requireApiScope('...')` literal and assert it equals the documented `security[0].bearer[0]`, (3) fail on any non-`.get.ts` route file under v1, and (4) add one seeded integration test per collection validating the 200 body against the emitted schema. Also set `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `Vary: Authorization` in the public-api middleware.

<details><summary>Verifier (high confidence)</summary>

Core claim confirmed by reading the code. openapi-route-parity.test.ts:70-77 has exactly one per-route assertion, `expect(openapiSource).toContain(`'${path}'`)`, and walk() at lines 30-42 filters to `.endsWith('.get.ts')`. There is no iteration over the document's `paths` keys, no reference to requireApiScope, and no response-body-to-schema comparison anywhere; packages/server/src/__tests__/publicApi.test.ts covers key hashing, scope matching, rate limiting and PII-leak guards on serializers, not OpenAPI conformance. Two consequences are live and I verified both sides: (1) '/contests', '/videos', '/docs', '/tags', '/learn', '/events' are documented with zero `parameters` (openapi.json.get.ts:381-405) while every one of those routes parses limit/offset plus filters (contests: status; videos: categoryId/authorId; learn: authorId/difficulty; events: status/hubId/upcoming/featured) - an integrator cannot page six collections from the contract; (2) '/content/{slug}' documents $ref PublicContentSummary (line 344) but the route returns toPublicContentDetail, which is `{...toPublicContentSummary(row, domain), content, tags}` (packages/server/src/publicApi/serializers.ts:175), so two real fields are undocumented. Note this is under-documentation rather than a schema violation - OpenAPI permits extra properties absent additionalProperties:false. Two supporting claims are overstated. The 'wrong documented scope' failure has no live instance: I extracted requireApiScope from all 32 route files and every one matches its documented security entry today. The Vary/cache leak is largely refuted: because NO Cache-Control is set at all (grep confirms only the two Vary:Origin sites in layers/base/server/middleware/public-api-auth.ts), RFC 9111 section 3.5 forbids a conforming shared cache from storing any response to an Authorization-bearing request without an explicit public/s-maxage/must-revalidate directive, so the cross-key disclosure of /members/open-to/recruiters needs a non-conforming intermediary, not merely the missing header. Severity P3 is correct: publicApi defaults to false (packages/config/src/schema.ts:124) so the surface is opt-in, and the confirmed damage is integrator-facing contract drift, not user-facing breakage or data exposure.

</details>

## Refuted

Findings that did not survive. Kept so nobody re-files them.

- **Combined registration mode creates a placeholder entry that suppresses the proposal form** — Mechanically the placeholder is created (submissions.ts:573-613 via registrations.ts:260) and does enter myEntries (entries.ts:41-44 keeps the viewer's own draft entry deliberately, "the submit-form gating relies on it"), hiding ContestProposalForm at index.vue:640. But the failure the finding describes cannot occur: currentProposalStage (index.vue:252) requires stage.submissionTemplate?.length — 
- **transitionContestStatus has no status guard on its UPDATE, double-firing results notifications** — The missing CAS predicate is real (contest.ts:266-278 updates WHERE id only, validating against a snapshot read at 246-250), but every failure the finding claims is already guarded. (1) The headline harm — duplicate "You placed 1st" / "Results Posted" notifications from an organizer double-click — cannot occur: packages/schema/src/social.ts:131 declares uniqueIndex('uq_notif_user_type_actor_link')
- **Member directory filters match on persona answers and location the response is required to withhold** — The structural asymmetry is real (filters at directory.ts:919-922 are unconditional; projection gates at :924-926 and :978 are scope-dependent), but the failure scenario cannot occur. (1) DIRECTORY_AUDIENCES is a compile-time `as const` list of exactly ['recruiters','sponsors'] and DIRECTORY_AUDIENCE_PURPOSES is a `satisfies`-pinned literal map — there is no operator-added audience. (2) PROCESSING
- **Username sign-in short-circuits before password verification, creating a username-enumeration timing oracle** — The timing asymmetry is real (line 39-41 throws 401 before any hash work, unlike the email branch), but it leaks nothing non-public and the stated failure scenario cannot occur. Username existence is already an unauthenticated, direct 200/404 oracle on three shipped routes using the identical predicate: server/routes/.well-known/webfinger.ts (ungated for user lookup; the features.federation check 
- **(unmatched)** — The `blocked` status is a documented directory-curation control, not a defederation control, and it does exactly what it claims. enums.ts:220-221 defines it as "admin-blocked (future pings ignored)"; registry.ts:7 says "Admin controls visibility via status"; the admin route status.post.ts:5-7 says "Admin sets a directory entry's status"; and the UI copy directly above the button (RegistryDirectory
- **Circuit-open delivery path returns without releasing the claim lock or advancing backoff state** — The code fact is real (the circuit-open exit at delivery.ts:216-219 does not clear lockedAt, and activities.updatedAt has no $onUpdateFn in schema/src/federation.ts:44), but the failure scenario cannot occur and the claimed harms are inverted. (1) The circuit cannot "close 10 seconds later": COOLDOWN_MS[0] is 5 minutes (circuitBreaker.ts:15-21), exactly LOCK_EXPIRY_MS, and the only early-close pat
- **Contest status change fires an unbounded, un-awaited notification fan-out (~5 queries per entrant)** — The code shape is real but the failure mechanism the finding describes cannot occur. Notes: (a) the function is `transitionContestStatus`, not `updateContestStatus` (packages/server/src/contest/contest.ts:239), reached via layers/base/server/api/contests/[slug]/transition.post.ts behind `requireFeature('contests')` + owner/editor authz — so it IS reachable by a deveco organizer. (b) The literal co
- **`editorial` feature flag is declared on all six surfaces but never checked anywhere** — The finding's core claim — no `features.editorial` read and no `useFeatures()` destructure of it in any route, page, or component — is factually false. `layers/base/pages/index.vue:58` destructures `editorial: editorialEnabled` from `useFeatures()`; line 97 gates the `/api/content?editorial=true&sort=editorial` fetch behind `editorialEnabled.value` (flag off = the request is never issued); line 33
- **ENV_FLAG_MAP parity guard reads stale dist/, so it passes vacuously on the drift it exists to catch** — Refuted on three independent grounds, two factual and one structural. (1) The cited evidence does not match the repo. `apps/reference/server/utils/envFlagMap.ts:22` DOES contain `contestConditionalFields: 'FEATURE_CONTEST_CONDITIONAL_FIELDS',` — it is part of the uncommitted diff (`git diff apps/reference/server/utils/envFlagMap.ts` shows it as an added line right after `contestPrivateFiles`). And
- **No admin page has a role or permission route gate — the only gate is the `admin` feature flag** — The finding misreads layers/base/layouts/admin.vue. Line 167 — cited as "one sidebar section" — is actually `<div v-if=\"isAdmin\">` inside `<main class=\"admin-main\">`, wrapping the page `<slot/>`; the `v-else` branch renders an "Access Denied" panel. `isAdmin` is `computed(() => user.value?.role === 'admin')` (composables/useAuth.ts:61), a ROLE check, not a flag check, so the layout has two gua
- **Federated interactions bypass notification dedup entirely (actorId never passed)** — The code fact is true but the failure scenario is refuted, and the proposed fix is structurally impossible. 1. MISREAD GUARD (fatal to the scenario). The finding says "the duplicate guard at inboxHandlers.ts:1196 only rejects a REPLAY of the same activity id, so distinct ids all pass." It does not. Lines 1186-1199 of onLike query `activities` for `type='Like' AND actorUri=? AND objectUri=? AND dir
- **Mirror page tags remote author HTML with a `prose` class that is defined nowhere** — The CSS facts are true (no `.prose` selector exists anywhere; `cpub-html-contain` has one call site, federated-hubs/[id]/posts/[postId].vue:142), but the failure scenario is unreachable because the element at layers/base/pages/mirror/[id].vue:120 never receives remote author HTML. That div is gated on `v-if="typeof transformedContent.content === 'string'"`. `transformedContent` comes from layers/b
- **Parts-list block clips instead of scrolling at 390px** — The CSS quoted is accurate (BlockPartsListView.vue:58-63 `overflow: hidden`, line 82 table with no scroll wrapper), but the failure scenario cannot occur. (1) Every surface that renders this block sets `word-break: break-word` on an ancestor, not just the `overflow-wrap: break-word` the finding dismisses: ProjectView.vue scoped `.cpub-prose` (~lines 873-878, renderer inside it at 341/343), Article
- **Design-system colour/font audit: essentially compliant — 10 literal colour declarations, 0 real font-family violations** — The literals are real but the failure is not. Confirmed by reading `layers/base/components/hub/HubHero.vue` lines 62-91: `.cpub-hub-banner-pattern` uses `rgba(255,255,255,0.04)` (L81-82) and `.cpub-hub-banner-dots` uses `rgba(255,255,255,0.08)` (L89), and my own independent scan of all 328 `.vue` files under `layers/base` + `packages/ui` reproduces exactly six genuine non-token colour declarations
